Chasing V8: re-architecture program (2026-10-15)
================================================

Decisions (owner, 2026-10-15)
-----------------------------
1. Target: V8 is the reference embeddable engine; "credible" means
   chasing it. Acceptance surface: framework workloads (Vue/React/Djot
   fixtures, Speedometer 3.1) compared against Node/V8 on this ARM64
   machine. x64 (zombie) remains secondary.
2. Direction: GC-managed object model first, then a speculative
   optimizing tier (inlining, type speculation, deoptimization).
3. Continuity: a big-bang branch is allowed. This work lives on
   `rearch/v8-chase`; `main` stays shippable and keeps receiving the
   incremental pilot line. The branch may break embedding/tests while
   the new object model is under construction, but must return to full
   gates before any merge.

Why object model first when GC collection is ~1%?
-------------------------------------------------
Collection pauses really are ~1% (measured below); nobody should sell
this phase as "fixing GC time". The argument is the representation tax
plus sequencing:

- Every object today is Rc<RefCell<ObjData>> plus a separately
  allocated property Vec (Props entries). That is 2+ allocations per
  object, incref/decref traffic on every Value copy in the
  interpreter, bytecode VM, templates, and regions, RefCell borrow
  checks on hot paths, and pointer-chasing layout (RcBox -> ObjData
  -> Props -> entries Vec -> entry). None of that appears as "GC" in
  a profile; it is spread across every operation.
- Value/object layout is baked into every JIT template and region via
  JitLayout offsets. A speculative tier built first would hardcode
  RcBox layout, exact refcount maintenance at every deopt point, and
  allocation shapes that a GC heap would invalidate; the tier would
  be built twice. The removed Cranelift tier (4.5x kernels, ~0 on
  apps) is the standing warning about optimizing on this substrate.
- Escape analysis / allocation sinking / cross-call load elimination
  (the optimizations that actually close app-level gaps) require
  cheap bump allocation and freedom from per-copy refcount semantics.

Phase 0 measurements (2026-10-15, crystal aarch64, CPU 5)
---------------------------------------------------------
Method: /tmp/spec-pilot/trust-js-R1-lto (main e3310993, fat LTO),
fixtures in benchmark-results/engine-optimization-20260929/,
TRUST_JS_PERF_METRICS=1, perf stat whole-process counters, Node
v24.21.0 same fixtures. Single runs; treat as attribution, not gates.

Vue (elapsed_ms trust 642 this run / ~550 paired-median; node 117):
- gc_pause 6.8ms (~1.1% of elapsed), 2 collections, 19,677 objects
  seen by the cycle collector. Collection is NOT the problem.
- native builtin time 0.369s total. Top: Array.prototype.shift
  0.231s across only 671 calls (~343us each), push 0.041s/1425,
  Map.set 0.038s/23796, Map.get 0.019s/78747, isArray 0.012s/140601.
- 26,480 user functions created (25,446 arrows); jit inline 9/38.
React (72ms vs node 17): native_s ~0.000; virtually all time is JS
execution (interp/bytecode/templates/regions), 4,502 functions
created. This is the substrate/call-cost fixture.
Djot: native 0.042s led by RegExp exec 0.025s/27412 and string ops;
JIT compile 0.038s is material at this scale.

perf stat, Vue whole process:
                task-clock  instructions  cache-misses  branch-misses
  trust-js        649ms       3.298e9        22.49e6        1.61e6
  node            168ms       0.737e9         2.72e6        2.98e6
We execute ~4.5x the instructions with ~8.3x the cache misses, and
fewer branch misses than V8. Read: too much generic code per
operation, and a cache-hostile object graph. That is exactly the
signature the object model + tier program targets; it is not a
"one hot function" profile.

Independent finding: Array.prototype.shift at ~343us/call on Vue is
an algorithmic/builtin problem (spec-ordered generic per-element
moves on large arrays, presumably) that no tier or GC change will
fix by itself. Such builtin work is branch-independent and may land
on main at any time; Phase 1 success metrics must not silently
depend on it.

Phases and gates
----------------
P0 (done): decisions + attribution above; branch created.
P1: GC heap + object model on this branch. Deliverables: tracing GC
  (mark-sweep first, generational later only if measured), handle/
  root discipline replacing Rc<RefCell<ObjData>>, single-allocation
  objects with inline property slots + out-of-line overflow, shapes
  carried over (incl. Pilot C descriptor-class encoding), string
  representation review. Design doc for P1 must be recorded and
  owner-approved before code. Exit gates to merge: full lib suite +
  Test262 parity with main; framework fixtures no slower than main
  beyond an explicitly accepted bound; Vue instructions and
  cache-misses measurably improved at equal or better elapsed;
  embed API restored.
P2: Baseline strengthening: call path (frame/argument costs),
  feedback completeness for the future tier, builtin algorithmics
  (shift class), RegExp.
P3: Speculative optimizing tier: inlining, type speculation with
  deopt, global load elimination, escape analysis/allocation
  sinking, range analysis. Reuse Pilot A-C/R1 guard infrastructure
  (class shapes, landing, region facts) as the proof layer.
P4: Long-tail parity: strings, JSON, collections, startup snapshots.

Expectation setting: P1 alone is expected to be roughly
performance-neutral-to-modestly-positive on elapsed time (its payoff
is enabling P3 and cutting instructions/cache misses); V8-class
ratios only become reachable after P3. If P1 measurements contradict
this (no instruction/cache movement), stop and re-plan before P2.

Status: P0 complete. Next action: P1 design record (heap/layout/
handle migration plan) for owner approval before code.

Phase 1 S0 findings (2026-10-15, crystal aarch64, CPU 5)
=========================================================
Plan: docs/v8-chase-p1-plan-2026-10-15.txt (a9e20f0f). S0.0 fixtures
preserved at 73c9d135. Census binary: main e3310993 + feature
architecture-diagnostics, no LTO (diagnostics are not acceptance
timings). Bucket binary: /tmp/spec-pilot/trust-js-R1-lto (= e3310993
fat LTO), perf record -F 4000 cycles, 8 Vue runs, ~20k samples.

S0a allocation census ([allocation-diagnostic] final report per fixture;
raw JSON in /tmp/spec-pilot/census-{vue,react,djot}.json):
- Vue: 154,075 object allocations; 2,969 live at first collection
  (98.1% dead). Families by allocation: ordinary 89,787; array 38,197;
  wrapper 26,091 (primitive wrappers, builtins/mod.rs:10104). Top sites:
  interpreter.rs:7635 function creation 26,480 (= PERF_METRICS function
  creations), interpreter.rs:6731 make_array_from_raw 25,793,
  interpreter.rs:5936 new_object 23,918, builtins/mod.rs:4961 23,095.
- React: 29,931 allocs, 1,020 live (96.6% dead); ordinary 25,421.
- Djot: 122,278 allocs, 1,305 live (98.9% dead); ordinary 69,715
  (eval.rs:7388 alone 54,539), array 49,747.
- 100% of tracked deaths on all three fixtures are
  deaths_before_completed_collection: the cycle collector reclaims
  approximately nothing in these workloads; refcounting does all of it.
  A tracing collector inherits the full churn (30k-154k cells per run
  to mark/reclaim), which is small in absolute terms but must be
  measured under sustained allocation, per plan.
- Lifetimes (allocation-ordinal buckets): Vue 85k/151k deaths within 16
  allocations of birth, long tail (7.9k > 65k); Djot more uniform.
  Infant mortality is high everywhere -> non-moving generational
  collection has plausible future value; S0 prices it as: worth
  revisiting post-P1 with nursery candidates = ordinary+array families.
- Live property shape: named entries per live object 1.79 (Vue), 3.01
  (React), 2.99 (Djot); entries capacity/length 2.48x-3.44x
  (over-reservation); 25% of live Vue objects have zero named entries.
  Eligible-vec histograms cover only the all-default subset (<=4 entries:
  783/796 on Vue) and do not directly give the general distribution.
  Working hypothesis for S3: 4 inline slots; MUST be confirmed by an
  overflow-rate measurement during S3, not assumed.

S0b ownership-cost bucket (sampled cycles self-time, Vue; symbols
classified in /tmp/spec-pilot/bucket.py over /tmp/spec-pilot/vue-report.txt):
- ownership drop/clone glue 9.05% (top: drop_glue<Value> 2.56%,
  PackedValue::drop 2.44%, drop_glue<LStr> 1.12%)
- PackedValue pack/unpack conversions 4.96% (pack 3.87%)
- Props indirection 3.88% (Props::get 2.33%)
- GC registry/edges 0.76%
- Rust alloc/Vec growth 4.70% (partly P1-addressable: fewer, larger
  allocations; not counted in the threshold proposal)
- call path 7.84% (Phase 2/3 territory, not P1)
P1-addressable lower bound ~= 18.7% of sampled cycles. Caveats: cycles,
not instructions; inline Rc increfs inside other symbols are invisible
to sampling (true ownership share is higher); tracing adds marking cost
back. Proposed S6 thresholds for owner go/no-go: (i) Vue whole-process
instructions -10% or better vs e3310993 (3.298e9 -> <=2.97e9), paired
perf stat; (ii) sampled ownership-bucket share at least halved under
the same bucket script; (iii) peak RSS <= +25% vs e3310993 on fixtures;
(iv) total GC time <=5% on fixtures and on the sustained-allocation
workload (to be added in S1/S2).

S0c inventories (counts are non-test greps unless noted):
- Roots: Interp has ~167 pub(crate) fields in the sampled range; root
  categories: intrinsics/prototypes (Gc fields), vm_pool
  Vec<CompactFrame>, native_arg_pool Vec<Vec<Value>>, stub/property-key/
  regex caches (ByteLru with LStr values), global_env_pins (Weak),
  gc_pins FastMap<usize, Gc> (existing explicit pinning),
  shadow_realms FastMap<usize, Box<Interp>> (nested agents on one
  thread), job_script_contexts, realm/promise/temporal state. Full
  per-field root registration list is S1/S2 implementation work against
  this struct; no field may be assumed scanned (Rust-heap containers).
- Address-keyed tables: 136 total (56 in interpreter.rs alone), incl.
  class_info, construct_ics, map_data (ordered_collection),
  weak_collection_data, array_buffers(+versions/dirty/immutable/
  host_keyed), typed_arrays, data_views, regexps, proxies, promises,
  unhandled_rejections, temporal(+cal), realms, gc_pins, ta_buffer.
  All need I8 sweep hooks at S2; map_data/typed_arrays/array_buffers
  are payload-move candidates (S3/S4).
- Opaque storage: NativeClosure = dyn Fn(&mut Interp, Value, &[Value])
  (value.rs:88), Callable::Opaque(Rc<NativeClosure>) (value.rs:1722) —
  captures are arbitrary Rust state and may hold Values untraceably
  today; wall_clock and two host hooks are Rc<dyn Fn> with non-Value
  signatures. NativeClosure needs a mandatory trace companion or a
  no-Values-in-captures rule at S2 (Decision 2).
- Refcount-observable: 194 strong_count/get_mut/is_unique sites
  non-test (incl. tests in same files). Production-semantic ones to
  disposition individually in S2: array_binding.rs ownership heuristics,
  value.rs:550/620/660-681/727 (pack/unpack owner counting),
  value.rs:3906 Rc::get_mut on shared layout (copy-on-write),
  parser.rs:3746 Rc::get_mut (AST, unaffected), jit_cache.rs strong
  counts on code objects (not JS heap), lstr unique append (Decision 4).
- I9 surface: 737 borrow_mut() call sites non-test; cross-allocation
  subset unknown (idiomatic temporary-borrow pattern confirmed at
  builtins/mod.rs:3278). Sizing requires the S2 retrofit pass with
  debug assertions on — plan assumes worst case (pervasive).
- Side-effectful Drop impls to disposition for I5: Object (value.rs:2074,
  registry removal), Gc (gc_handle.rs:103), PackedValue, Property,
  InlinePacked, LStr, SymbolData, WeakMetadata, JsonRecord; AST/regex/
  snapshot Drops are compiler-side, unaffected.
- Pointer-hiding vs scanning contract: 68 into_raw/as_ptr-as-usize
  round-trip sites non-test; 629 Rc::as_ptr sites total. Address-keyed
  usize tables hold addresses as integers (false-retention risk only,
  while a real handle exists elsewhere; sole-ownership-via-usize sites
  must be found in the S0d contract audit).
- Threads: production thread::spawn uses are test-only except a regex
  test canceller carrying Arc<RuntimeInterrupt> (no Values); execution
  is single-threaded per agent. stacker::grow is used on execution
  paths (interpreter.rs:4123 eval, regex, snapshot) -> scanner must
  enumerate grown segments; stacker exposes no segment-list API, so S0d
  must wrap grow calls with our own segment registry or scan from a
  recorded stack base per segment. This is the spike's hardest sub-item.
- Downstream: trust-js-host re-exports trust_js::embed (curated);
  borrow-pattern mentions in trust-js-web (lib.rs, wasm/exec.rs) and
  trust-js-node (napi.rs) need the S6 audit; nothing found that reaches
  Object internals outside the embed surface in host/runtime crates.

Pending in S0: (d) rooting spike + written scanning contract + wasm32
mechanism decision; (e) string accumulation replacement selection +
scaling tests. Owner go/no-go after those land.

S0d rooting spike + scanning contract draft (2026-10-15)
--------------------------------------------------------
Spike code: crates/trust-js/src/gc_scan_spike.rs (test-only, cfg(test)).
Toolchain: rustc 1.99.0 (b940084d7 2026-09-28), aarch64, release opt.
All results below are this toolchain only.

Results (cargo test --release, 3 passed):
1. Recognized forms, with safepoint spill: raw pointer, tagged word
   (tag-masked), interior pointer (+3 words), struct-field pointer all
   found through a call boundary + volatile spill area.
2. Without spill, same-frame scan in optimized code found only
   {arena-base, struct-field} of {raw, tagged, interior, struct-field}:
   the compiler kept sole references in registers. The register-spill
   step at collection entry is therefore mandatory, exactly as Boehm
   warns; scanning is a contract, not a construction.
3. stacker segments are invisible to a main-stack scan and visible in
   their own range. Guessing bounds around a segment local segfaulted
   twice during the spike (guard pages both sides): segment bounds
   must come from a registry with exact ranges.
4. Scan cost: 100 scans of 1 MiB in 24ms with volatile reads
   (~0.24 ms/MiB, an upper bound; production plain reads are faster).
   At typical live stack depths (<=256 KiB scanned) this is tens of
   microseconds per collection — acceptable, will re-measure in S1.

Scanning contract (draft, for S1 to encode; owner-approved at go/no-go):
- Supported: rustc/LLVM as recorded above, aarch64 (+x64 re-validation
  on zombie at S6), shipped codegen settings. Re-run spike tests on
  every toolchain bump (they are compliance evidence, not proof).
- Recognized forms across any allocation point: raw cell pointer;
  tagged word (recognized after payload masking); interior pointer
  within cell bounds (page metadata). FORBIDDEN as sole reference:
  integer-cast addresses (as_ptr as usize round-trips — 68 sites
  inventoried), XOR/offset-obscured pointers, references reconstructable
  only from non-pointer data.
- Safepoint protocol: collection entered only via allocation slow
  path (a call); entry captures a register snapshot (S1 chooses asm
  capture vs setjmp-style buffer) and scans snapshot + stack ranges +
  registered root ranges. Rust-heap containers are traced/registered,
  never scanned.
- stacker: grown segments are no-GC regions (I9 pending-collect) until
  registered with exact bounds. S1's first task chooses: (a) wrapper
  deriving exact bounds, validated by a bounds-precision test, or
  (b) replace stacker::grow on GC-relevant paths with an engine-owned
  mmap grow with known bounds. Until then, collecting allocation
  inside an unregistered segment defers, never scans blindly.
- JIT frames comply via I7 (registers never hold sole references;
  canonical slots are stack-resident and scanned).

wasm32 decision: no stack scanning possible; no wasm toolchain exists
on this box (no rustup/wasm-pack/wasmtime; system rustc only), so the
prototype run is DEFERRED to S1 with tooling installation as its
prerequisite — recorded honestly rather than faked. Mechanism decided:
collection at (i) task boundaries, (ii) bytecode-VM loop safepoints
where state is canonical in CompactFrame slots (precisely rootable,
no Rust-local sole references at those points by VM design — S2 must
prove per-safepoint), (iii) native call entry/exit, never mid-native.
Long JS evaluations therefore stay bounded; a single long native
builtin allocating unboundedly is the residual risk, accepted for the
playground-scoped wasm crate and revisited if that changes. The S2
wasm32 sustained-allocation acceptance test remains mandatory.

S0e string accumulation decision (2026-10-15)
---------------------------------------------
Current mechanism: concat_owned(self) consumes the handle;
append_in_place only when strong==1 (lstr.rs:311-326);
append_prop_fast (interpreter.rs:8783) takes the slot value out,
drops the stack copy to manufacture uniqueness, grow-copies once if
aliased, then appends amortized. Oracle test added:
lstr::tests::accumulation_scaling_oracle_geometric_capacity_and_aliasing
(10k appends, capacity <= 4x len, alias at 1k unaffected) — passes.

Decision: builder-cell typestate. GC strings are immutable flat
cells. A distinct builder cell kind holds a geometric buffer and
exists ONLY inside the single slot being accumulated: first += on a
flat string converts it (one copy) to a builder in that slot;
subsequent += appends in place; ANY other read of the slot freezes
(builder -> flat copy handed out; builder may persist in the slot).
Builders never escape as Values, so uniqueness holds by construction
with no refcount and no sole-reference proof. Aliasing cost = one
freeze copy per aliasing read, matching today's grow-copy-once
behavior class. concat_owned maps directly (flat->builder convert or
builder append). Cons/rope rejected for P1: every string consumer
would need rope-aware reads; builder/freeze confines complexity to
the existing += fast paths (AppendProp, eval Add, builtins concat).

S0 status: a/b/c/d/e complete except wasm32 prototype run (deferred,
above) and Speedometer smoke (not run; fixtures only). Proposed S6
thresholds in S0b section. Awaiting owner go/no-go for S1.

S1 heap core (2026-10-15, owner go/no-go: GO)
---------------------------------------------
Audit outcome (Decision 4/5): heap.rs (1,419 lines) is a handle-table
design (HeapRef index+cookie -> boxed storage); its storage model
contradicts Decision 1, so it is NOT extended. Harvested: NoGcScope
pattern (tagged.rs, reimplemented over a shared control block),
stats/verifier concepts. tagged.rs TaggedValue encoding stays for
S5; its RootMap/DeoptRecord remains Phase-3 scaffolding. gc_edges.rs
trace_gc functions become descriptor sources at S2. gc_handle.rs is
the S2 seam. New core: crates/trust-js/src/gc_core.rs behind feature
gc-heap (default off; default build verified unchanged).

Implemented: 64KiB pages segregated by (size class x descriptor),
side-table page metadata with interior-pointer resolution, allocation
bitmap (conservative hits on free cells ignored — a stale stack word
marking free-list linkage was caught by the verifier during bring-up),
large-object path, mark-sweep with precise descriptor tracing,
conservative roots (aarch64 callee-saved register spill via asm,
stack range + registered extra ranges, tagged root ranges, traced
container roots), ephemeron fixed point, weak-slot clearing, sweep
hooks with liveness predicate (I8), I9 machinery (no-GC regions with
pending-collect deferral, zeroed allocate-then-publish), heap
verifier + debug cell poisoning, GC stress knob (collect armed per N
allocations), finalizers.

Tests: 11 gc_core tests (chains, interior/tagged roots, ephemeron
chain fixed point, weak slots, nogc deferral, finalizers, large
objects, sweep-hook side-table pruning, 200-node stress graph under
collect-every-alloc, stack-resident rooting, debug verifier panic)
pass in debug and release; S0 spike tests still pass in release.
Death assertions collect on a fresh thread — same-thread collect
correctly retains via this frame's registers (observed in release:
ephemeron/weak tests initially "failed" by retaining 5/5 cells).
Clippy -Dwarnings clean with and without gc-heap; fmt clean.

Known S1 limits carried forward: no per-target spill on x64 yet
(stack scan only; contract requires it before authority there);
stacker segment exact-bounds registration is API-only (engine wrapper
lands at S2; unregistered segments stay no-GC regions); sweep-hook
liveness set is O(live) memory per collection (tune later); verifier
runs automatically only in debug builds.

S1 review fixes (2026-10-15)
----------------------------
An outside review of 6b0b469c found defects in the S1 deliverables, each
reproduced against a byte-identical copy of gc_core.rs before fixing:
- Weak slots and ephemerons were registered globally and never pruned.
  A reused weak-holder cell had an ordinary word zeroed by the collector
  (memory corruption); a reused key cell next to a still-free value cell
  made mark_ephemerons spin forever (mark_cell ignored the free cell but
  `progress` was still set).
- collect() on a stacker segment scanned [segment SP, entry stack top)
  and crossed a guard page: SIGSEGV in debug and release, against the
  contract's "defer, never scan blindly"; nothing implemented "unregistered
  segments stay no-GC regions".
- The spike's adversarial tests exercise the spike's own scan(), not
  gc_core: the S1 suite passed with the register spill deleted.
- Five tests asserted deaths after same-thread conservative collects;
  sweep_hook_sees_liveness_for_side_tables failed at opt-level=1 and in a
  fat-LTO standalone build.
- Registrations were add-only safe fns over raw pointers; Heap had no
  Drop; the I9 debug assertions were missing; a fixed 1 MiB trigger,
  SipHash page lookups, a HashSet liveness set for sweep hooks, linear
  large-object lookup and O(released x pages) page release would have
  dominated the S2 GC-time and pause gates.

Fixes (crates/trust-js/src/gc_core.rs):
- Weak data is rediscovered every collection from reachable holders.
  LayoutDesc.weak reports in-cell weak slots and ephemeron entries; edge
  sources (Heap::add_edge_source) report side-table edges conditional on
  an owner cell and, for ephemerons, a key. A conditional edge fires at
  most once, when its conditions are marked (per-cell waiter bits), so
  marking stays linear and a condition that is never marked (a free
  cell) only leaves its edge unfired. Dead in-cell entries are zeroed;
  side tables prune through sweep hooks. add_ephemeron/add_weak_slot are
  gone. Unconditional trace roots are for true roots only: a side table
  traced as a root would keep every cycle through it alive.
- Native stack = per-thread chain of registered regions: the entry stack
  (enter_native_stack, RAII) and stacker segments entered through
  gc_core::grow. collect() defers when nothing is registered or SP is
  outside the innermost region; collect_at_safepoint() collects with
  registered roots only (wasm32 safepoints, task boundaries, tests).
- Contract option (a) chosen for stacker: grow() spills callee-saved
  registers into its own never-inlined frame, records that SP as the
  previous region's scan start, and derives the segment's exact bounds
  in the callback from stacker's limit (page-aligned base) and its
  sizing rule (max(1, ceil(size/page)) pages, stacker 0.1.25). A
  bounds-precision test compares the region with /proc/self/maps for
  64 KiB, 100,000 B and 1 MiB segments; a mismatch yields an empty
  region (collections inside defer) and panics in debug builds. Engine
  grow call sites switch to it at S2.
- Spill protocol encoded: Heap::mark_native_stack is #[inline(never)],
  spills x19-x30/d8-d15 (aarch64) into its own frame, reads SP with asm
  and scans [SP, top) per region. The x86_64 SysV spill/SP asm is written
  but NOT compiled here (rustc's and the system LLVM have only the
  AArch64 backend, `llc --version`): validate on zombie before x64
  authority.
- Registrations are RAII handles; a handle dropped from inside a
  collector callback is skipped for the rest of that pass and removed
  after it. Raw-pointer root ranges are unsafe fns and can be re-pointed
  after their container reallocates.
- Heap::drop finalizes the remaining cells and frees all memory (I5).
- I9: Heap::borrow_scope is the hook for S2's GcCell; a collection
  requested with a borrow outstanding panics in debug builds and defers
  in release; debug_assert_quiescent catches leaked guards; re-entrant
  collection asserts. Recorded deviation (see plan): a deferred
  collection runs at the next allocation or request after the outermost
  no-GC region exits, not at region exit (exit is not a safepoint).
- Heap growth: the next collection is due when allocated bytes exceed
  max(1 MiB, 2 x live) (set_heap_growth); allocation is charged at cell
  size. Pages: stable slots, FxHash map keyed by page number (page
  addresses would zero FxHash's low bits), per (descriptor, class)
  current page + avail list rebuilt each sweep, O(1) release. Large
  objects: BTreeMap by address plus span rejection. Sweep hooks read mark
  bits directly. deferred_collections counts pending requests, not
  allocations; deferred_native_stack added.

Evidence (crystal aarch64, rustc 1.99.0):
- gc_core: 33 tests in debug, 31 in release (+3 spike), 5 ignored timing
  probes. Green in-tree under the no-LTO working build and the shipped
  fat-LTO profile; a standalone copy is green at opt-level 0/1/2/3/s/z,
  target-cpu=native and fat LTO.
- Mutation check (standalone copy, release): removing the spill fails
  reference_only_in_a_callee_saved_register_is_found (the only reference
  sits in d8, which no frame on the collection path saves); dropping the
  outer regions, a segment top one page short, unconditional ephemerons,
  skipping dead-weak clearing, no mid-pass deferral, the old per-1-MiB
  trigger, teardown without finalizers and no avail reuse are each caught
  by their tests.
- clippy -Dwarnings clean with --features embed and embed,gc-heap; fmt
  clean; default build unchanged (gc_core.rs is the only source change).
- Timing probes (gc_core::tests::perf_*; CPU 5 pinned; release, opt3, no
  LTO; same standalone harness both sides; before = 6b0b469c):
                                              before          after
    alloc, 32 B cell                          69.7 ns         30 ns
    16 MiB live chain + 1M x 16 B churn       2.46 s, 16 GCs  0.082 s, 1 GC
    collect, 1M live cells                    150 ms          59 ms
      ... with one no-op sweep hook           329 ms          59 ms
    collect, 4000 x 8 KiB large + 32k words   121 ms          0.81 ms
    release 1000/2000/4000 empty pages        21/62/215 ms    0.6/1.2/2.1 ms
  Marking still costs ~59 ns per live cell (one FxHash page lookup per
  edge); in-page headers or a page table are tuning, not S2 blockers.

gc_native.rs audit (missing from the S1 record): NativeGcGraph is a
transient mixed graph (host nodes; js->native edges activated when the JS
owner is marked; native roots; old-domain prefixes for nursery
collections) keyed by Rc::as_ptr addresses. It is the reference design for
the S2 host-root API: JS identities re-key to cell pointers, and the
js->native activation maps onto owner-conditional edge sources.

wasm32 prototype run: still impossible on this box. Beyond the missing
wasm-pack/wasmtime, this rustc's LLVM has no WebAssembly backend
(`--target wasm32-unknown-unknown` fails on wasm llvm-args; llc lists only
AArch64). Re-deferred to the S2 wasm32 acceptance gate, which needs a
rustup toolchain with wasm32 std. The mechanism is encoded: on targets
without a scanner collect() always defers, so the VM must call
collect_at_safepoint() at its safepoints.

Corrections to the S1 record above: release ran 10 of its 11 tests (the
verifier test is debug-only), and "spike green in release" was evidence
about the spike's scanner only. "Known S1 limits" is superseded except
the x64 note (asm now written, still unvalidated).

S2 ownership switch (started 2026-10-15; owner: proceed after S1)
-----------------------------------------------------------------
Baseline at 75e1b77d, default features (`cargo test -p trust-js --lib
--features embed`, debug): 2149 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored, ~4.5 min
of test time.

Strategy. The GcCell payload is literally RefCell<Object>: Gc derefs to
RefCell<Object> in both builds, so the thousands of .borrow()/
.borrow_mut() sites, Ref/RefMut types and RefCell::as_ptr callers compile
unchanged. Rc-specific operations go through Gc associated functions
(as_ptr, ptr_eq, downgrade, strong_count, as_rc) that gc-heap
reimplements; each as_rc use marks a site that genuinely needs an Rc.
Planned I9 enforcement without a mutator-side borrow counter: the tracer
borrows each reachable object, and a mutably borrowed one aborts the
collection and defers it (debug builds assert, locating the site).

Step 1, seam (default build, no behavior change): Gc's Deref target
changed from Rc<RefCell<Object>> to RefCell<Object>. 755 call sites
(440 as_ptr, 123 ptr_eq, 108 downgrade, 84 strong_count) were rewritten
by a fixer driven by rustc's JSON diagnostics — only calls rustc flags,
never textually similar Rc<SymbolData>/Env calls — plus qualified paths
and ~15 hand fixes (weak upgrades in tests; jit_x64_property_tests,
found by inspection because this host cannot compile x86_64; a
trust-js-node test now compares identity through RefCell::as_ptr, since
Gc is not part of the embed API). Lib suite unchanged (2149/0/3);
workspace builds with tests; clippy clean with embed, embed+gc-heap and
for trust-js-node.

Step 2a, remaining Rc tricks behind the seam (default build, no behavior
change): WeakGc is a newtype whose upgrade() yields Gc (35 explicit
Weak<RefCell<Object>> types replaced); object refcount manipulation goes
through Gc::clone_from_raw (FnFrame::callee, old-collector marking) and
Gc::release (five consumed-callee fast paths in the bytecode VM);
jit_prepare_packed_numeric_mirror borrows the RefCell directly instead of
rebuilding an Rc; Gc::new_in(heap, ..) receives the owning Agent heap;
array_binding's destruction-observing strong-count tests became
Gc::outlives_release (always true on a tracing heap). Lib suite unchanged
(2149/0/3); clippy clean (embed, embed+gc-heap); workspace builds.

Step 2b, the gc-heap object model (collection still disabled): under
--features gc-heap, Gc is the Copy address of a gc_core cell holding the
RefCell<Object> (gc_handle.rs `cell`; the default `rc` implementation is
unchanged). GcState owns the cell heap (allocation never collects:
collect_on_alloc = false); the object finalizer is drop_in_place, so
Object::drop still runs at reclamation. WeakGc is a shared (address,
alive) cell tracked per thread and cleared by collections or heap
teardown. HeapRelease, the interpreter's LAST field, finalizes and frees
the Agent's cells after every other field has dropped. Tier::admitted()
clamps Jit to Bytecode (the JIT refuses admission in S2). Every
old-collector entry point routes to gc_collect_with_cause, a no-op under
gc-heap until step 3. Clippy allows clone/drop/forget on Copy handles
under gc-heap only (code is shared with the default build).
Default build: lib suite 2149/0/3 unchanged; clippy clean.
gc-heap: lib suite 1756 passed / 426 failed / 8 ignored. Failure
families: strong_count assertions in tests (no reference counts), ~150
JIT-tier tests (refused by design), reclamation and weak-clearing tests
(need collection: step 3), ownership-precision tests asserting
refcount-timed releases.

S2 design decisions from the step-3 survey (recorded in the plan before
acting):
- Collections run only at interpreter safepoints (gc_check, task
  boundaries, buffer pressure, explicit), as today; allocation never
  collects. The rooting obligation (I1) therefore applies at safepoints.
- Environment records must become heap cells in S2: Env is
  Rc<RefCell<Scope>> held in Rust locals throughout the tree-walker and
  VM, and the conservative scan can only discover handles that point into
  the GC heap. The same seam approach as Gc applies.
- Rust-heap Value containers live across a safepoint (spread and
  argument buffers, literal element vectors) need a rooted container
  (JavaScriptCore's MarkedArgumentBuffer is the analog), found and
  verified by stress collection at every safepoint with poisoning on.
- Roots: Interp::trace_gc_roots destructures Interp exhaustively, so a
  new field cannot compile until classified (Decision 2 enforcement);
  owner-keyed side tables become owner-conditional callbacks that trace
  an owner's side slots once it is marked.

Step 3a, environment seam (default build, no behavior change): Env is a
newtype (env_handle.rs) over Rc<RefCell<Scope>>, dereferencing to the
RefCell<Scope>; identity and ownership go through Env::as_ptr/ptr_eq/
downgrade/strong_count/from_raw/clone_from_raw and WeakEnv, as for Gc.
The four Scope constructions became Env::new(scope) (the scope already
names its heap). 173 calls rewritten by the same rustc-driven fixer
(generalized to pick the handle type from what rustc reports finding),
13 Weak<RefCell<Scope>> types became WeakEnv, and the bytecode VM's
raw-environment reborrows use Env::from_raw. Default suite 2149/0/3
unchanged; workspace builds; clippy clean on both feature sets; gc-heap
suite unchanged (1756/426/8).

Step 3b, environments as GC cells (gc-heap; collection still disabled):
Env is the Copy address of a gc_core cell holding RefCell<Scope>, with
its own descriptor (trace_scope_cell / finalize_scope_cell); WeakEnv and
WeakGc share one untyped WeakRef (address + alive flag). The cell
descriptors' trace functions are written: an object reports its own edges
(prototype, every property and internal slot, array-iterator target,
closure environment, bound/wrapped targets, native captures and retained
reporters), an environment its bindings, imports, parent and with-object.
Object, Callable, Exotic, Scope and Binding are destructured or matched
exhaustively (gc_trace.rs GcTrace), so a new field or variant does not
compile until classified. Default suite unchanged (2149/0/3); clippy clean
on both feature sets. gc-heap suite 1747/435/8: the 9 newly failing tests
assert refcount-timed environment releases (expected until collection).

Step 3c, collection at safepoints (gc-heap): the heap collects. Entry points
are the interpreter's safepoints — gc_check (live objects over the old
policy's gc_next threshold, the cell heap's byte budget of max(64 MiB,
2x live), a stress interval or a deferred request), task boundaries,
buffer pressure and explicit requests; allocation never collects.
- Marking (gc_collect.rs). Interp::trace_gc_roots destructures Interp
  exhaustively: each of the 169 fields is a root, an owner-conditional
  side slot, a Realm-group member, an ephemeron, weak, or value-free, and
  a new field does not compile until classified. The heap's MarkClient
  reports roots (interpreter fields; registered VM frames, running
  coroutines and rooted buffers; host roots and native-graph roots), side
  slots traced once their owner is marked (the 12 owner-keyed tables,
  generator continuations, native-graph JS owners, and Realm groups: a
  Realm is not a root, any marked member activates its whole group and
  its template cache), and ephemerons (WeakMap/WeakSet entries, symbol
  keys conservatively; the [[Realm]] edge of built-ins; host edges).
- Two-phase collection: mark, then the engine evicts the dead from its
  tables (the shared gc_sweep_dead_objects over dead_cells) while every
  cell is intact and new cells are allocated marked (gc_core: allocate
  black between the phases), weak handles are cleared (a dead cell a weak
  handle still names stays reserved, finalized, until the handle goes:
  inline-cache address compares stay ABA-safe), and finish frees.
- ShadowRealm interpreters share their parent's heap (one heap per Agent,
  I6: no edge crosses heaps). Only the root of the ShadowRealm tree
  collects, over the whole tree; a child never releases or detaches the
  shared heap.
- I1 rooting. drive_vm registers its frame state (slots, operand stack,
  references, class and disposal state, environments) for the call;
  Interp::call, construct, dispatch_native and the native fast path root
  their argument slices; a native invocation roots its captures. Rust-heap
  value containers live across a safepoint use rooting.rs: RootedVec
  (growable), root_slice (borrowed), Rooted<T> (any GcTrace state) and
  ValueRoots (values kept beside an opaque structure); all are plain
  buffers without gc-heap. Applied to argument evaluation, spread, rest,
  tail calls, groupBy, Iterator.zip/concat/toArray/chunks/windows, class
  definitions and decorators (both tiers), sort/toSorted/toReversed/with/
  toSpliced/flat, Set methods, Object.values/defineProperties, RegExp
  match/replace/split, Reflect.apply/construct, TypedArray.from, wrapped
  ShadowRealm calls, timers and Intl formatToParts. Found by GC stress
  (every lib test run in its own process with TRUST_JS_GC_STRESS=1:
  spread, Iterator.zip, toArray/chunks, and class decorators crashed or
  failed the heap verifier) and by a source audit of value buffers built
  across calls into JavaScript.
- Stack hygiene. Conservative false retention was dominated by stale cell
  addresses in reused stack memory, including the collector's own: its
  setup enumerated every Realm member and host value in frames the marker
  then reused, and its sweep handled dead addresses that later matched
  new cells. The client is built out of line and the stack scrubbed
  before marking; marking and sweeping are sibling calls; explicit
  collections and embedder entries (script evaluation, microtask
  checkpoints) scrub below themselves on entry and exit (1 MiB debug,
  128 KiB release). The scrub is inlined into its caller and zeroes from
  a frameless asm leaf: a scrubbing frame of its own left its unwritten
  slots stale, and the collector's next frame inherited a dead cell's
  address from one of them (six collectability tests failed once an
  unrelated edit moved the collector's frame layout). Retention diagnostics: TRUST_JS_GC_WHY=1 records why
  each cell was marked (root, stack word with its owning frame, cell,
  side slot, conditional edge).
- Stack growth on execution paths goes through gc_core::grow (registered
  segments); a thread registers its whole stack when it creates an
  interpreter.
- Test triage under gc-heap: JIT test modules are gated off (the JIT is
  refused until S5) and tier-looping tests assert JIT facts only for an
  admitted JIT; tests observing reference counts, refcount release timing,
  eager death notification, the refcount collector or its nursery,
  cross-Agent object edges, the HostGc omission fallback or handles that
  outlive their Agent are ignored with that reason; collectability tests
  keep strong handles and cell addresses out of the test frame
  (gc_test::in_frame / alive / HiddenAddress), since the stack scan
  retains whatever a frame still holds. Test builds also zero a thread's
  stack when it exits: libtest runs each test on a new thread, glibc
  hands it a cached stack with the previous test's words in slots not yet
  written, and a new heap whose pages land at the old heap's addresses
  took them for roots (an intermittent WeakSet collectability failure).
- GC stress over the suite: every lib test in its own process with
  TRUST_JS_GC_STRESS=1 (a collection at every safepoint; debug build, so
  the heap verifier and freed-cell poisoning run at each). Tests asserting
  when collections or cleanup jobs happen skip those assertions under
  stress (gc_test::stress_active). Tests of deep recursion, long chains
  and allocation loops exceed the 15-minute limit at interval 1 (each
  collection rescans the whole stack and heap) and run at interval 100,
  the three heaviest at 1000. Stress found one engine bug here: a stress
  or byte-budget collection reset the live-object threshold to the limit,
  so the handler of the limit's RangeError threw again at its first
  safepoint; only a threshold collection now enforces the limit or
  re-arms the threshold, and collections keep the handler's headroom.
Results: gc-heap lib suite 1825 passed / 0 failed / 114 ignored (six
consecutive runs clean). Default suite unchanged (2149/0/3). Stress,
each of the 1939 lib tests in its own process: 1806 pass at interval 1,
the 19 slow ones at interval 100 (16) or 1000 (3), 114 are the suite's
gc-heap ignores; none fail. gc_core unit tests cover allocate-black and
stack scrubbing (debug, and release with fat LTO). Clippy clean on both
feature sets; the workspace and its tests build.

Step 4 plan (2026-10-16): strings, symbols and BigInts become cells in
the Agent heap, in the order 4b BigInt, 4c Symbol, 4d String (the
largest: string accumulation per Decision 4, per-heap caches for the
thread-wide one-unit and index string tables). Each sub-step keeps the
default build unchanged and is stressed per test on the code it moves;
the full per-test stress run repeats at the end of step 4.

Step 4b, BigInt payloads as cells (gc-heap): JsBigInt's digits live in
a cell of the active Agent heap (gc_box::GcBox: the cell's address,
Copy, no count; a per-heap "bigint" descriptor with no edges whose
finalizer frees the digits). Values, packed property words and BigInt
wrapper objects report the cell. Source literals stay outside any heap:
tokens, the AST and snapshots hold a BigIntLiteral (Rust-heap digits,
shared by every evaluation) and evaluation makes the value. Compiled
chunks root their constants (RootedVec) for as long as the chunk lives.
New payload_cell_tests: BigInts survive collections in every holder
(array elements, Map keys, wrappers, typed arrays, closures, literals),
and dead ones are reclaimed. The survival test fails, under stress, with
BigInt tracing removed. Results: gc-heap lib suite 1827/0/114, default
2149/0/3; the 234 BigInt-, typed-array-, literal- and parser-related
tests pass per test at stress interval 1 (11 ignored as in the suite);
clippy clean on both feature sets; the workspace builds.
Follow-up: host roots, host and native-graph edges, Realm template values
and WeakMap ephemeron values still converted only objects to edges, so
a BigInt held only there was freed. Every value-to-edge conversion now
goes through Value::cell_word; a new test covers WeakMap values and
FinalizationRegistry held values (it fails without the fix). gc-heap
lib suite 1828/0/114.

Step 4c, Symbols as cells (gc-heap): a Symbol value's handle (SymbolRef:
an Rc without gc-heap) is a cell in the Agent heap that minted it, with
a "symbol" descriptor whose finalizer runs SymbolData's drop, which
journals the death for WeakRefs, WeakMaps and FinalizationRegistries
(gc-heap journals deaths when a collection finds them, not when a last
handle drops). Weak handles (WeakSymbol) share the weak-cell table with
objects. Roots: the Agent's Symbol.for registry and well-known Symbols,
the interpreter's cached Symbol.iterator. Edges: values, packed property
words, Symbol wrapper objects, Symbol-keyed properties' per-object
sidecar (traced from object cells), class-evaluation property keys; a
Symbol key of a WeakMap is now an ephemeron key like an object's. The
Agent's id-to-Symbol index names each Symbol weakly, so a dead Symbol's
cell stays reserved until the index drops its entry right after the
collection that found it dead, and is freed by the next one. New tests:
Symbols survive collections in every holder; dead ones are reclaimed and
their WeakRef, WeakMap entry and FinalizationRegistry cell are cleared.
Removing Symbol value tracing, or the registry roots, fails the survival
test. Two weak_metadata tests asserting eager Symbol death notification
are ignored with that reason; a third keeps its collection-based half.
Results: gc-heap lib suite 1828/0/116, default 2149/0/3; 291 Symbol-,
weak-collection-, property-key-, class- and ShadowRealm-related tests per
test at stress interval 1: 268 pass, 23 ignored as in the suite; clippy
clean on both feature sets; the workspace builds.

Step 4d, strings as cells (gc-heap): an engine string (LStr) is a cell of
the active Agent heap, a flat buffer or a view of one (one "string"
descriptor; a view's edge is its owner). Handles are addresses: cloning
copies, nothing is counted. Accumulation follows the revised Decision 4,
frontier append (plan, recorded deviations): word 0 of a flat buffer is
how far it has been written; an append to the string ending at that
frontier, with spare capacity, writes past it and returns a view of the
same buffer, anything else copies into a buffer with geometric
capacity. JS-level check (debug build, global variable, s += "ab"):
100k/200k/400k appends take 2.3/4.6/9.0 s, linear. The counted build is
quadratic on that pattern (10.5/46.9/164 s; its unique-append fast path
covers locals and properties, and the base commit behaves the same).
- Roots and edges: values, packed words, String wrappers and iterators,
  error stack frame names (Error objects, code frames, resumed frames),
  the interpreter's string caches keyed by a string's address (their
  pins become roots), the RegExp legacy match, prepared RegExp subjects,
  private-name site keys (Rooted), function-name templates (pinned by
  the template), and the heap's interned one-unit and index strings
  (moved from thread-locals, which would share cells across Engines).
- Allocation outside any Agent (before an Engine exists, or between
  activations) goes to a per-thread default heap that never collects and
  lives until the thread exits; a heap made per activation gap freed such
  strings as soon as an Engine activated its own (found by a test that
  built its subject string before the Engine).
- I1 re-audit for primitive cells. Step 3c rooted value buffers that held
  objects; lists that could hold only strings, Symbols or BigInts were
  safe then and are not now. Own-key lists (ordinary, Proxy, enumerable
  Proxy keys), Set value snapshots and Reflect argument lists are
  RootedVecs, whose by-value iteration keeps the rest rooted; PropertyKey
  lists carrying Symbols are Rooted; TypedArray sort and filter
  buffers, RegExp replace captures, String.prototype.concat parts, the
  host-indexed Object.keys path and the array-destructuring prefix are
  rooted. Per-test GC stress found each of these (UAF, freed-cell reads,
  wrong strings) or the audit did.
- Tests: strings survive collections in every holder; accumulation keeps
  aliases (an alias's own append copies); dead strings are reclaimed;
  interned strings outlive their references (fails, returning another
  string, without the interned roots). Removing string value tracing
  fails the survival test (the heap verifier reports the missing edge).
  Tests of the counted representation keep their behavioral assertions
  and observe buffer identity instead of pointer identity; their
  reference-count assertions run only in the counted build.
Results: gc-heap lib suite 1832/0/116, default 2149/0/3 unchanged;
per-test GC stress with the verifier: 1813 pass at interval 1, the 19
slow ones at interval 100 (15) or 1000 (4: the live-object-limit handler
test now needs more than 15 minutes at 100), 116 ignored as in the
suite, none fail; clippy clean on both feature sets; the workspace builds.

Step 5, mandatory host enumeration (Decision 2): every value an Agent
keeps for its host declares its JavaScript values at the type level.
- HostTrace (trace_gc reporting root / edge / internal) is required by
  OpState::put and its retained-memory variants and by ResourceTable::
  add; value_free!(T, ...) declares types that hold none (common std
  types are declared in trust-js). HostGc is now HostTrace plus the
  sweep and end-of-job hooks of caches. Under gc-heap every slot and
  resource is traced and a host keeps alive exactly what it reports; the
  counted build still traces registered caches only.
- Opaque native closures declare their captures (NativeCaptures::None or
  Reported(reporter)); the *_with_retained_memory constructors merged
  into new_native_fn / make_native_closure. This found one gc-heap bug:
  Error.captureStackTrace's getter captured a stack trace whose frame
  names are string cells; it now reports them.
- Downstream: trust-js-host's CallbackQueue and TaskRegistry report their
  callbacks, arguments and settlement functions; timers their handlers
  and arguments; web servers, WebSockets and SSE their dispatch functions
  and wasm stores their memory buffers; node its FFI callbacks, N-API
  references (rooted whatever their count, as before) and deferreds; the
  runtime its workers' dispatch functions. The other registries, the
  console, process and fs resources are declared value-free.
  trust-js-host and trust-js-runtime gain a gc-heap feature that forwards
  to the engine.
- Tests (gc-heap): host slots, resources and reported closure captures
  survive collections; an edge target lives while its owner does and dies
  with it (mutation-checked: without tracing the slots the tests fail).
Results: gc-heap lib suite 1835/0/116, default 2149/0/3; downstream
crates (counted build) 334/0/2; the runtime on a gc-heap engine passes
its tests (180/0/1), and per test at GC stress interval 10 its unit tests
(all but the BigInt-heavy crypto ones) pass 103 of 105 with one ignored:
the failure asserts a worker terminates promptly, which took 227 s at that
interval (a timing assertion; it passes without stress). Clippy clean on
both feature sets and the workspace.

Step 6, I8 sweep inventory: Interp::gc_evict_dead_owners destructures
the interpreter exhaustively, like trace_gc_roots for marking, so a new
field does not compile until classified by what it keeps about objects
that may die. 30 tables keyed by an object's address evict a dead
object's entries there (the batch formerly listed by hand); 8 are swept
by the steps before it (host caches through HostGc, Realm templates,
[[Realm]] pins, weak targets and owners, FinalizationRegistry readiness);
9 hold weak pins pruned after the collection, a pinned dead address
staying reserved (zombie) meanwhile, and 2 proofs name addresses through
such pins; 7 are keyed by values they keep alive (rejection tracking,
host settings, the string caches' pins); 1 is keyed weakly and checked
on use; the other 111 are not keyed by object addresses. Outside the
interpreter, WeakMetadata's ready-registry set is swept with the weak
owners; per-heap tables are released by the cells' finalizers; the
gc_core sweep-hook registry stays unused, since the engine sweeps
between the collector's two phases instead. Shared by both collectors.

Step 7, I9 (no collection with an outstanding borrow): collections run
only at interpreter safepoints and allocation never collects, so a
RefCell borrow held across an allocation cannot meet a collection, and
the S0 borrow-across-allocation list needs no no-GC regions. A safepoint
reached while an engine cell or a traced container is mutably borrowed
panics when traced (try_borrow_unguarded in object cells, the GcTrace of
RefCell); per-test GC stress, which collects at every safepoint of every
test, found none.
Results: gc-heap lib suite 1835/0/116, default 2149/0/3; clippy clean.

S2 exit gate (2026-10-16)
-------------------------
Builds: main = e3310993 (the branch's merge base; main has no later
commits); branch = d6ef1e66 plus this commit. crystal aarch64
(Cortex-X925 + A725), rustc 1.99.0; release = opt-level 3, fat LTO,
codegen-units 1, panic=abort. Under gc-heap Tier::Jit is admitted as
bytecode.
- Lib suite: gc-heap 1835/0/116 (each ignore carries its reason);
  default 2149/0/3 unchanged (gc_collect.rs, the only engine file this
  commit touches, is gc-heap-only).
- Test262 (release runners for main, branch default and branch gc-heap;
  TRUST_JS_TIER=interp and =bytecode): built-ins/{Object,Array,Proxy,
  WeakMap,WeakRef,FinalizationRegistry,TypedArray} and language/
  {expressions,statements,types}, 29029 files. Every run: 29028 pass,
  0 fail, 1 skip (upstream: missing immutable exclusion); the eight
  summary.json reports (six, plus gc-heap again after the floor change
  below) are byte-identical. Parity.
- GC stress + verifier + poisoning (debug, the final binary, every test
  in its own process, 900 s each): 1835 pass, 116 ignored, 0 fail;
  1815 at interval 1, 16 at 100 and 4 at 1000 per the recorded policy.
  One interval-1 test (a_dropped_realm_that_ran_shared_unresolvable_
  lookups_is_collected: three child Realms at each of three tiers) needs
  854 s alone and timed out in a loaded parallel run; it now runs at
  100.
- Perf tripwire (benchmarks/p1-s2-tripwire-2026-10-16.json): fresh
  processes pinned to CPU 5, 11 interleaved rounds; object_alloc_bench,
  benchmarks/property-access.js (new: named reads/writes, method calls
  and computed keys on shape-stable objects), the new sustained-
  allocation workload and the Vue/React/Djot fixtures, at interp and
  bytecode. Outputs are identical to main everywhere (fixtures modulo
  elapsed_ms). On these JS workloads the branch's default build is
  within -4.1%..+0.6% of main's wall time and +-6% of its peak RSS (one
  object_alloc_bench component regresses; see the review fixes below).
  gc-heap against main, wall time (peak RSS, MiB, main -> gc-heap),
  re-measured after the review fixes:
                         interp                 bytecode
    property access      +4.2%  (12 -> 46)      -3.0%  (13 -> 42)
    sustained alloc.     +3.6%  (179 -> 218)    +7.4%  (147 -> 206)
    Vue                  +18.5% (27 -> 68)      -9.2%  (28 -> 42)
    React                +20.7% (13 -> 52)      -13.2% (13 -> 40)
    Djot                 +17.9% (36 -> 58)      -4.6%  (32 -> 41)
  The first run had the step-3c byte-budget floor of 64 MiB and peak RSS
  up to 333 MiB on these workloads (Vue at interp: +1187%; React at
  interp, 322 MiB: +2337%). Environment records are cells but not
  objects, so the object-count trigger never saw scope churn; up to 64
  MiB of them and their binding vectors accumulated before the first
  collection. A floor sweep (4-64 MiB, in the record) showed, at 8 MiB
  against 64, bytecode-tier time flat with peak RSS 1-4x lower, and
  interp-tier peak RSS 4-6x lower for up to 6.5% more time (more
  collections, each with fixed costs). The floor is now 8 MiB;
  TRUST_JS_GC_MIN_HEAP_BYTES overrides it for tuning (DIAGNOSTICS.md).
  The remaining interp-tier cost is mostly sweeping dead environment
  records: the tree-walker creates one per call and per loop iteration
  with a heap-allocated binding vector, which the counted build frees at
  once into fastalloc's per-thread cache and gc-heap frees in batches
  that overflow it into glibc (profile: Vec<(Rc<str>, Binding)> drop
  glue, _int_free, unlink_chunk). S4's tailored environment cells own
  that. The bytecode tier keeps non-captured bindings in frame slots.
- object_alloc_bench (no safepoints run in it, so gc-heap reclaims
  nothing during the bench), ns/op main -> gc-heap: Object::new + drop
  61 -> 99 (allocation without reclamation), templated {x,y} 106 -> 121,
  Gc::downgrade + drop 9.4 -> 94 (each gc-heap weak handle allocates an
  Rc<WeakCell> and registers it in a thread-local list), Props::new 7.6
  -> 5.8. "Object::new retained" (the heap at ~15M cells, 5.4-5.8 GB)
  runs 2-4 us/op and varies between runs: gc-heap's user time (2.0 s) is
  below main's (2.25 s), its system time 10-33 s. This machine's glibc
  2.44 defaults glibc.malloc.hugetlb=1, so malloc madvises its heaps for
  transparent huge pages, and THP defrag=madvise compacts memory
  synchronously at each huge-page fault (memory here is fragmented: 861k
  compaction stalls since boot). GC pages are 64 KiB alloc_zeroed blocks
  from glibc; giving the page allocator its own chunked mmap source with
  an explicit THP policy is an S3 item.
- Sustained-allocation workload (benchmarks/sustained-allocation.js:
  200k retained objects, 400k short-lived objects, arrays and closures,
  40k retained replacements whose displaced originals die after
  surviving collections; TRUST_JS_PERF_METRICS=1 and TRUST_JS_GC_LOG=1,
  3 runs, re-measured after the review fixes): post-GC live bytes 36.8
  MiB in 621-622 small-cell pages (38.8-38.9 MiB, 95% occupied: no
  fragmentation at this scale); 20 collections, most pauses 25-60 ms,
  max 60 ms interp / 47 ms bytecode (main: 81 / 62 ms); GC share of wall
  32% interp / 40% bytecode (main: 25% / 28%). Both builds are far from
  the S6 target of 5%. gc-heap inherits the counted build's object
  threshold, live + max(live/8, 100k), which suits a nursery: a full
  collection every 100k objects here re-marks the retained heap, and on
  larger heaps the 1/8 rule marks up to 8 objects per object allocated.
  A doubling rule would about halve this workload's collections but let
  garbage reach live size, against the S6 peak-RSS bound of +25%; that
  trade, or a nursery over the cell heap, is for S3/S6 with the owner.
- wasm32 acceptance: blocked (see the plan's S2 deviations): this
  rustc's LLVM rejects the wasm llvm-args for a no_std cdylib, and no
  wasm32 std is installed.
- Outside review checkpoint: pending (owner).

S2 review fixes (outside review of dcde3ef6, 2026-10-16)
-------------------------------------------------------
- [P1] Class Symbol keys were not traced. ClassInfo's trace skipped
  FieldInit.key (classified "not cells" before Symbols became cells), so
  an explicit collection dropped a class's [Symbol('x')] field from
  later instances. PreparedClassEvaluation's gc-heap trace went through
  an adapter (trace_direct) that reported objects and environments only:
  a suspended class evaluation lost its computed Symbol keys, and its
  decorator receivers when not objects (`@a.b` with a string `a`: the
  decorator then saw another string's bytes, a use-after-free). Both
  now trace every cell; PreparedReferenceSlot got a native exhaustive
  trace and trace_direct is deleted. An audit of every `field: _` in
  the exhaustive destructurings whose declared type can hold a cell
  found no other stale classification; GcEdgeSink's object-only
  value() serves only the counted collector and diagnostics. Test
  (both tiers, collections between separate evaluations so no stale
  stack word keeps a cell): payload_cell_tests::class_definitions_keep_
  symbol_keys_and_decorator_receivers; without the field-key trace the
  field is lost, without the key and receiver traces the test aborts on
  a poisoned string.
- [P1] The runtime ran callback batches from an unrooted Vec: it moved
  the traced CallbackQueue out, so a collection in one callback freed
  the ones after it (SIGBUS/SIGSEGV). Unhandled rejections taken from
  the engine for reporting had the same shape. Both batches now live in
  traced host slots (RunningCallbacks, ReportingRejections) and are
  popped one at a time; Interp::call roots a popped entry's arguments.
  The error-reporting shims are rooted in a host slot as well
  (fire_rejection has no JavaScript holder, and a boxed Runtime is not
  on the scanned stack). Timers were already safe: begin_task validates
  a due task against its traced entry before the handler is touched;
  wasm run_func moves only weak host-function handles and a store
  without JavaScript values. Tests (trust-js-runtime): queued_callbacks_
  survive_a_collection_inside_their_batch and unhandled_rejection_batch_
  survives_a_collection_in_its_handler; with the batches untraced, the
  first crashes (SIGSEGV) and the second trips the heap verifier.
- [P2] benchmarks/sustained-allocation.js kept 39,999 of its 40,000
  displaced originals reachable: each replacement linked to the next
  slot's original, not yet replaced. Replacements now link to the
  previous slot, already replaced; a WeakRef probe reclaims 400 of 400
  sampled originals (1 of 400 before). Its evidence in the exit gate
  above is re-measured, with the other JS workloads.
- [P2] The default build's parity holds for the JS workloads only:
  object_alloc_bench's "templated {x,y} + drop" is +8.9% there (115.1 vs
  105.7 ns/op, non-overlapping samples). Object layouts are identical
  and S2 left the templated path's source unchanged, so this is a
  codegen or drop-path effect; left open.
- Validation: gc-heap lib suite 1836/0/116; trust-js-runtime 182/0/1
  in both builds; at GC stress interval 1 the 124 class, decorator,
  prepared-reference, payload-cell and Symbol tests (118 pass, 6
  ignored as in the suite) and both new runtime tests pass; the
  reviewer's reproductions (class field, suspended computed method,
  setImmediate after $262.gc()) pass at interp and bytecode; clippy and
  fmt clean for the engine and the runtime in both builds.

S3 Layout A (started 2026-10-16; owner: go after the S2 review fixes)
---------------------------------------------------------------------
Stage boundary: main has no commits past the merge base e3310993, so
there is nothing to sync.

Plan, recorded before the work. Scope is the gc-heap object layout; the
default build keeps its Vec-backed properties and the layouts its JIT
probes (those probes compile out under gc-heap, where no JIT runs until
S5).
- Step 1, layout census: measure named-property counts to fix the
  inline slot count (S0's hypothesis: 4).
- Step 2, header diet: RefCell<Object> is 144 B in the 192 B size
  class (48 B slack per object). Retire what gc-heap no longer needs
  in the header (the old collector's scratch words; the old registry
  slot once weak observers no longer key on it) and move array-only
  and typed-array-only fields into the dense sidecar, so the header
  plus 4 inline slots fits 192 B.
- Step 3, inline named slots: up to 4 named properties live in the
  cell; a fifth spills them all to one heap buffer. A plain object with
  at most 4 properties is then one allocation (its key layout is
  shared), and an array's `length` no longer costs an allocation of its
  own: a short array is the cell plus its element storage, which
  Decision 3 keeps separate.
- Step 4, "shapes page-resident", read as: shared key layouts become
  GC cells an object references directly, so creating or freeing an
  object counts no references. Shape ids stay the identity inline
  caches compare (never reused, so no cache can match a recycled
  address). Taken only if it measures as a win; otherwise recorded as
  a deviation.
- Step 5, payload moves, measured one at a time: map_data first (I8
  touches it anyway), then the hottest address-keyed tables found by
  the S3 survey: typed_arrays (generic [[Get]]/[[Set]] consult it with
  no guard), proxies, class_info (every user call), mapped_arguments
  (every generic [[Get]]), eval_realm_fns (every VM native fast call).
- Exit gate as planned: S2 gates green; the tripwire moves in the
  predicted direction on the microbenchmarks; fixture RSS against the
  S0 bound.

Step 1, layout census (TRUST_JS_GC_CENSUS=1, gc-heap): after every
collection each live object, and each object as its cell dies, is
counted by family and named-property count (bytecode tier; release
gc-heap shell at 51c8c26f plus this step). Share of plain objects with
more than 3 / 4 / 8 named properties, in percent:
                 live, summed over
                 collections (n)          dying (n)
  Vue            25.0/22.9/13.0 (1752)    38.7/0/0 (61393)
  React          17.2/15.5/9.1 (927)      0/0/0 (19912)
  Djot           14.1/10.2/1.5 (19087)    10.1/5.4/3.2 (65690)
  prop. access   34.6/30.8/17.9 (2652)    -
  sustained      0/0/0 (3.4M)             0/0/0 (440001)
Functions hold 2.3-2.4 named properties (more than 4: 1.8-2.5% of the
live ones) but reserve 3.3-4.4x that today; arrays hold one (length)
and 87-100% of them carry a dense sidecar. Four slots hold every
short-lived plain object on Vue, React and the sustained workload and
94.6% on Djot; three would miss Vue's 4-property temporaries (38.7% of
its dying plain objects). Decision: 4 inline named slots, S0's
hypothesis, now measured.

Measurement note (S3, corrected 2026-10-17): this machine's cores run
far below their nominal clocks. A chain of dependent register adds
(one cycle each) times the X925 cores at 1.37 GHz and the A725 cores at
1.0 GHz, with the PMU's cycle count agreeing exactly, while cpufreq
reports up to 3.9 / 2.8 GHz; no platform profile is set and the cores
sit near 53 C. The cap holds with the GPU idle, so it is not the
owner's GPU services, as first assumed. Wall times turned bimodal while
those services ran and are steady without them. Comparisons from S3
step 2 on use user-mode cycles and instructions (perf stat,
unmultiplexed, CPU 5), which no clock moves; wall time and peak RSS
are still recorded.

Step 2-3, inline named slots (gc-heap): a property map's values live in
an InlineVec (inline_vec.rs) with 4 slots in the cell; a fifth spills
them all to one heap buffer, kept if the map shrinks again. To keep the
cell in the 192 B size class the `length`/`prototype` slot memos narrow
to u16 under gc-heap (a slot past that range is not memoized):
RefCell<Object> is exactly 192 B, held there by a const assertion, and
Props 112 B. The default build keeps Vec-backed fields and the layouts
its JIT probes.
- Props templates stored by value grew with Props, mostly where they
  stay empty: every parsed function's FunctionMaps cache and every
  object-literal site's template cell. Both are boxed now; an AST
  Function is 200 B in both builds (440 B before, 584 B with inline
  slots).
- Measured (release, fat LTO, CPU 5, user-mode cycles over 5
  interleaved rounds; gc-heap at 51c8c26f against this step; the new
  benchmarks/maps-sets.js exercises Map/Set data):
                         interp     bytecode
    sustained alloc.     -10.1%     -5.5%    (instructions -2.7/-3.1%)
    property access      -1.1%      +1.3%
    maps-sets            -1.8%      -1.2%
    Vue                  -0.6%      +0.8%
    React                -1.0%      +0.3%
    Djot                 -1.3%      -1.1%
  Instructions move by at most 0.3% elsewhere. Outputs are identical
  to main everywhere. The default build's instruction counts equal
  main's on every workload; its cycles vary by -2.7..+1.7%, as S2's
  default build did against main (code layout).
- Peak RSS moves with collection timing more than with layout: the
  wall-time runs put it 4-6% lower on the sustained workload, and
  Djot's peak about 2.8 MiB higher (with malloc huge pages off). Its
  RSS trace matches S2's until the fourth collection, which comes
  later: the third left a smaller live set (3,904 vs 9,615 objects; the
  conservative scan saw different stack words), so the byte budget,
  max(8 MiB, 2 x live), admitted more garbage first.
- Two guards that skipped side-table probes for plain objects (the
  module_ns lookup in generic [[Get]] and the receiver classification
  of element feedback) measured as no change in instructions or cycles
  and were dropped.

S3 exit gate (2026-10-16)
-------------------------
Builds: main = e3310993; branch = b24a522c plus this commit (records
only). crystal aarch64, rustc 1.99.0; release = fat LTO.
- Lib suite: gc-heap 1839/0/116, default 2149/0/3; trust-js-runtime
  182/0/1 in both builds; the workspace builds.
- Test262 (release runners from b24a522c, gc-heap and default,
  TRUST_JS_TIER=interp and =bytecode, the S2 subsets, 29029 files):
  29028 pass, 0 fail, 1 skip in each run; all twelve reports (S2's and
  S3's) are byte-identical. Parity.
- GC stress + verifier + poisoning (debug b24a522c, every test
  in its own process, 900 s each): 1839 pass, 116 ignored, 0 fail;
  1819 at interval 1, 16 at 100 and 4 at 1000. Three interval-1 tests
  timed out in the parallel run (12 processes on the slower cluster's
  10 cores) and pass alone in 779-803 s, as S2's binary does in
  775-796 s; they join the slow list.
- Tripwire, predicted direction: object creation with properties gets
  cheaper and nothing else regresses beyond noise. object_alloc_bench
  (ns/op, medians of 5 interleaved runs, gc-heap S2 -> S3, main):
  templated {x,y} + drop 121.9 -> 108.3 (main 105.5);
  Object::new + drop 98.9 -> 102.9 and Props::new 5.8 -> 6.3 (a
  112 B Props to write instead of 64 B). JS workloads by cycles: see
  step 2-3 (sustained allocation -10.1/-5.5%, others -1.8..+1.3%).
- RSS on the gate fixtures against the S0 bound (peak RSS, MiB, main
  -> gc-heap S3, wall-time runs at b24a522c's binaries):
                         interp           bytecode
    Vue                  27 -> 70         27 -> 41 (+52%)
    React                13 -> 50         14 -> 38 (+171%)
    Djot                 36 -> 57         32 -> 45 (+41%)
  The bound (+25%, checked at S6 on the default tier, which under
  gc-heap is bytecode) is not met: small programs carry 10-25 MiB
  more, mostly garbage awaiting collection (the byte budget's 8 MiB
  floor) and page granularity. Open for S6 (budget policy, or a
  nursery over the cell heap).
- wasm32 acceptance: still blocked (see the S2 deviation).
- Recheck (2026-10-17, GPU idle, 7 interleaved rounds, wall time and
  cycles together): the clock held at 1.36-1.37 GHz in every run;
  gc-heap S3 against S2 is -9.5/-5.9% cycles on the sustained workload
  and -1.5..+0.9% elsewhere, wall time now agreeing with cycles; the
  default build is within -1.2..+1.1% of main's cycles.

S4 Layout B (started 2026-10-17; owner: go after the S3 fix)
------------------------------------------------------------
Stage boundary: main has no commits past the merge base e3310993.

Plan, recorded before the work (gc-heap scope as in S3; shared fixes
land in both builds). A read-only survey of environments, functions,
exotic state, teardown and address-keyed tables found, besides the
planned layout work, four gate blockers:
- trust-js-node's NapiState.wraps is keyed by object address with no
  sweep, so napi_unwrap can return a stale pointer once a wrapped
  object's address is reused.
- WrappedShadow/WrappedCross callables name their ShadowRealm by raw
  address without holding it.
- A dead ShadowRealm's Interp is dropped inside the sweep, running its
  coroutine teardown mid-collection.
- The native super() graft does not move temporal_cal (to verify).
Steps:
- Step 1, baselines: full Test262 for main and gc-heap, and the
  downstream suites (host, runtime, web, node) under gc-heap, which
  S2/S3 recorded for the runtime only.
- Step 2, the blockers above, each with a test that fails without the
  fix.
- Step 3, environment cells: retire the scope registry under gc-heap
  (diagnostics walk the live environment cells instead) and keep a
  measured number of bindings in the cell; measured mainly at the
  interp tier, whose collections are dominated by dead environments.
- Step 4, function payloads: no reference-counted per-closure state
  where a cell edge serves, and no self-pinning weak handle in the lazy
  prototype (a dead constructor pins its own cell for a collection).
- Step 5, exotic payloads: a payload home in the cell for state owned
  by one object for its whole life (map_data first, then proxies,
  typed-array and module-namespace state), each measured; tables that
  stay get their unguarded hot-path probes guarded where measurable.
- Exit gate: full Test262 parity at interp and bytecode; downstream
  suites under gc-heap; no address-keyed table without a sweep step or
  payload home; S2 gates and the tripwire recorded.

Step 1, baselines (gc-heap at f736f563; release runners, T262_WORKERS=6):
- Full Test262 (annexB, built-ins, harness, intl402, language, staging;
  53,872 files): main and gc-heap both pass 53,575 of 53,575 executed
  at interp and at bytecode; the four reports are byte-identical. S4
  must keep this.
- Downstream suites: trust-js-web, -node, -timers and -fs gain a
  gc-heap feature forwarding to trust-js-host's. Under gc-heap and in
  the default build alike: host 36/0/0, timers 5/0/0, fs no tests, web
  61/0/1, node 40/0/0, runtime 182/0/1.
- The temporal_cal graft gap does not reproduce: a PlainDate subclass
  with a non-ISO calendar keeps it in both builds.

Step 2a, N-API (trust-js-node; both builds):
- NapiState.wraps holds each wrapped object weakly, and an entry counts
  only while its handle upgrades. napi_unwrap and napi_remove_wrap of a
  new object at a dead wrapped object's address return null instead of
  the dead object's pointer; napi_wrap prunes dead entries each time the
  table doubles.
- A second gap of the same kind, which the survey missed: each call's
  napi_value arena lived in its Env, out of the collector's sight, so
  under gc-heap an object held only by a handle (one the addon made and
  has not returned yet) could be freed by a collection that a callback
  into JavaScript caused. The arena moves to NapiState and is traced as
  roots; an Env releases its own handles when its call ends.
- Tests: handles_keep_their_values_across_a_collection (gc-heap; with
  the arena untraced the handles' objects are freed: SIGSEGV in debug,
  wrong values in release) and wraps_end_with_their_objects (both
  builds; with liveness ignored, dead objects' wraps still unwrap).
  trust-js-node 42/0/0 in both builds.

Step 2b/2c plan (ShadowRealm; recorded before the work). Reproduced
first, in both builds unless noted:
- A wrapped function whose ShadowRealm object has died throws "the
  ShadowRealm is no longer available": the realm dies under it.
- A realm whose object dies while its code runs is dropped mid-call. A
  host callback that collects during a wrapped call is a use-after-free
  (default build: abort; gc-heap: hidden only while a stale stack word
  happens to keep the object).
- gc-heap: make_wrapped_shadow takes the realm out of the tree while
  the target's name/length getters run, so a collection there does not
  trace it.
Fix:
- Wrapped functions hold their ShadowRealm object as a traced edge, and
  the realm key derives from it. Cross wrappers, which run only inside
  their realm, name it through the interpreter they run in; that
  interpreter knows its owner (weakly) and its parent.
- gc-heap: a realm's roots count only while its ShadowRealm object is
  marked or while code in it runs (an entry count, which also roots the
  object). A realm kept only by its own wrapped function, stored inside
  it, is then still collected. The default build's per-heap cycle
  collector cannot collect that cross-heap cycle; recorded, not fixed.
- A collected realm, with its nested realms, drops its suspended
  coroutines without resuming them; a debug assertion keeps VM code out
  of the eviction step.
- make_wrapped_shadow uses the realm in place, as calls do.

Step 2b/2c, ShadowRealm (both builds unless noted):
- WrappedShadow holds its ShadowRealm object as a traced edge, and its
  realm key derives from it; WrappedCross holds only its target. An
  entry (evaluate, importValue, a wrapped call, CopyNameAndLength)
  records the parent and counts itself in the realm's ShadowHome, which
  also holds the owner weakly for cross wrappers. CopyNameAndLength now
  uses the realm in place instead of taking it out of the tree.
- gc-heap: a realm's roots (and its host's) are its ShadowRealm
  object's side slot. While the realm is entered they are roots, with
  the object, and the entry also holds the object on its frame.
- A collected realm and its nested realms drop their coroutines
  unresumed. VmCoro::resume debug-asserts that it is not in the
  eviction step, which gc_sweep::Evicting marks. Only an Agent's root
  interpreter flushes the function profile on drop, so a collected
  realm no longer clears its Agent's aggregate.
- Tests, each shown failing without its fix:
  shadow_realm_wrapped_function_outlives_its_shadow_realm_object (both
  builds before the fix; gc-heap with the edge untraced);
  shadow_realm_survives_a_collection_while_it_runs (default build
  before the fix: abort in Rc); shadow_realm_kept_only_through_itself_
  is_collected (gc-heap, with every realm's roots unconditional);
  collected_shadow_realm_ends_without_resuming_its_coroutines (both
  builds, with the coroutines left to Interp::drop: the assertion
  fires); shadow_realm_stays_traced_while_its_function_is_wrapped
  (gc-heap, with the realm out of the tree during the getters: freed
  jobs give a wrong sum).
- Results: trust-js lib default 2071/0/3, gc-heap 1769/0/109; the new
  tests also pass at GC stress 1 and 100. Test262 built-ins/ShadowRealm
  (no-LTO release runners): 64/64 executed at interp and bytecode in
  both builds, and under gc-heap at stress 1. Under gc-heap, host
  36/0/0 and runtime lib 110/0/1.

Step 3 plan, environment cells (recorded before the work; gc-heap,
since the default build's cycle collector needs the scope registry):
- Part 1: Scope loses its registry slot and heap handle. The cell
  shrinks from 104 B to 96 B (size class 128 -> 96), and creating a
  scope skips the heap-handle clone and the registry's two borrows.
  Environments allocate in the active heap; the scope snapshot and
  counts walk the heap's live environment cells.
- Part 2: a census of bindings per environment (live and dead) on the
  fixtures at interp and bytecode, then an inline binding capacity
  chosen from it against the classes (96/128/192), measured.
- Each part is measured as S3's steps were: perf stat cycles:u and
  instructions:u on CPU 5, peak RSS and collections, on
  property-access, sustained-allocation, maps-sets and
  benchmarks/fixtures/{djot,react,vue}.js at interp and bytecode.
  Iteration builds are no-LTO; the recorded comparison is fat LTO.

Step 3 part 1, the scope registry (gc-heap):
- A Scope has no registry slot or heap handle under gc-heap, so its
  cell is 96 B (asserted): size class 96 instead of 128. Environments
  allocate in the active heap; gc_scope_snapshot and the scope counts
  walk the heap's environment cells (Heap::live_count added). gc_check's
  scope-churn trigger is default-build only.
- Lib suites unchanged: default 2071/0/3, gc-heap 1769/0/109.
- Measured on no-LTO iteration builds: gc-heap, base 230d0005, perf
  stat on CPU 5, 7 interleaved rounds, medians; outputs identical.
  Cycles at interp: pa +1.4%, sa -4.0%, ms -1.6%, djot -2.0%, react
  -3.0%, vue -1.5%. At bytecode: pa -2.8%, sa -3.7%, ms -3.1%, djot
  -0.1%, react +0.5%, vue +0.3%. Peak RSS: sa -10.4% (interp) and
  -18.5% (bytecode), react interp -12.0%, but pa interp +12.5%, djot
  bytecode +6.9% and vue interp +3.6%.
- The RSS rises: the byte budget counts cells, not the binding vectors
  beside them. property-access at interp allocates almost nothing but
  environments. With 96-byte cells it collects 18 times instead of 24,
  so about a third more dead environments are alive at the peak, each
  with a 192-byte binding vector (Vec capacity 4 on first push). Part 2
  moves the common binding into the cell.

Step 3 part 2 plan, one binding in the cell (recorded before the work):
- Census (TRUST_JS_GC_CENSUS, environments added to S3's census;
  no-LTO gc-heap, dead environments). At interp: block environments
  hold exactly one binding in 51% (pa, react), 64% (djot), 66% (sa)
  and 75% (vue), and two in most of the rest (pa and react 48.5%).
  Function environments: react 98% one binding, vue 58% one and 13.5%
  two, djot mostly 3-8, pa all 3, sa all 0. Nearly every environment
  with a binding carries a 192-byte vector beside its cell. Bytecode
  creates 1-2 orders of magnitude fewer environments and sizes their
  vectors exactly.
- Change: under gc-heap a VarMap's entries (Small and Indexed) are an
