  InlineVec with one inline entry (48 B), so the cell goes from 96 to
  128 B (size class 128, asserted). An environment with one binding
  then allocates nothing beside its cell. Bytecode activations and
  block records build their entries in place instead of cloning a
  vector. The default build keeps its Vec.
- Expected per dead interp environment: 288 -> 128 B with one binding;
  -64 B with two (a 96-byte buffer instead of 192); +32 B with none or
  with 3-4. The native JIT reads Small storage as a Vec; it is refused
  under gc-heap until S5, so there the layout probe reports none.

Step 3 part 2, one binding in the cell (gc-heap):
- VarMap's Small and Indexed entries are an InlineVec with one inline
  entry. InlineVec's buffer pointer now overlays its in-place elements,
  so its header is two 32-bit words. The environment cell is exactly
  128 B (asserted); object cells shrink from 192 to 184 B, staying in
  the same class. Bytecode activations and block records collect their
  entries in place, and the native JIT's Small-storage probe reports
  no layout under gc-heap. Census: environments added to S3's census
  (bindings per record, storage beside the cell).
- Lib suites: default 2071/0/3, gc-heap 1770/0/109 (one new InlineVec
  test). Outputs identical to base on every fixture and tier, in both
  builds.
- Measured as part 1 (no-LTO gc-heap, base 230d0005, 7 rounds; part 2
  includes part 1). Cycles at interp: pa -1.1%, sa -4.7%, ms -0.7%,
  djot -1.4%, react -4.2%, vue -1.9%. At bytecode: pa -4.7%, sa -3.8%,
  ms -2.7%, djot +0.4%, react -2.1%, vue +0.3%; the two rises are inside
  run-to-run ranges. Instructions fall everywhere (-0.2..-4.6%)
  except djot bytecode, unchanged. Peak RSS falls everywhere:
  interp pa -16.6%, sa -8.1%, ms -13.8%, djot -8.3%, react -19.4%,
  vue -16.0%; bytecode pa -18.1%, sa -6.3%, ms -3.3%, djot -2.8%,
  react -9.4%, vue -1.4%. Part 1's RSS rises are gone. sa gives back
  some of part 1's RSS gain: its 400k empty function environments
  each grow by 32 B.

Step 3 recorded comparison (fat LTO; base 230d0005, step 3 bb394ad0;
perf stat cycles:u/instructions:u, CPU 5, 7 interleaved rounds,
medians; outputs identical everywhere):
- gc-heap cycles at interp: pa +0.0%, sa -3.2%, ms -1.4%, djot -0.8%,
  react -4.9%, vue -3.4%. At bytecode: pa -1.5%, sa -1.3%, ms -3.0%,
  djot +0.4%, react +0.6%, vue +0.3% (the rises sit inside the
  run-to-run ranges). Instructions fall everywhere (-0.1..-4.4%).
- gc-heap peak RSS at interp: pa -16.2%, sa -7.8%, ms -5.2%, djot
  -10.3%, react -35.8%, vue -15.7%. At bytecode: pa -26.2%, sa -6.1%,
  ms -3.4%, djot -14.6%, react -9.9%, vue -1.1%.
- Default build: cycles -1.4..+0.2%, instructions within 0.2%. RSS is
  within its run-to-run spread, which is bimodal on the small fixtures
  (pa bytecode 12.0-14.1 MiB in both). Its code changed only in the
  shared scope constructor and how fixed bindings are collected.

Step 4 plan, function payloads (recorded before the work; gc-heap):
- 4a: a deferred `prototype` (LazyFunctionPrototype) names its function
  through a weak handle, used only to set `constructor` when the
  prototype materializes. Each constructor's creation then allocates a
  weak cell and registers it, and a dead constructor's cell stays
  reserved (a zombie) until the collection after its death. A lazy word
  lives only in its function's properties (cloning one materializes it),
  so under gc-heap the function is named by an untraced handle instead.
  The default build keeps the weak handle: a strong one would be a
  reference cycle there.
- 4b: each closure's Callable::User holds an Rc<UserCallable>: a malloc
  of 64 B at creation, and a reference-count round trip on every call
  (the call path clones its Callable). Under gc-heap the UserCallable
  becomes a payload cell (32 B class) that traces its environment, the
  function object traces the cell, and handles are Copy. The JIT's
  UserCallable probe reports no layout under gc-heap (refused until
  S5).
- Each part measured like step 3 (no-LTO gc-heap iteration builds; a
  fat-LTO recorded comparison at the end of the step).

Step 4 deviation, recorded before the work: a page pool (4c).
- Measured first (fat LTO, against step 3): 4a's cycles are within
  +-1.2% everywhere, with fewer instructions at vue/djot interp. 4a+4b
  cycles: djot bytecode -2.2%, ms interp -1.5%, vue interp -1.4%,
  react interp -1.0%, pa bytecode +1.3%, sa within 0.3%. (At no-LTO,
  4b cost sa 1-3%: the cell allocation path does more work than
  malloc's thread cache for 400k short-lived closures.)
- But peak RSS rose where pages empty out: vue bytecode +4-5 MiB with
  4a, djot bytecode +4 MiB with 4a+4b (distributions disjoint). The
  heap itself shrank: with 4a, vue bytecode keeps 47 pages after a
  collection instead of 125, because zombie constructor cells no
  longer pin pages. gc_core frees every empty page to the allocator at
  once and takes fresh zeroed 64 KiB-aligned blocks for new ones, so
  the pages 4a empties churn through glibc each cycle and fragment it.
- 4c: gc_core keeps empty pages in a bounded pool (up to the larger of
  64 pages and the live page count) and reuses them, re-zeroed, before
  allocating; teardown frees the pool. Measured with 4a and 4b.

Step 4 results (gc-heap; parts committed separately):
- 4a: under gc-heap, a deferred prototype names its function with an
  untraced handle. Test dead_constructors_leave_no_reserved_cells:
  1000 dead constructors are freed by the collection that finds them,
  and with a weak handle restored their cells stay reserved
  (2052 -> 3054 live cells).
- 4c: gc_core keeps released pages for reuse, up to the larger of 64
  and the live page count. Test emptied_pages_are_reused.
- 4b: a closure's UserCallable is a payload cell (32 B) traced from its
  function object; it traces its environment. memory.rs identifies it
  by address; the JIT's code-keyed call probe reports no layout under
  gc-heap.
- No-LTO measurement against step 3 (CPU 5, 7 rounds; outputs
  identical). 4a+4b+4c cycles are within +-1.3% except sa (+2.1%
  bytecode, +1.3% interp; 4b's cell path for 400k short-lived
  closures) and react bytecode +1.3%. vue interp -1.3%, djot interp
  -0.5%. Peak RSS falls: djot -18.3% (interp) / -8.8% (bytecode), ms
  -11.9/-13.1%, sa -4.1/-2.3%, vue -2.4/-1.8%, react flat, pa +0.8%
  at interp. Without 4c, 4a and 4b had raised bytecode RSS by 4-6 MiB
  (vue, djot).

Step 5 plan, exotic payloads (recorded before the work; gc-heap):
- Survey (read-only): Map/Set methods probe map_data by address once
  or twice per call (two for add/delete/size/clear, three for
  getOrInsert), and iteration once per step plus the iterator table.
  Proxies are probed on calls, constructs and [[Get]]/[[Set]] misses
  once any proxy exists. Map and Set objects are Exotic::None today
  and take the receiver inline caches.
- map_data first: under gc-heap a Map/Set's OrderedCollection moves
  into its cell as Exotic::Collection(Box<_>) (Exotic stays 16 B; the
  cell stays 184 B). Methods reach it through the object they hold,
  borrowed for one step and never across JavaScript, instead of by
  address. The inline-cache checks that admit plain exotics also admit
  Collection. Collections stop pinning themselves (the pin only kept
  the table owner's address). Exotic loses its Clone derive (the
  super() graft moves the payload). The default build keeps map_data.
- Then proxies, typed-array state and module namespaces as measured
  results warrant; any not taken is recorded with its measurement.

Step 4 recorded comparison (fat LTO; step 3 bb394ad0, step 4 a73169a0;
CPU 5, 7 interleaved rounds, medians; outputs identical everywhere):
- gc-heap cycles at interp: pa -0.3%, sa +0.0%, ms -0.3%, djot -0.4%,
  react -0.8%, vue -1.0%. At bytecode: pa +0.4%, sa +0.0%, ms +1.2%,
  djot -0.6%, react -0.6%, vue +1.8% (its ranges overlap). Instructions
  within +-0.7%.
- gc-heap peak RSS at interp: pa -1.6%, sa -4.1%, ms -10.7%, djot
  -17.7%, react +0.9%, vue -4.5%. At bytecode: pa -0.6%, sa -2.2%, ms
  -16.5%, djot -4.7%, react -4.0%, vue -2.4%.
- Default build: cycles within +-0.7%, instructions unchanged, RSS
  within its spread.

Step 5a, a Map's or Set's data in its cell (gc-heap):
- Exotic::Collection(Box<OrderedCollection>) holds [[MapData]]/[[SetData]].
  It is declared next to None, and Exotic stays 16 B (the cell stays
  184 B). Interp::collection/collection_mut borrow the data with its
  object (map_data in the default build), and set_collection and
  move_collection install and move it. Map/Set methods brand-check the
  object they already hold (coll_obj_kind). Iteration and forEach
  borrow the data for one step, never across JavaScript. Maps and Sets
  no longer pin themselves; map_data, its sweep and its side-slot entry
  are default-build only.
- Inline-cache and fast-path checks that admitted plain objects use
  Exotic::is_ordinary (None or Collection), so Maps and Sets keep their
  caches; element-receiver feedback classifies them as ordinary. Exotic
  lost its Clone derive: the super() graft moves the native object's
  exotic state, and JSON.stringify classifies its space argument
  instead of copying it.
- Lib suites: default 2071/0/3, gc-heap 1772/0/107 (two refcount-
  collector tests that touch map_data are now compiled out under
  gc-heap instead of ignored). Test262 (no-LTO runners) built-ins/Map,
  Set, Map/SetIteratorPrototype, WeakMap, WeakSet, JSON/stringify,
  Array/prototype/concat and language/statements/class/subclass: 1079/
  1079 in both builds at interp and bytecode, and under gc-heap at GC
  stress 1 at bytecode. At interp under stress, two concat typed-array
  tests time out, as they do with step 2's runner (230d0005).
- Measured (no-LTO gc-heap against step 4, CPU 5, 7 rounds, outputs
  identical): ms -6.0% cycles at bytecode and -5.3% at interp
  (instructions -2.8/-1.9%, peak RSS -6.1/-5.0%). Others within
  +-1.6%. pa interp is +1.4% instructions, which a symbol profile puts
  in Props::get and get_member_recv_impl inlining, not in the new
  checks; it is left to the fat-LTO comparison.

Step 5b/5c, the remaining exotic tables (gc-heap and default):
- Probes counted per table (a scratch build whose proxy, typed-array
  and module-namespace tables count lookups, by call site). Proxies:
  vue 3.4M probes at bytecode and 8.5M at interp against 19 proxies;
  the other fixtures make essentially none outside unguarded sites. At
  ~5 ns a probe that is at most ~1% of vue's runtime, for a 53-site
  change. Moving proxy state into the cell is not taken in S4: S5's
  specializing tier needs Proxy's state in the object anyway, so it
  moves then, measured there. Typed arrays and module namespaces: no
  fixture creates either, but their tables were probed on [[Get]]/
  [[Set]] slow paths for every object (pa interp 5.7M typed-array
  probes on an empty table, 1M namespace and 1M proxy probes). Not
  moved; their hot probes are now guarded.
- 5c: typed_array_at and is_namespace answer an empty table without a
  probe, and [[Set]]'s prototype walk uses proxy_at (already guarded).
  Measured against 5a (no-LTO gc-heap, 7 rounds): pa interp -0.8%
  cycles (-0.2% instructions); everything else within +-0.5%.

Exit gate finding, fixed before the gate (pre-existing since S2):
- The per-test stress run failed one test,
  bytecode_fragment_cache::shared_syntax_never_shares_fragment_ic_state_
  between_engines (two engines on one thread run one parsed program,
  in two rounds). Under GC stress the heap verifier reported a
  function's `name` holding a cell outside its heap. Labeled tracing
  located the edge (a props value, key "name"); bisecting located the
  exposure in 4c (with 4c reverted the test passes; with 4a or 4b
  reverted it still fails), not the cause.
- Cause: ast::FunctionMaps, cached on the parsed function and so shared
  by every engine that runs the code, held `name` strings allocated in
  the heap that first instantiated the function, pinned by thread-local
  roots. A second engine copied them into its closures (a cross-heap
  reference), and after the first engine dropped, freed cells. Page
  reuse used to land the stale addresses in live pages, hiding them;
  the page pool changed that, and the verifier saw them.
- Fix: FunctionMaps records the heap that built it (heap ids are never
  reused), and its strings are rooted in that heap for the heap's
  lifetime (GcState.template_strings, traced with the interned
  strings). Another heap's closures take their own name strings.
  run_program also activates its interpreter's agent (persistently,
  as the engine's entry points do: a scoped guard switched back
  afterwards and broke nested_calls_keep_the_surrounding_agent_
  activation_on_the_fast_path), so a program allocates in its own heap
  whatever heap was active when it was called. Test
  function_name_templates_serve_only_their_heap: two engines, the first
  dropped before the second runs; with the heap check removed, the
  second's closure names are not cells of its heap.

S4 exit gate (2026-10-17)
-------------------------
Builds: main = e3310993; branch = fd1da511 plus this commit (records
only). crystal aarch64, rustc 1.99.0; release = fat LTO unless noted.
- Lib suite: --features embed 2153/0/3 (S3: 2149/0/3; four ShadowRealm
  tests), embed,gc-heap 1848/0/114 (S3: 1839/0/116; nine new tests, and
  two refcount-collector tests compiled out under gc-heap instead of
  ignored).
- Test262, full (no-LTO release runners from a7e7da88, gc-heap and
  default, TRUST_JS_TIER=interp and =bytecode; annexB, built-ins,
  harness, intl402, language, staging; 53,872 files): 53,575 of 53,575
  executed pass in each run, and all four reports are byte-identical to
  main's from step 1, as they were at 0eb6ff6d. Parity. fd1da511 only
  changes run_program's activation, which the runner reaches through
  Engine::eval with its agent already active.
- Downstream suites (fd1da511, both builds alike): host 36/0/0, timers
  5/0/0, fs (no tests), web 61/0/1, node 42/0/0 (step 2a's two tests),
  runtime 182/0/1 (45 test binaries).
- GC stress + verifier + poisoning (debug fd1da511, embed,gc-heap,
  every test in its own process, 900 s each): 1848 pass, 114 ignored,
  0 fail; 1825 at interval 1, 19 at 100 and 4 at 1000. The gate's first
  run (0eb6ff6d) failed one test: the finding above.
- Address-keyed tables: gc_evict_dead_owners destructures Interp
  exhaustively and classifies every field. Tables keyed by an object's
  address are evicted there or swept with the weak state; the others
  are keyed by values they keep alive, through weak pins (a dead
  address stays reserved until the pin is pruned), or not by address.
  Under gc-heap map_data and the scope registry are gone: Map/Set data
  and environments live in their cells. Tables outside Interp are S2's
  I8 inventory; S4 adds none that outlives a collection (Marking's
  shadow_owners lives for one). Downstream, NapiState.wraps is keyed by
  address but holds each object weakly (step 2a): a dead object's entry
  misses on lookup and is pruned as the table grows. The survey found
  no other.
- I5 teardown: the plan's survey audited every Drop with effects beyond
  freeing memory, in the engine and downstream. Downstream resources
  (executors, task and thread pools, workers, sockets and servers,
  native libraries, TLS/net/dgram, child processes) live in OpState
  slots or Runtime fields; JavaScript holds integer ids, and they close
  explicitly or at Agent teardown, before the heap is released. In the
  engine one teardown depended on collection timing, a collected
  ShadowRealm's Interp dropping inside the sweep (fixed in step 2b/2c).
  The downstream suites now run under gc-heap and pass with the default
  build's counts. Found and left open, none of them caused by the
  collector: napi_wrap never runs finalizers (by design, noted there:
  memory is reclaimed at process exit); AddonRegistry says addons stay
  loaded for the process lifetime but lives in OpState, so they are
  dlclosed at Agent teardown; SqliteState has no Drop, so databases
  still open at Agent teardown are never closed; and native memory
  released by FinalizationRegistry callbacks (TextDecoder, compression
  codecs, wasm store handles) is not counted in the byte budget (S6's
  budget policy).
- Tripwire, JS workloads (base 230d0005, S3 with step 2's fixes, against
  fd1da511; perf stat cycles:u/instructions:u on CPU 5, 7 interleaved
  rounds, medians; clock 1.35-1.41 GHz in every run; outputs identical
  everywhere). gc-heap cycles at interp: pa -0.0%, sa -3.1%, ms -6.1%,
  djot -1.6%, react -5.1%, vue -4.6%. At bytecode: pa -2.4%, sa -0.7%,
  ms -8.3%, djot -1.9%, react +2.4%, vue -1.6%; react rechecked over 15
  rounds is -0.4%. Instructions fall everywhere (-0.1..-4.3%) except
  vue bytecode (+0.2%). gc-heap peak RSS at interp: pa -16.6%, sa
  -11.7%, ms -21.8%, djot -26.3%, react -21.1%, vue -17.1%. At bytecode:
  pa -17.3%, sa -8.1%, ms -20.5%, djot -18.0%, react -8.0%, vue -5.3%.
  Default build: cycles -2.3..+0.8% and instructions -0.3..+0.0%, except
  react bytecode at +3.6% cycles (+3.1% over the 15-round recheck, with
  0.2% fewer instructions and overlapping ranges); RSS within its spread
  (pa interp takes the same bimodal values, 11.7 or 12.7-13.7 MiB, in
  both).
- Tripwire, object_alloc_bench (release lib test binaries; ns/op,
  medians of 5 interleaved runs, base -> fd1da511): gc-heap templated
  {x,y} + drop 108.5 -> 111.6 (+2.9%), Object::new + drop 102.4 ->
  102.9, Props::new 6.3 -> 5.9, the rest within 1.5%. Default build:
  templated 103.1 -> 99.2 (-3.8%), the rest within 0.5%. Bisected
  (gc-heap, 5 runs each, spreads under 0.4 ns): 106.8 at step 3's end
  (bb394ad0), 107.6 at step 4's (a73169a0), 111.6 after step 5 and the
  gate fixes. Not added work: Object::new_with_parts has the same 249
  instructions in both binaries, its operands differing only by GcState
  fields 32 bytes further on (template_strings, a7e7da88) and by address
  bits; the whole bench executes the same instructions (medians of 3
  runs: 10.43G, +0.02%) in 1.6% more cycles. The default build's -3.8%
  is placement in the other direction: its function differs only in
  address bits.
- wasm32 acceptance: still blocked (see the S2 deviation).

S4 review fixes (outside review of e31e419b, 2026-10-17)
-------------------------------------------------------
- [P2] The page pool kept up to half of a heap that emptied at once.
  release_page checked the cap (the larger of 64 and the pages in use)
  as each page went, so the cap shrank with the pages still to go:
  300 emptied pages left 150 pooled, and nothing trimmed them later.
  The sweep now pools every page it releases and trims the pool to the
  cap when it is done. Test spare_pages_stay_capped_after_the_heap_
  shrinks: a 300-page burst that dies, then one that keeps 100 pages
  and shrinks to none (64, 100 and 64 pooled; with the release-time
  check, 150 after the first).
- [P2] Function-name templates rooted their strings for the heap's
  lifetime (a7e7da88's GcState.template_strings), so discarded code
  left its names behind. Each FunctionMaps now owns its roots
  (value::TemplateRoots, shared by clones), and the heap lists them
  weakly (GcState.template_roots): it traces the live ones and drops
  dead entries as it goes. The collection that finds discarded code
  dead still traces its templates, which die in its sweep, so their
  strings go at the next collection. Roots are registered only in the
  heap that built the template, so heap isolation is unchanged. Test
  discarded_function_templates_release_their_name_strings: 16
  discarded functions with 64 KiB names; with the roots leaked for the
  heap's lifetime, live bytes after two collections go from 230,128
  to 1,278,998.
- [P2] Sustained-allocation evidence. The validation plan asks at every
  stage from S2 for post-GC live bytes, allocated against live pages,
  the pause distribution and total GC %; S2 recorded them, S3 and S4
  did not. GcStats gains pages_spare (released pages kept for reuse),
  and TRUST_JS_GC_LOG lines report it. The measurements follow in the
  next entry (a batch render took the machine when these fixes were
  ready).
- Lib suites: --features embed 2153/0/3, embed,gc-heap 1850/0/114 (the
  two new tests). Clippy clean for both feature sets; gc-heap with
  heap-bridge checks. The new tests pass at GC stress 1.

S4 review fixes, measured (2026-10-17)
--------------------------------------
- The pool's cap as first enforced (af79a759: the larger of 64 and the
  pages in use) brought back allocator churn where the live heap is
  small. Vue at interp keeps 0.4 MiB live in about 39 pages while each
  cycle's 8 MiB budget refills about 100: 64 waited in the pool and 36
  a cycle went back to glibc and came back fresh (peak RSS 56.9 MiB;
  58.2 with fd1da511's release-time check). The cap is now the pages
  the next cycle's allocation budget can fill (trigger minus live
  bytes, at least 64), applied once the sweep has set that budget. All
  of Vue's released pages wait for the next cycle (peak RSS 42.6 MiB),
  and a heap that shrank keeps no more than its smaller budget. Test
  spare_pages_follow_the_next_cycles_budget replaces spare_pages_stay_
  capped_after_the_heap_shrinks: with the engine's 8 MiB floor, a
  300-page burst of garbage leaves 128 pooled (150 with the release-time
  check); 300 live pages raise the budget so a 200-page burst on top
  stays pooled; when they die the pool returns to 128.
- Contention: another session's batch renders (Blender, 4-12 threads)
  ran during a first measurement of these fixes. measure.py now records
  the CPU time other processes used during each run (/proc/stat); the
  runs below saw 0.24-1.14 other cores (desktop background, no render),
  and all are kept.
- Sustained-allocation workload (benchmarks/sustained-allocation.js,
  TRUST_JS_PERF_METRICS=1 and TRUST_JS_GC_LOG=1; fat LTO, CPU 5, 5 runs
  each; main = e3310993, S3 = S4's base 230d0005, S4 = this commit;
  medians, pauses over all runs):
                     collections  pause total  max     GC share  peak RSS
    interp   main    16           700 ms       82 ms   25.2%     180 MiB
             S3      20           686 ms       47 ms   29.1%     206 MiB
             S4      20           655 ms       45 ms   28.8%     185 MiB
    bytecode main    16           486 ms       63 ms   28.4%     147 MiB
             S3      20           566 ms       39 ms   39.1%     198 MiB
             S4      20           543 ms       36 ms   37.5%     184 MiB
  Pause distribution (S4, 100 pauses per tier): interp 6 under 5 ms, 9
  at 5-10, 20 at 10-25, 65 at 25-50; bytecode the same (S3: interp
  5/7/20/68, bytecode 5/10/20/65; main mostly 25-100 ms). Post-GC live
  bytes 36.8 MiB in every build and tier, in at most 621-625 small-cell
  pages (94-95% of their bytes live, as at S2); after a collection up
  to 570-575 released pages (36 MiB) wait for the next cycle's 36.8 MiB
  budget. The GC share stays far from S6's 5% target.
- Tripwire recheck (gc-heap, fd1da511 against this commit; fat LTO,
  perf stat on CPU 5, 7 rounds): instructions equal everywhere
  (+-0.1%), cycles -1.4..+2.3%, outputs identical. Peak RSS: vue interp
  -27.2% (58.0 -> 42.2 MiB), react interp -16.0%, ms -9.6% (bytecode)
  and -15.6% (interp), pa bytecode -0.6%; elsewhere +0.9..+6.4% (+1.5
  to 3.7 MiB), where glibc used to recycle freed pages for other
  allocations and the pool now keeps them for the next cycle.
- Lib suite gc-heap 1850/0/114 (the default build has no gc_core);
  clippy clean.

S5 JIT re-pointing (started 2026-10-17)
---------------------------------------
Stage boundary: main has no commits past the merge base e3310993.

Plan, recorded before the work. Two read-only surveys (emitted code and
layouts; collector interaction, caches and tests) found:
- Generated code does reference-count work at about 140 sites in nine
  families (exec clone/drop/release, drop-helper calls, 47 inline
  commit decrements, increments, 46 "count > 1" guards, frame teardown,
  x64), all assuming a strong count at payload+0. Under gc-heap word 0
  of an object cell is its RefCell flag, of a string cell its append
  frontier: any of them corrupts a cell. jit_layout() panics under
  gc-heap (it probes Gc::strong_count).
- Layout probes that pass but would be wrong under gc-heap: named
  fields are an InlineVec<Property, 4> (u32 len, u32 cap, inline
  elements or a heap pointer), probed as a Vec; SlotMemo is u16, read
  as u32; Exotic::Collection is not None to the JIT's guards; the
  function probe expects an RcBox for Rc<Function>; environments are
  header-less cells whose first binding is inline.
- Correction to the S0 record (I7): JIT registers never hold sole
  references, and the native stack is scanned, but the canonical slots
  and operand stacks of JIT frames are not stack-resident. They live in
  vm_pool vectors, boxed JitFrame records (frame_pool, untraced) and,
  for OSR and continuations, a VM stack whose length is set to 0 for
  the native run. None is registered with the collector, and the
  JIT-only Rust helpers (about 7k lines) never ran under GC stress.
- Polls emitted into JIT code test the object count only, not gc-heap's
  byte budget, stress interval or pending request; direct JIT-to-JIT
  calls skip gc_check_amortized.
- Gates that pass vacuously: --tier=jit and set_tier(Jit) silently run
  bytecode under gc-heap, and difftest files a crashing child as
  inconclusive.
- Possible address reuse (VM and JIT, unconfirmed): pruning a dead
  creation pin does not fence the IC_CREATE states holding the
  prototype's raw address.
- Coverage under gc-heap: 31 lib tests ignored as "needs the JIT", 257
  tests in 38 JIT modules compiled out, and about 20 reference-count
  ignores that are JIT-ABI tests needing rooting counterparts.

Steps (aarch64; x64 stays compiled but refused under gc-heap until S6's
zombie pass; the default build keeps its code, checked by identical
code dumps where a step touches emission):
- Step 1, honest gates and baselines: difftest builds with gc-heap and
  reports crashes apart from timeouts and memory kills (a crash is a
  failure); --tier=jit says when it is clamped; fixture and gate runs
  prove native compiles (TRUST_JS_TIER_LOG or the perf metrics' compile
  counts). Baselines in the default build: difftest seed 7 count 150,
  the fixtures at jit (outputs and compile counts), and code dumps for
  the steps that must leave its emitted code unchanged.
- Step 2, the creation-pin candidate: an address-reuse test for
  IC_CREATE (and the other pinned caches' prune paths) in both builds;
  fence what it finds.
- Step 3, ownership emitters (default build, no code change): every
  reference-count sequence goes through named emitters (exec clone/
  drop/release, rc increment, non-final guard, frame teardown, the
  PropertyLayout Rc kept apart), on ARM64 and x64. Gate: JIT tests
  pass; code dumps byte-identical on the fixtures.
- Step 4, JitLayout v2: an ownership mode (reference count at an offset,
  or traced); no count probe under gc-heap; the InlineVec header and
  SlotMemo width exported; the function probe, Collection tag, scope
  and binding storage probed per build. Layout tests in both builds.
- Step 5, one named-fields accessor emitter (Vec, or InlineVec: inline
  while cap is 0) and the gc-heap scope small-storage probe. Default
  code dumps unchanged.
- Step 6, gc-heap emission: the ownership emitters emit nothing under
  gc-heap (tag guards and the layout Rc stay), Collection passes the
  ordinary-object guards. A test-only switch admits the JIT under
  gc-heap.
- Step 7, rooting: active JIT frames are registered (Rust entries and
  machine-code direct calls); helpers that can collect see the
  published stack pointer; slots, operands up to it, and the context's
  this/return/error/activation are traced; borrowed frames keep their
  stack length; frame_pool is traced or empty. Tests force a collection
  inside each collecting helper family under the verifier.
- Step 7b, polls: JIT polls and direct-call entry see the heap's
  "collection due" (budget, stress, pending). Test: call-only and
  loop-only JIT code collects at stress 1.
- Step 8, admission: Jit is admitted under gc-heap on aarch64; the 38
  modules and 31 ignores return, the JIT-ABI reference-count tests are
  rewritten as rooting tests; per-test GC stress with the verifier and
  the JIT on; fixes as found.
- Step 9, the tagged word in execution: under gc-heap the execution
  word is Copy with no ownership (lazy prototypes kept out of
  execution), `this` travels packed, constant strings are immediates;
  Binding.value stays wide (measured; any part not taken is recorded).
- Exit gate: lib suite at the default tier in both builds; difftest
  --seed 7 --count 150 agrees in both builds with crashes counted;
  fixtures correct at --tier=jit with native compiles shown; per-test
  GC stress + verifier with the JIT on; tripwire (fixtures at jit, both
  builds) recorded. Outside review checkpoint.

Decided before the work: "allocation slow path may collect" (the plan)
would reopen S2's safepoint-only collection (I9) and its I1 audit.
Instead the JIT's allocating helpers end in an explicit poll once their
result sits in a canonical slot, so I7 is exercised at those points
under stress without collecting inside allocation (a deviation).

Step 1, honest gates and baselines:
- difftest: a --check child that dies without being killed for its
  budget is rerun one tier at a time (--observe, same caps). When the
  interp oracle completes and bytecode or jit crashes, the seed fails
  as a crash (saved to the corpus, and replayed as a regression); when
  the oracle crashes too, or the allocation cap aborts the tier, it
  stays inconclusive. The crate gains a gc-heap feature and says when
  its jit tier runs as bytecode. Tier::admitted is public, and the
  engine binary says when --tier=jit is not available.
  TRUST_JS_JIT_CODEDUMP=* dumps every chunk.
- Baselines (no-LTO release builds of this commit unless noted):
  difftest --seed 7 --count 150 (interp oracle against bytecode and
  jit): 138 agree, 12 skipped, in both builds (gc-heap's jit tier runs
  as bytecode). Fixtures at --tier=jit in the default build (fat LTO,
  af79a759): vue 55, react 9 and djot 160 native compiles, all
  successful; outputs identical to this commit's default jit and
  gc-heap bytecode runs. gc-heap compiles none.
- Code dumps (default build, ASLR off, every chunk; MOVZ/MOVN/MOVK
  immediates, ADRP pages and branches that leave the chunk masked,
  since they carry addresses): vue 55 chunks (202,161 words), react 9
  (28,748), djot 160 (1,331,341), identical run to run. Steps that must
  leave the default build's emitted code alone are compared with them.

Step 2, the creation-pin candidate (both builds; pre-existing, also on
main):
- Confirmed. A property-creation cache (IC_CREATE) proves "no setter,
  read-only or own copy of this name on the chain" for the receiver's
  prototype, named by address and an epoch. When that prototype died,
  gc_prune_dead_pins dropped its pin and the address became free, but
  the cache stayed valid: a new object at the address, given a setter
  for the name (a new key on an unmarked object does not bump the
  epoch), was skipped by `this.x = v`, which created an own property
  instead. Pruning a dead creation pin now bumps the epoch first,
  fencing every creation cache, as dead global-environment pins fence
  the call caches. The other pinned caches (construct, overflow,
  callback, proxy-trap, capacity hints) drop each entry with its pin.
- Test creation_caches_do_not_trust_a_reused_prototype_address (both
  builds, bytecode and jit): fill the cache through Reflect.construct,
  let the prototype die, allocate until an object takes its address,
  define a setter there, construct again. Without the fence the setter
  is skipped ("0:true") in the bytecode VM and in the native template.
- Lib suites: embed 2154/0/3, embed,gc-heap 1851/0/114.

Step 3, ownership emitters (default build, no code change; 95d3d589):
- Every reference-count sequence the JIT emits goes through a named
  emitter that reads one description, JitOwnership (from
  JitLayout::ownership(); for now only Refcount { strong_off }).
  ARM64 (jit_ownership.rs): the execution-word owner guard; clone,
  shared drop and word release; increments, the non-final guard and
  its load and check halves, commit and non-final decrements,
  release-shared and release-unless-last; drop-helper calls; the
  direct-call frame teardown (surplus arguments, callee, `this`,
  slots). The PropertyLayout Rc keeps three emitters of its own. x64:
  an increment and two guarded decrements. Routed, by the step's
  census: 21 clones, 7 shared drops, 6 word releases and one inline
  copy, the drop-helper calls, 44 inline decrements, 4 increments, 41
  guards, the frame teardown, 7 x64 sites, 3 PropertyLayout sites,
  and one site the survey missed (the computed-method key release).
  The 20 sequences that hardcoded offset 0 read the description. Tag
  guards, alias compares and gates stay at their sites.
- Code dumps (default build, ASLR off) against the step-1 baselines:
  vue (55 chunks) and react (9) identical; djot (160) identical when
  each MOVZ/MOVK run is compared as one masked address. Compared word
  by word, three or four djot chunks differ: the binary's new size
  moves its heap, a string constant's address needs one 16-bit lane
  more or fewer, and later in-chunk branches shift by a word
  (disassembled; nothing else differs). With the base binary's
  program break aligned to the new one's, the word-by-word comparison
  matches as well, also with per-chunk and inline stubs and on 20
  other benchmark workloads. A temporary unit test found the x64
  emitters byte-identical to the sequences they replace.
- Lib suites: embed 2154/0/3; embed,gc-heap 1851/0/114 in four runs
  (the step-2 test failed in a fifth, fixed below); clippy clean in
  both builds.

Step 2 follow-up, a flaky test (gc-heap): the address-reuse test
failed in some full multi-threaded runs (every --nocapture run),
never alone. Each tier made a fresh heap, which in a full run
allocated at the previous tier's addresses: the second tier's
prototype sat where the first tier's reused object had been, and
that object's handle, left in the test function's frame, retained it
(the retention trace named the test's own frame, 496 bytes below its
record). Alone, the two heaps landed apart. Each tier now runs in a
helper between stack scrubs (`scrubbed`, the embedder-entry
wrapper), so no handle outlives its tier. Full gc-heap runs after the
fix: 4 of 4 pass (1851/0/114), 3 of them --nocapture, which failed 3
of 3 before; the default build passes it.

Step 4, JitLayout v2 (6c665386):
- jit_layout() no longer panics under gc-heap; each probe measures the
  build's own representation on live values. Facts of the build's types
  live in a JitRepr, probed once per process, which JitLayout reaches
  through the word rc_strong_off occupied. Deviation from the plan's
  "fields on JitLayout": every Interp caches a JitLayout inline and
  generated code reads Interp fields at probed offsets, so 120 bytes of
  new fields moved all of them and changed every default-build dump.
- Ownership: Refcount { strong_off } in the default build (found in a
  live object's RcBox header as an extra owner comes and goes, checked
  against the count), Traced under gc-heap (no count probe). The
  rc_strong_off gates are ownership predicates (fits_unscaled,
  count_first, x64 fits_disp32): unchanged in the default build, true
  for Traced. Every ownership emitter's Traced arm is unreachable until
  step 6.
- PropertyLayout's count probed on a live Rc in both builds. Handle
  offsets measured from the stored word (under gc-heap the cell
  address; gc_data_off 0). Named fields: Vec { ptr, len, cap } by
  default; under gc-heap an InlineVec (u32 length and capacity, the 4
  elements in place exactly while the capacity word is 0, else a heap
  pointer), probed in place and spilled. SlotMemo width and "no slot"
  (4 / u32::MAX; 2 / 0xFFFF). The call probe verified on a live closure
  of a parsed function in both builds (valid under gc-heap now). The
  Collection tag (directly after None's, so one range test can admit
  both). Binding storage per build (gc-heap: the first binding in the
  environment cell); the parent field checked on a live child scope.
- No path compiles JIT code under gc-heap: Interp::tier is clamped
  where it is initialized and in Engine::set_tier, every compile entry
  requires Tier::Jit, and tests that compile directly are compiled out
  or ignored there.
- value::jit_layout_tests (7, both builds) read every exported offset
  through raw memory on live structures and compare with the safe
  accessors.

Step 5, storage emitters (88f3914b):
- jit_storage.rs: named-field length, capacity, data and length store;
  binding-map entries; slot memo load and "no slot" branch. About 40
  ARM64 sites use them, gated by matching *_fits predicates. Default
  layouts get exactly the old instructions; gc-heap's get u32 length
  and capacity words, in-place elements while the capacity word is 0
  (the inline capacity while in place), and LDRH memos compared with
  0xFFFF through a scratch register. x64 keeps its Vec reads inline and
  declines the InlineVec layouts (checked helpers until S6's x64 pass;
  a deviation).
- jit::storage::tests (4, both builds) assemble sequences from the
  emitters and run them against synthetic sequences and live objects
  (1-9 fields, in place and spilled), memos and scopes: the first
  generated code run under gc-heap. Inverting the in-place rule or
  widening a gc-heap load makes them fail.
- Gates (steps 4 and 5, rechecked at 88f3914b): code dumps identical
  to the step-1 baselines with each MOVZ/MOVK run compared as one
  masked address (vue 55, react 9, djot 160; word by word one djot
  chunk differs by the heap-address lane of step 3); outputs identical;
  lib embed 2165/0/3, embed,gc-heap 1863/0/113; clippy clean in both
  builds.

Step 6, gc-heap emission (5489ceb6):
- The ARM64 ownership emitters' Traced arms emit no count instruction.
  Clone, shared drop and word release keep the execution-word owner
  guard: BigInt (its checked path kept), property-only and unknown tags
  still take the checked path, since a lazy prototype owns an Rc in
  both builds, and a word release still reaches H_DROP_PACKED_AT for
  them; heap kinds and scalars fall through. Increments, the non-final
  guard and its halves, commits, decrements, release-shared and
  release-unless-last emit nothing, so no count guard branches. The
  direct-call teardown emits nothing; the finish stub still stores
  Undefined over `this` and moves `ret`. drop_helper_call and the
  PropertyLayout Rc emitters are unchanged. x64 stays refused (its
  Traced arms unreachable until S6).
- Call-site audit: logic beside the emitters that only serves counts
  is emitted only while JitOwnership::counted(): the direct call's
  surplus-argument count (w7) and its save-area word; alias compares
  that keep a count above zero (Math, isNaN and sqrt intrinsics,
  instanceof, set-property and wide set-element old values, the
  linked-scan region; TDZ object pairs keep only their property-only
  rejection); old-value markers and release registers read by later
  decrements; loads made only for a decrement. Store-name overwrites an
  old String, Symbol or Object (BigInt and lazy prototypes still take
  the drop helper). jit_callstat's Rust mirror of the shared-callee
  gate (Gc::strong_count panics under gc-heap) is default-build only.
- Maps and Sets: where a guard means "an ordinary object"
  (Exotic::is_ordinary), gc-heap admits Collection with one unsigned
  range test from None's tag (Array keeps its own compare): property
  loads (receivers, hops, holders, absence levels), stores, creations
  and updates, computed methods, dense element methods, element get,
  set, update and `in`, numeric and loop chains, region own entries and
  borrowed reads, property references. Left alone: the name templates'
  global-object checks (a global object is never a Map or Set; the VM
  checks Exotic::None there), instanceof's constructor operand, the
  regions' typed-array element, Array-only sites. The default build
  emits its old compares; the range test's scratch register is dead at
  every gc-heap site (checked in review).
- Test-only admission: Engine::admit_jit_for_test (gc-heap test builds,
  ARM64) runs one engine at Tier::Jit past the clamp and, because JIT
  frames are not rooted yet, stops it collecting for the rest of its
  life (gc_suppressed for safepoints, a no-GC region deferring explicit
  requests).
- jit::traced_tests (10, gc-heap): programs for every emitter family
  and for Map and Set access run at jit (through the hook) and at
  bytecode with the same pinned results and native code asserted.
  Warm property loads are all native hits; direct calls return through
  the direct sequence on every iteration, recursing to depth 300 (past
  the 256-record pool); Map and Set access is 300 native hits and no
  fallback (50 and 250 with Collection admission removed). The hook
  collects nothing even when a collection is due or requested (2
  collections without its no-GC region). Assembled Traced count and
  teardown emitters are empty, and Traced clone and drop send exactly
  BigInt and the tags above Object to the checked path, over all 65,536
  tags. An uncommitted smoke run sent 427 programs from the existing
  JIT test files through the hook: no disagreement with bytecode.
- For step 7: under gc-heap a finished callee's record returns to the
  pool with its slot words unchanged (stale, owning nothing), and
  surplus arguments and the callee are popped by moving the stack
  pointer, so stale words sit above the operand top. The direct call no
  longer needs a second owner of the callee: during the call the
  caller's operand word below the arguments can be the closure's only
  root, so the caller's operand words must stay traced.
- Gates (rechecked at 5489ceb6): code dumps identical to the step-1
  baselines with each MOVZ/MOVK run compared as one masked address (vue
  55, react 9, djot 160; word by word the djot address lane of step 3);
  outputs identical; lib embed 2165/0/3, embed,gc-heap 1873/0/113;
  clippy clean in both builds.

Step 7, rooting (64fcd6b9, 9ad0012b):
- Records: under gc-heap FramePool keeps a registry of every record
  allocated for its interpreter and not yet freed (JitFrame::alloc
  registers it; release past FRAME_POOL_LIMIT and FramePool's Drop
  unregister it; nothing else creates or frees one). A registered record
  not in pool[..len] is active, since generated code pops with len--,
  pushes back in the finish stub and never allocates. The collector
  traces each active record: its context's values precisely and all
  FRAME_BUF words conservatively as packed words, with no stack pointer
  (stale words above the operand top, or left by a finished callee, can
  only retain). Pooled records are not traced. No per-call cost: a
  collection builds the set of pooled records only when some record is
  active. JitFrame::alloc zero-fills the words under gc-heap.
  InterpLayout::frame_pool probes the pool Vec itself (the same offset in
  the default build). Memory reports count the registry (9ad0012b).
- Rust entries whose context lives on the native stack register for
  their native run through an RAII guard (gc_trace::enter_jit_frame,
  popped on unwind) and are traced with the thread roots: jit::run (its
  slot Vec; its operand buffer up to max_stack), run_moved_oversized (its
  n_slots slot words; its operand buffer up to max_stack) and
  run_borrowed_frame (OSR and continuations: the VM frame's stack over
  its capacity; drive_vm keeps registering the slots, environments and
  references, and jit_continuation_operation asserts the buffer never
  moves). run_moved, run_moved_shared and run_moved_env run on records.
- Contexts: GcTrace for JitCtx destructures every field. this_val, ret,
  error, activation and resume_step are traced precisely; each raw
  identity is kept alive by an owner it names (env_raw by the entry's
  frame, by the callee closure on the caller's operand stack and in its
  FnFrame, by the activation, or by drive_vm's registered frame). New
  traces: NativeActivation (env, cap_env, stack, references,
  disposal_frames, class_states), Abrupt, VmStep, DisposeCompletion.
- Never-written memory: records are zero-filled, and under gc-heap a
  ValueStack's whole capacity is initialized (new capacity zero-filled
  where it is created or grown; push writes in place at Vec::push's
  cost), so buffers lent past their length expose only written words.
- The test hook no longer stops collections: admission_collects
  replaces admission_refuses_every_collection.
- jit::rooting_tests (10, gc-heap): a test native collect(), called from
  JIT code, runs the explicit collection and reports, for each object
  the program tracked, what marked it first (a root, the stack or a
  cell, or nothing), so a stale stack copy cannot pass for the JIT root.
  Values held only in a direct callee's slot, the caller's operand words
  across a direct call, a record's this_val and ret, a native
  activation's environments (a per-iteration loop environment, a catch
  environment), jit::run's buffers (the Script, a function a native calls
  back, Array.prototype.map callbacks), an oversized frame (300 locals),
  borrowed frames (an OSR'd Script loop, a generator slice), recursion to
  depth 300 (301 active records) and a fresh closure called directly are
  marked by a root and read back; afterwards nothing tracked survives a
  collection. The same tests and step 6's run again at
  TRUST_JS_GC_STRESS=1 in a child process: 1,279 collections over 19
  tests, about 10 s.
- Mutation check (temporary switches, not committed): dropping the
  records' trace or word scan, the entries' trace or word ranges, or the
  NativeActivation trace fails the tests holding values there. With a
  root removed the values mostly still survived through the conservative
  stack scan, which is why the tests check what marked them first and
  not survival alone. Not discriminated: this_val (the caller's operand
  word also keeps the receiver), ret, error and resume_step (moved out
  before any safepoint on every path found), and the fresh closure (the
  direct sequence's FnFrame also roots the callee).
- Deviation from the design: jit::run's and run_moved_oversized's
  operand buffers are scanned up to max_stack, not over their capacity
  (pooled VM buffers can be much larger and hold stale words).
- Review: checked every site that creates or frees a record; every
  assignment of a context's raw environment (entries, activations,
  borrowed continuations, the direct call's callee; each has a traced
  owner); that no ValueStack growth path is left unzeroed; and that
  tracing another engine's activations on the same thread is harmless
  (the sink ignores words that address no cell of the collecting heap).
  One gap fixed in 9ad0012b: memory reports did not count the registry.
- For step 7b and 8: generated code's polls still compare only the
  object count, so collections reach JIT code only through helpers and
  natives (all now safe). The whole-buffer scans can keep garbage alive
  while an activation runs (a Script's entry, say); if step 8's memory
  gates show it, publish the stack pointer at collecting helpers and
  scan only the live range. x64 uses no records and no direct-call
  sequence; its calls go through the registered Rust entries.
- Gates (rechecked at 9ad0012b): code dumps identical to the step-1
  baselines with each MOVZ/MOVK run compared as one masked address (vue
  55, react 9, djot 160; word by word the djot address lane of step 3);
  outputs identical; lib embed 2165/0/3, embed,gc-heap 1883/0/113; the
  rooting and step-6 tests alone at TRUST_JS_GC_STRESS=1, 20 passed in
  10 s; clippy clean in both builds; rustfmt clean.

Step 7b, polls (f61f10fb):
- The due word: under gc-heap the core keeps a word equal to
  collection_due() (gc_core::Heap::attach_due_word; the heap is its only
  writer). Heap::alloc adds one compare against a precomputed threshold
  (the byte trigger; under a stress interval the current total;
  usize::MAX once due) and recomputes out of line, so the allocation
  that spends the budget or reaches the interval sets it. A deferred
  request sets it; marking, the end of each sweep and the settings
  recompute it. It sits beside GcState's live-object count
  (value::LiveCount, repr(C), gc-heap only) and is attached when the
  heap is created. gc_check asserts in debug builds that it agrees with
  collection_due().
- Polls (ARM64, gc-heap): JitCtx::live_objects already holds the count's
  address, so entry and loop polls load count and word with one LDP and
  take the H_INTERRUPT slow path while the word is set (12 fast-path
  instructions; 11 in the default build, unchanged). A general region's
  backedge guard (11 to 12) bails to its canonical header the same way.
  A direct call's safepoint is its callee's entry poll: the comments
  that said the direct call checks a gc tick are corrected, and the
  callstat mirror gains the inline-retry gate it lacked and loses two
  gates the sequence never had.
- While collections are suppressed or deferred, and in a ShadowRealm's
  interpreter (its root collects), the word stays set and every poll
  calls the helper. A region then bails on each backedge after a whole
  iteration and re-enters at the header, so the loop progresses.
- The recorded deviation, as emitted code: in a chunk body's baseline
  code each call to an allocating helper ends in an explicit poll once
  the helper has returned with its results in canonical places (3
  instructions while the word is clear, else H_INTERRUPT). It is emitted
  after the call, not run in the Rust helpers, because region checked
  effects call the same helpers and must not poll; region code and
  shared stubs emit none. Polled: exec, make_object, make_array,
  make_regexp, make_closure, object_literal, abstract_operation,
  iteration, add_strings, get_prop, set_prop, get_elem, set_elem,
  instanceof and reference_op; every call site once at its end; new
  after its sequence; string accumulation on its committed exit. Not
  polled: helpers that allocate no cell (cond, return, the handler
  pushes and pops, the drops, strict_eq, loop_backedge), that route a
  completion already made (unwind, complete), that run inside a direct
  call whose site polls (direct_finish), that leave native code
  (slice_op), and the poll itself.
- jit::poll_tests (6, gc-heap) count collections by poll kind. At
  stress 1, loop-only code collects every iteration: at helper ends (two
  per iteration), at the loop header with end polls off, and at its
  header as a region. At stress 1, loop-free recursion through direct
  calls collects at callee entry polls. Without stress, a string loop
  collects 82 times in 12,000 iterations on an 11,520-byte budget, with
  allocation at most 80 bytes past the trigger; the tick divider alone
  would have run none. A request deferred inside a native is honoured
  at the next poll of each kind. Operand temporaries survive end-poll
  collections, marked first by a root. A region loop completes while
  collections are suppressed or deferred.
  gc_core::due_word_tracks_collection_due follows the word through each
  input.
- The stress child (step 6's and 7's tests at TRUST_JS_GC_STRESS=1) now
  runs 3,966 collections in about 30 s (1,296 in 10 s before).
- For step 8:
  - While the word stays set, a deferred attempt costs two 256 KiB
    stack scrubs per poll; no engine path is known to defer while
    JavaScript runs.
  - Allocation in an acyclic region waits for the first poll after it.
  - End polls add 3 instructions per allocating-helper call site and per
    call site; the tripwire measures them.
  - Deep recursion under stress would gain from scanning records only
    up to a published stack pointer.
- Gates (rechecked at f61f10fb):
  - Code dumps identical to the step-1 baselines (vue 55, react 9, djot
    160); outputs identical.
  - Lib suites: embed 2165/0/3, embed,gc-heap 1890/0/113.
  - The step-6, 7 and 7b tests alone at TRUST_JS_GC_STRESS=1: 26 passed
    in 29 s.
  - Clippy clean in both builds; rustfmt clean.

Step 8 runs as three sequential sub-steps:
- 8a: admits the JIT under gc-heap on aarch64 and retires the test
  hook. It returns the 38 modules and 31 ignores, giving each count-only
  test a gc-heap form or keeping it default-only with its reason, and
  fixes what admission breaks, checked on the fixtures and difftest.
- 8b: rewrites the JIT-ABI reference-count tests outside those modules
  as rooting tests.
- 8c: runs per-test GC stress over the suite with the verifier and the
  JIT on, with fixes.

Step 8a, admission (28e718a8, 531d01b1, 6121b0b1):
- Admission: Tier::admitted keeps Jit on the bytecode VM only in an
  x86-64 gc-heap build, whose backend refuses traced ownership until S6.
  An ARM64 gc-heap build runs the JIT, and a target without a native
  backend behaves as in the default build. Jit being the default tier,
  every ARM64 gc-heap engine that does not choose a tier now runs
  generated code, so most of the gc-heap lib suite now runs the JIT. The
  test hook Engine::admit_jit_for_test is retired: the traced, rooting
  and poll tests select Tier::Jit through set_tier. The engine binary's
  and difftest's notes come from admitted(): silent on ARM64 gc-heap,
  unchanged on x86-64 gc-heap.
- The 38 modules (257 tests) compile wherever the JIT runs: ARM64 in
  both builds, x86-64 in the default build. 241 pass unchanged. 14
  asserted counts or release timing and gain gc-heap forms that check
  the same property through collection: survival across the collections
  at entry polls (stress 1) or the return test's collection, then
  collectability; call-cache and inline pins as reserved addresses
  (is_allocated_word) until the last pin goes; destructor witnesses run
  by the collection that finds their owner dead; probes that must not
  touch an owner leave operands and cells intact through a collection
  and its verifier. 2 stay default-only (native_array_local_tests'
  foreign-engine pair tests): a destructor writing another Agent's array
  pins the TDZ pair's count guard, which has no gc-heap meaning (I6).
- The 31 "needs the JIT" ignores: 27 pass unchanged and 4 gain gc-heap
  forms (tests::jit_direct_call_releases_the_last_callee_reference_...,
  stack_trace's shared_inline_sites_keep_positions_live_..., call_guard_
  tests::checked_calls_borrow_last_callee_owner_..., and value::jit_heap_
  tests::jit_live_object_counters_..., now with two Engines). On x86-64
  gc-heap 27 keep an ignore ("needs the JIT; gc-heap keeps x64 on
  bytecode until S6"), 3 need no JIT and run there, 1 is ARM64-only. The
  x86-64 cfgs are reasoned, not compiled (this host has only the AArch64
  backend; S6 validates x64 on zombie).
- Fixes: none were needed. The first admitted run's 29 failures were the
  clamp's assertion (8 traced tests and the stress child), 16 count or
  timing assertions, and 2 crashes in the cross-Agent pair tests now
  default-only.
- For 8b, 8c and step 9:
  - Inline planning depends on collection timing. The planner skips a
    cached callee whose weak pin no longer resolves; the default build
    frees acyclic garbage at once, gc-heap at the next collection. So
    gc-heap compiles vue 56 and react 10 (default 55 and 9: it splices a
    callee the default build skipped as dead), and djot 160 in both with
    5 functions planned differently. The guards never match a dead
    callee; tripwire comparisons should allow for it.
  - A collection inside a running activation can find stale copies of
    dead values in live Rust frames, at every tier (seen in Interp::eval,
    call_inner, call_native_committed). Death assertions run after the
    activation; 8c will meet this.
  - Collectability tests keep more than handles out of the test frame: a
    call-cache entry's copy carries raw callee and environment
    addresses, and run_classic_program's completion value sits in its
    caller's frame.
  - The gc-heap suite's long pole is now jit::int_typed_tests::integer_
    comparisons_and_branches_match_in_every_tier, about 300 s (254 s in
    the default build).
- Gates (rechecked at 6121b0b1):
  - Code dumps identical to the step-1 baselines (vue 55, react 9, djot
    160); outputs identical.
  - Lib suites: embed 2165/0/3; embed,gc-heap 2176/0/82 twice (1890, plus
    255 module tests and 31 former ignores; ignored 113 - 31).

Step 8b's scope (from a read-only census of the 47 reference-count
ignores at f61f10fb): the 20 whose subject is a JIT path. 9 become
rooting counterparts (8 programs), each mutation-checked; 6 carry stale
S2 reasons and are lifted (S2's layout probe counted, step 4 removed
it); 3 get count-free forms; 2 stay default-only. The other 27 test the
runtime, the VM and the collector; they keep their ignores, recorded
open with the census's proposed forms (most drop a count line or check
collectability instead).

Step 8b, JIT-path count tests (e02fa8f0, 4c805bb7):
- The 20 reference-count ignores whose subject is a JIT path, by the
  census's numbering (tests only; no emitted code changed):
  - Rooting counterparts: 8 tests stand in for 9 under gc-heap
    (jit::rooting_tests (10) to (15); callback::tests::long_callback_
    calls_root_operands_and_cache_weakly; bytecode::osr_tests::osr_entry_
    operand_prefix_is_a_root). Each runs the path in generated code (the
    return helper through a context registered with gc_trace::enter_jit_
    frame), forces a collection inside it, checks that the values under
    test are marked first by a root (R), reads them back, and checks that
    nothing it tracked survives once the activation ends. The paths: a
    self-cycle held only by a slot; the computed-read helper's operand
    stack and GetMethodElem's receiver; the named-read helper's committed
    result and its throw path; ctx.ret through jit_return; the committed
    native call from a warmed call cache entry (also through a throwing
    callback and a drained tail call); a callback with six arguments into
    a compiled callee, whose shared cache pins it weakly; an OSR entry's
    operand prefix. The 8 originals become default-only with a comment
    naming their counterpart; the OSR original counts nothing and runs in
    both builds.
  - Lifted unchanged (stale S2 reasons): 7, with the x86-64 gc-heap
    ignore where they reach the backend.
  - Count-free forms: 3 (raw frame words, raw literal factories, the
    packed native ABI; a lazy prototype's word, the one packed word that
    still owns under gc-heap, checks jit_drop_packed_at).
  - Default-only: 2 (the counted arm of the execution-word emitters,
    whose Traced arm jit::traced_tests covers; count churn of moved
    native arguments, whose rooting (15) covers).
- Mutation checks (temporary, not committed), each failing its
  counterparts: without the records' word scan (10) to (13), (15) and
  the callback's argument lose R; without this_val's trace, the
  callback's receiver; without ret's trace, (14); without the borrowed
  frame's operand range, the OSR prefix; without the native call's
  root_slice, (15)'s Rust-side leg.
- Findings:
  - In generated code a native call's argument is also held by its
    moved word in the caller's operand stack, which records keep and
    scan whole, so only a call from Rust shows the boundary's own
    rooting; (15) checks both.
  - With a root removed, a test can read freed or reused cells before it
    asserts; the counterparts assert markers first.
  - Census corrections: on ARM64 gc-heap the callback cache fills; the
    throw path's receiver is a precise root only while the getter uses
    `this` (the counterpart's getters do).
  - gc_scan_spike::spike_scan_cost_is_linear_and_small's 1 s wall-clock
    bound failed once on a loaded machine (1.05 s).
- The other 27 reference-count ignores (runtime, VM and collector
  subjects) remain ignored, open.
- Gates (rechecked at 4c805bb7):
  - Lib suites: embed,gc-heap 2194/0/62 twice (from 2176/0/82: 7
    lifted, 3 count-free and 8 new tests pass; 10 originals leave the
    gc-heap build). embed 2165/0/3 in three of four runs. The first, run
    beside 12 per-test stress processes, failed tests::creation_caches_
    do_not_trust_a_reused_prototype_address (step 2's test) in the
    default build: no object took the dead prototype's address within
    200,000 allocations. It passed 20 runs alone, 48 runs as 12 parallel
    processes and the three full runs after. The default build's objects
    come from the process allocator, which the suite's other test threads
    share, so another test can take the freed address first (inferred;
    nothing in the engine depends on time). Step 8c isolates it.
  - The stress child (27 tests, from 19) alone: 29.4 s.
  - Clippy clean in both builds; rustfmt clean.

Step 8c, per-test GC stress with the JIT on (8db05b95, 9d8c20de):
- An exploratory run at 08122116 (8a's code, beside 8b's agent, so its
  timeouts are inflated): each gc-heap lib test in its own process,
  debug (the heap verifier and freed-cell poisoning at every
  collection). The JIT is the default tier, so a test runs generated
  code where its functions tier up (8 calls, a hot loop, a self-hosted
  built-in's first call) or where it forces compiles. At interval 1:
  2146 pass, 82 ignored, 1 fails, 6 time out; no crash, no verifier
  report. The 6 and S4's 19 slow tests pass at interval 100, except
  jit::int_typed_tests::integer_comparisons_and_branches_match_in_
  every_tier, which passes at 1000 with S4's 4 (alone, it and tests::
  long_expression_chains_run took 859 s and 839 s, close to the
  runner's 900 s limit). No engine bug found.
- The failure: tests::compiled_allocation_guard_accepts_maximum_signed_
  threshold asserted at least 1000 interrupt ticks for Tier::Jit.
  Generated code's interrupt poll counts a tick only on its fast path;
  under stress every poll after an allocation finds a collection due
  and takes the slow path (jit_interrupt), which checks the host
  interrupt on every call and counts nothing. Interrupt latency is not
  at stake. The tick check now holds for the VM tier, which counts
  first, and for the JIT without stress, where it shows that the guard
  treats gc_next = i64::MAX as never due (8db05b95).
- Step 8b's default-suite failure: tests::creation_caches_do_not_trust_
  a_reused_prototype_address (step 2) now reruns itself alone in a
  child process (--exact, --test-threads=1) and requires the child's
  pass, so no other test thread shares its allocator (9d8c20de). With
  the prototype-epoch bump (step 2's fence) removed, it still fails in
  both builds ("0:true", through the child).
- Gates (at 9d8c20de, quiet machine):
  - Per-test GC stress + verifier + poisoning (debug, embed,gc-heap,
    each lib test in its own process, 900 s each, 1800 s at 1000): 2194
    pass, 62 ignored, 0 fail, no crash, no timeout, no verifier report.
    2165 at interval 1 (2978 s at 12 processes), 24 at 100 (S4's 19 and
    the exploratory run's other 5 timeouts), 5 at 1000 (S4's 4 and

  - Clippy clean in both builds; rustfmt clean.

Step 9 plan, from a read-only survey at 08122116 (with sub-surveys of
the emitter sites, `this`, string constants and bindings, spot-checked
against the source):
- Under gc-heap a packed word owns nothing but a lazy prototype's
  Rc<LazyFunctionPrototype>: BigInt, String, Symbol and Object payloads
  are cell addresses. A lazy word is only ever the value of a function's
  "prototype" property (one creation site). Rust materializes it at
  every property-to-execution boundary; generated code keeps it out at
  3 emitter sites and 2 region read guards; execution storage never
  holds one.
- Generated code still routes ownership for nothing under gc-heap: the
  13-instruction owner guard at 30 sites whose word is never lazy, and
  14 BigInt-only and 4 never-lazy inline tag tests. Two traps: in
  emit_store_local the BigInt test before the slot release and the
  clone after it go together; the region materialize clone has no slow
  path and relies on the read guards before it.
- `this` is packed up to each JIT entry and widened there (JitCtx.
  this_val is a Value, reached through this_raw); a direct call decodes
  it (33 instructions). JitCtx is repr(C) and later fields are reached
  by offset_of!, so a packed this_val must be gc-heap only.
- String constants: the template loads the cell from the chunk's
  constant slot and tags it. An immediate is safe under gc-heap (the
  chunk's RootedVec roots the cell, cells don't move, chunks don't cross
  Agents) but must stay out of the process-wide stubs; the default
  build keeps its increment (in-place append needs a count of 1).
- A packed Binding.value would leave the environment cell in its
  128-byte class; the gains are in the binding load and store
  sequences.

Deviations from the plan's step 9, recorded before the work:
- "The execution word is Copy": not literally. PackedValue keeps its
  Cell<u64>, since shared slots are written through &self (mapped-
  argument aliases, SlotAccess, slots the VM lends to generated code)
  and a Copy type cannot contain a Cell. Under gc-heap it gets no Drop
  and a Clone that copies the bits (debug-asserting a non-lazy word),
  pinned by a !needs_drop assertion, so copies and drops are free.
  Literal Copy needs one Cell<Word> slot type, simpler once the counted
  build is gone: an S6 item. Value stays non-Copy under gc-heap; its
  drop glue is already empty (also S6).
- "Lazy prototypes kept out of execution" already holds. Step 9 also
  makes the lazy prototype a gc-heap payload cell (as S4 did for
  UserCallable), so that no word owns an Rc once Drop goes and property
  overwrites release nothing. If the cell measures badly, Property owns
  the Rc instead (recorded if taken).
- "`this` travels packed" covers JIT contexts, entries and direct
  calls; the VM's `this` (run, drive_vm, VmCoro) stays wide.

Step 9 runs as six sequential sub-steps, one agent each, both builds
green after each:
- 9a, gc-heap emission: under Traced the exec clone, drop and release
  emitters emit nothing, and the BigInt-only and never-lazy tag tests
  go. Where the key can be "prototype" (named reads of it, the computed
  method read, LoadRef through a with object, the region own reads), a
  named property-word guard sends a lazy word to the slow path: a
  materialization boundary, not ownership. Generated code only.
- 9b, lazy prototypes as gc-heap payload cells; the old-value release
  paths of property stores become default-only.
- 9c, PackedValue without Drop and with a bit-copy Clone under gc-heap;
  an explicit materializing read where properties reach execution.
- 9d, `this` packed in JitCtx under gc-heap (a JitRepr fact for the
  emitters; the finish stub resets a full Undefined word).
- 9e, string constants as immediates under gc-heap.
- 9f, Binding.value measured and decided; the step's tripwire.
Order: 9b before 9c (Drop goes only once no word owns an Rc); 9d and 9e
after 9a, so their templates assume guard-free words.
Gates for each sub-step: default code dumps identical to the step-1
baselines; lib suites in both builds; clippy and rustfmt; the stress
child with the sub-step's tests at stress 1; fixtures at --tier=jit and
difftest --seed 7 --count 150 in both builds. Measured per sub-step:
gc-heap code-dump words against the step-8 code (no-LTO, ASLR off: vue
56 chunks, 229,659 words; react 10, 33,677; djot 160, 1,257,266; equal
run to run). At the end: fixtures at --tier=jit in both builds (fat
LTO; cycles, instructions, peak RSS) against step 8's code, and the
per-test GC stress run with the JIT on over the whole suite.

Forced tier-up baseline (exploratory, after 8c, with 8c's binaries at
9d8c20de): the suite at TRUST_JS_TIER_THRESHOLD=0, so every eligible
function compiles at its first call, each lib test in its own process.
- Without stress: embed,gc-heap 2191 pass, 3 fail, 62 ignored (308 s
  at 12 processes); embed 2161, 4, 3 (264 s). Each failure is a
  threshold assumption: eval::prepared_reference::own_tests::prepared_
  reference_own_cold_global_updates_take_actual_hint_paths_in_every_
  tier; lazy_snapshot_body_tests::huge_run_once_bootstrap_bodies_are_
  interpreted_until_the_call_threshold; tests::fragmented_array_
  buffer_writes_preserve_host_synchronization (0..4093 written, against
  0..4096); default only, tests::typeof_unresolved_name_does_not_
  allocate_error_objects (1020 allocations against 1019: one compile
  allocation).
- gc-heap debug at stress interval 1 (verifier and poisoning at every
  collection): 2161 pass, 4 fail, 62 ignored, no crash, no timeout,
  no verifier report (2720 s at 12 processes); the slow lists pass, 24
  at 100 and 5 at 1000. The fourth failure, tests::collected_shadow_
  realm_ends_without_resuming_its_coroutines, fails at stress 1, 2 and
  10 with threshold 0 and passes with either setting alone:
  TRUST_JS_GC_LOG shows a stress collection inside the evaluation
  taking the unreachable realm (objects 2047 -> 1019) before the test
  checks for its parked module body. A test assumption: 0c834fe7 holds
  the realm in a global until the check; it passes 5 of 5 under each of
  those settings and without them, in both builds.

Step 9a, gc-heap emission (09b28cd4):
- Under Traced, exec_clone, exec_drop_shared, exec_release_word and
  exec_release_loaded emit nothing, and the 13-instruction owner guard
  is counted-only. Checks that share a slow path stay (Empty, NO_SLOT,
  accessor, a kind decode before an encode). What only the owner guard
  used is counted-only: the checked paths of Dup and of the region's
  Pop, Dup and pop-condition; a local store that keeps nothing (no
  guard is left); the SetProp create-commit reload; the numeric chains'
  slot loads and bail markers; the name load's preferred-Number
  shortcut; the TDZ pair's register scrub.
- property_word_guard (jit_exec_values.rs), 3 instructions: movz
  0xfffa<<48, cmp, b.hs slow; a const assert pins PACK_LAZY_PROTO as
  the first tag above PACK_OBJ. Emitted only where the key can be
  "prototype": the GetProp family when the name is prototype; the
  computed method read always; LoadRef when the Reference's
  ResolveNameRef (the nearest before the use) names prototype, or none
  is found; the region's borrowed own and method reads (one shared
  helper) when the key is prototype. Rechecked: the one creation site
  (make_function_named, slot 2 = "prototype", non-configurable);
  unpack, into_value and Clone materialize; the global object, which
  name loads read, is never a function.
- The 14 BigInt-only tests are counted-only; the Empty, mutability and
  ic_plain checks beside them stay, and semantic BigInt routing
  (equality, ToBoolean, wrapper prototypes) is unchanged. The nullish
  and strict different-tag equality exits no longer route a BigInt:
  null == 1n is false (IsLooselyEqual's last step), as is strict
  equality across tags. In emit_store_local the BigInt test before the
  slot release and the clone after it went together. The survey missed
  one site: store-name's decode of the incoming word before the
  counted release (now counted-only; the later decode guards both
  stores).
- The region materialize clone, which has no slow path, receives only
  tagged homes (frame slots) and borrowed reads, which are guarded
  wherever the key can be prototype (commented).
- LoadRef through a with object is reachable only in generators and
  async functions: functions containing `with` are not compiled, and
  closures created under `with` stay interpreted.
- Unchanged: the property stores' lazy old-value paths (SetProp's miss,
  store-name's H_DROP_PACKED_AT), now the only Traced code that reaches
  that helper, for 9b; the default build's dead branches; the x64
  backend (it never runs gc-heap code; reasoned, not compiled here).
- Tests: jit::traced_tests::traced_ownership_emitters_emit_nothing
  (every Traced emitter assembles to zero words and no branch) and
  property_word_guard_routes_exactly_the_tags_above_object (3
  instructions; all 65,536 tags with a null and a wild payload; the
  word is preserved), replacing the routing test. New
  jit::word_routing_tests (both builds; in the stress child): a
  deferred F.prototype before and after materialization through the
  named read, the computed method read, LoadRef (in a generator) and
  the region's own and method reads, each checking identity,
  constructor and parent and a native-path counter (for example, 10
  checked named reads and no native hit while deferred, then 10 native
  hits); BigInts through the baseline and region sites that used to
  route them, values checked (under gc-heap the property stores and
  borrowed reads now hit natively). With the guard emitting nothing
  (a mutation, not committed), four of the prototype tests abort
  ("malloc(): unaligned tcache chunk detected") and a fifth panics in
  materialize.
- Gates (the agent's, at 09b28cd4 on a loaded machine; lib suites,
  stress child, clippy and rustfmt rechecked at 0c834fe7):
  - Lib suites: embed 2172/0/3 (2165 + 7 new); embed,gc-heap 2201/0/62

Step 9b, deferred prototypes as payload cells (b155ff10):
- Under gc-heap a function's deferred prototype (PACK_LAZY_PROTO) is a
  payload cell in the Agent heap, as S4 made a closure's UserCallable.
  value::LazyProto is GcBox<LazyFunctionPrototype> under gc-heap and
  Rc in the default build, whose types and code are unchanged. The
  cell is 48 bytes, in the 48-byte class with no slack.
- The cell's descriptor, "lazy prototype", traces the function and the
  State edge: the parent, or the materialized prototype. It reads the
  state without a borrow guard and panics if it is mutably borrowed:
  materialize holds its borrow across one allocation, which never
  collects (I9). Its finalizer drops the key layout.
- PackedValue::trace_gc_edges reports the cell. The function is now a
  traced edge, so a stale stack word that keeps a cell keeps its
  function too.
- with_object_edge still lends the State edge, the logical edge that
  the reference-counting collectors walk (Interp::visit_object_edges:
  the rc collectors, the nursery's counting pass and the realm-path
  diagnostics). All of them compile under gc-heap, but none runs
  there: gc_collect dispatches to gc_heap_collect first.
- No packed word owns anything under gc-heap.
  - Under Traced, SetProp's way and the name/Reference store neither
    load nor test the old word (5 and 13 instructions fewer per site).
    An overwritten deferred prototype materializes nothing, and its
    cell dies at the next collection.
  - No Traced code calls H_DROP_PACKED_AT or H_DROP_AT, and
    jit_drop_packed_at panics in gc-heap debug builds. jit_drop_at
    stays callable, since the gc-heap ABI test calls it directly.
  - x86-64 compiles none of these templates (reasoned, not compiled
    here).
- Unchanged: Object.defineProperty and Reflect.set over a deferred
  prototype materialize it through their descriptor snapshot
  (Property::clone), in both builds, as before. Clone and unpack still
  materialize; that is 9c's.
- Memory reports count 48 bytes per lazy word in property storage,
  once; no report counts gc-heap cells by bytes. Replacing one lazy
  word lowers property storage by 48 bytes, as at 78b705e7.
- Tests. The gc-heap ones all run in the stress child at stress 1.
  - Cells live and die with their functions: 256 functions keep 256
    cells across 3 collections with nothing materialized; a cell's
    first marker is its function; all are gone one collection after
    release.
  - The trace reports [function, Object.prototype] while deferred and
    [function, prototype] after materializing.
  - Prototypes of a function of this realm, a function made by a
    $262.createRealm() realm's Function, and a generator of that realm
    each materialize once after collections. Identity, constructor
    and the creation realm's parent hold.
  - Rust overwrites free the old cells (and materialize, as above).
  - Native SetProp and with-object StoreRef overwrites, 10 each:
    no set-property helper call, 10 native Reference stores, no
    materialization, and the old cells are freed.
  - A static check that the Traced SetProp and store templates load no
    drop helper.
  - In both builds, the memory report's 48 bytes.
  - The step 8b ABI test's lazy Rc check is default-only. Its gc-heap
    form overwrites a lazy word, which releases nothing, and frees the
    cell at the next collection, when the finalizer drops the layout.
- Mutations (a separate source copy; 21 tests, each in its own
  process; the control passes 21 of 21):
  - Marking through with_object_edge instead of the cell fails 5 tests
    without stress and 18 at stress 1 (swept, poisoned cells).
  - A trace without the owner, or without the State edge, fails the
    trace test.
  - Traced SetProp retesting a lazy old value fails the SetProp test.
  - The Traced store calling H_DROP_PACKED_AT fails the static test,
    and the StoreRef test aborts on the debug panic.
- Gates: the agent's, at b155ff10. I reran the lib suites, the stress
  child, clippy and rustfmt at the same commit.
  - Lib suites: embed 2173/0/3 (2172 plus the memory test);
    embed,gc-heap 2209/0/62 twice (2201 plus 8 new; the ABI test was
    adapted in place). My rerun gave the same counts, with gc-heap run
    twice.
  - Stress child (44 tests, from 34): 29.8 s; 31.2 s on my
    rerun.
  - Targeted per-test stress, 382 tests (8c's 368, adjusted for 9a,
    plus the new ones):
    - At stress 1, 377 pass and 4 are ignored.
    - At stress 1 with threshold 0, the same, apart from one threshold
      assumption (lazy_snapshot_body_tests::huge_run_once_bootstrap_
      bodies_are_interpreted_until_the_call_threshold).
    - tests::proxy_prototype_cycles_throw_range_error_instead_of_
      overflowing, which is on 8c's slow list, passes at 100 (253 s;
      250 s with threshold 0).
    - No crash and no verifier report.
  - Default code dumps are norm2-identical to step 1, stdout equal.
    gc-heap: chunks 56/10/160, stdout equal to the default build's.
  - Fixtures at --tier=jit: exit 0, stdout equal, compiles 55/9/160
    and 56/10/160, no failure.
  - Functions with a prototype, both builds: vue 1,000 of 26,480 user
    functions, react 2 of 4,502, djot 277 of 2,920.
  - difftest --seed 7 --count 150: 138 agree, 12 skipped, 0 crashes
    in both builds.
  - Clippy clean in both builds; rustfmt clean.
- gc-heap code-dump words, 9a -> 9b: vue 189,452 -> 189,014 (-0.23%;
  18 chunks changed), react 26,017 unchanged (0 changed), djot
  1,059,043 -> 1,056,905 (-0.20%; 89). Against step 8: vue -17.7%,

  - gc-heap at --tier=jit: vue 3,363.1M -> 3,347.0M (-0.5%), react

- Microbenchmarks at --tier=jit, gc-heap 9a -> 9b (the default build is
  unchanged):
  - creating 2M closures, each with a deferred prototype: -3.7%
    (11,685M -> 11,255M);
  - the arrow-function control: -1.5%, with the same 87 collections.
