  The cell measures well, so the plan's alternative (Property owns the
  Rc) is not taken.
- For 9d, layout (B) of the survey: under gc-heap, JitCtx holds the
  packed `this` word itself at [48], in place of this_raw. The pointer
  and its four set-up writes go, saving one dependent load per access
  of `this`. The default build keeps this_raw and the wide this_val.

Step 9c, packed words copy as bits and drop as nothing (e1630212):
- Under gc-heap PackedValue has no Drop, and its Clone copies the
  bits. A const assertion pins that the type needs no drop. The
  default build keeps both impls: its .text is byte-identical to 9b's
  (only panic line numbers in .data.rel.ro differ).
- The explicit read is PackedValue::clone_materialized, in both
  builds. A deferred prototype materializes and the source word is
  promoted in place, as unpack does; any other word is cloned. In the
  default build it is Clone, whose lazy arm does the same.
  Property::clone and Property::clone_value_packed use it; value,
  into_value and take_value already materialized. Property::clone
  keeps materializing: 9a's guards rest on a lazy word living only in
  its function's prototype slot.
- The gc-heap Clone debug-asserts that the word is not a deferred
  prototype.
- The clone sites were found by removing Clone from PackedValue,
  Property and every container of properties, and compiling all
  targets under six feature sets.
  - Those that can see a lazy word read through Property::clone or
    clone_value_packed: [[GetOwnProperty]] snapshots, the OrdinaryGet
    and OrdinarySet chain walks (Reflect.get and Reflect.set),
    ValidateAndApplyPropertyDescriptor, the interpreter's [[Set]]
    chain walks, super() to a native parent (which grafts the made
    function's properties), and snapshot_cached_get (VM and JIT named
    reads).
  - Six direct clones read execution storage: Dup, Dup2 and LoadLocal
    in drive_vm and jit_exec_inner. Execution storage never holds a
    lazy word: words enter it as packed Values, cached-get snapshots
    and moves, or by generated loads, which 9a guards wherever the key
    can be "prototype".
  - The other property clones cannot see one: builtin method aliases,
    the mapped arguments object, internal and private slots, class
    private methods, module namespaces, function templates (whose
    prototype slot is an Undefined placeholder) and object-literal
    templates.
- The teardown loops compile to nothing under gc-heap; the optimizer
  removes them, and nothing is cfg'd. In 9b each drop dispatched on
  the tag and called an out-of-line drop_word that only returned.
  No-LTO objdump, gc-heap 9b -> 9c instructions (packed-drop calls):
  jit_direct_finish 431 -> 242 (20 -> 0), jit::run 581 -> 532
  (20 -> 0), run_moved_inner 574 -> 364 (25 -> 0),
  run_moved_oversized 643 -> 375 (30 -> 0).
- jit_add_strings' retire of the owner that the next StoreLocal or
  StoreCap overwrites is default-only: gc-heap appends at the buffer's
  frontier and needs no uniqueness (242 -> 181 instructions).
  clone_raw is default-only. The VM's own Op::Add retire in drive_vm
  is the same count-only logic and still runs under gc-heap.
- clippy::drop_non_drop joins the crate's gc-heap allowances, beside
  those for Copy handles.
- Tests. The gc-heap ones all run in the stress child at stress 1.
  - Seven tests ignored under gc-heap since S2 have gc-heap forms:
    three in value.rs and four in execution_storage.rs. They check
    exact bits (an object word is the tag over the cell's address),
    bitwise clones, values intact after their copies drop, and that
    drops and replacements release nothing.
  - object_allocations_attach_and_release_central_handles stays
    ignored: it tests heap-bridge's central handle, not packed words,
    and compiles only with heap-bridge.
  - In both builds and at every tier, each of the five reads
    materializes a function's deferred prototype exactly once
    (prototypes_materialized). An object word comes out, the property
    holds it, and constructor and the parent hold; a second read and a
    script's read materialize nothing more. take_value leaves
    undefined.
  - gc-heap debug builds: copying a lazy word with Clone trips the
    assertion.
- Mutations (a separate source copy; each test in its own process, at
  stress 0 and 1; the control passes 22 of 22 with debug assertions
  and 21 of 21 without):
  - Property::clone without the explicit read: with debug assertions
    6 tests fail and 1 aborts, the transfer test among them; without,
    3 fail.
  - clone_value_packed without it: 7 fail and 1 aborts; without debug
    assertions 5 fail.
  - The gc-heap Clone keeping its lazy arm fails the should-panic
    test.
  The aborts are the assertion firing inside extern "C" helpers.

Step 9d, `this` travels packed in JIT contexts under gc-heap (e56fb053):
- Layout (B). Under gc-heap JitCtx holds the packed `this` word at
  [48] (this_word) in place of this_raw. The wide this_val and the
  pointer's set-up writes go, and the Rust-only fields after [72]
  move up 16 bytes; every emitter reaches them through offset_of!.
  The default build keeps this_raw and this_val. The prefix asserts
  in jit.rs and the ABI test gain gc-heap forms: the word is at 48
  and 8 bytes.
- Rust reaches `this` through four accessors beside JitCtx, not the
  brief's one or two. this_value returns a wide copy. this_view lends
  the default build's field without a new owner and without
  borrowing the context; gc-heap widens the word into the view.
  set_this and clear_this write it. `type JitThis` is Value, or
  PackedValue under gc-heap. The nine struct literals still name the
  cfg'd fields.
- The fact is JitRepr::ctx_this, JitThisRepr::Wide { value } or
  Packed. It is derived from the cfg'd layout (JitThisRepr::BUILD),
  not probed; the prefix asserts pin [48].
- Emitted sites under gc-heap, instructions per site 9c -> 9d (sites
  in vue/react/djot):
  - LoadThis, baseline and region copy: 62 (56 without the end poll)
    -> 3 (27/6/244).
  - GetPropThis through the wrapper probe: 20 -> 41 (8/0/226). The
    packed probe the brief prescribes decodes the word's kind. An
    object receiver executes 6 instructions instead of 5, with one
    dependent load fewer.
  - The other receivers (GetPropThis without the probe, SetPropThis,
    the region own read, numeric chains): 5 -> 6 (5/0/136). A reused
    region read stays at 2.
  - The direct call's move of the receiver: 37 -> 2, or 3 past 30
    arguments (40/10/281).
  - The finish stub's reset: strb wzr -> str x5 (9/4/41). x5 holds
    the complete Undefined word from the ret store just before; a
    zero word would be +0.0.
  The site lengths sum to vue -2,784, react -686 and djot -19,157
  words. The rest of each dump delta (-1, 0, -52) is exactly
  worddiff's MOVK change.
- Rust paths.
  - The moved entries (run_moved, _shared, _env, _inner, _oversized,
    and the non-JIT stubs) take JitThis. jit::run and
    run_borrowed_frame pack at entry.
  - call_jit_env_committed_packed, call_jit_committed_packed and
    call_jit_fast bind the word with a gc-heap-only
    Interp::bind_this_word: OrdinaryCallBindThis on the tag, where
    boxing a primitive is the one widening. It matches
    bind_compiled_this(.., false), with uses_this = needs_frame_this
    and strict = is_strict.
  - construct_jit_fast packs the new object. The arguments-apply
    forwarder's round trip goes with call_jit_fast's widening.
  - The property helpers, jit_exec_inner and both VM bridges widen
    locally through the accessors.
  - jit_make_run_env_packed widens only when env_this &&
    !lexical_this, the one case in which either environment path
    (make_run_env_from, ActivationPlan::instantiate) stores `this`.
  - JitFrame::release's reset is one store, and GcTrace for JitCtx
    traces the word.
  - The VM's `this` stays wide: each bridged operation widens once,
    and run_borrowed_frame packs once per native slice.
- x86-64's `this` property forms decline unless the fact is Wide, so
  under gc-heap they take the checked helper. x86-64 never runs
  gc-heap code (Tier::admitted); S6's x64 pass needs a packed form.
- The default build's generated code is unchanged: code dumps are
  norm2-identical to step 1, and chunk summaries identical to 9c's.
  Unlike 9c, its Rust .text is not byte-identical:
  - the emitters read the fact;
  - four helper sites read this_val through the accessors instead of
    through this_raw. These are the same reads: at 771b6dcf every
    this_raw write points it at its own context's this_val, and no
    context moves afterwards.
  - the no-LTO, 16-unit build re-partitions inlining in unrelated code
    (intl, typed arrays).
  Default instructions are within 0.05%; 9f's fat-LTO tripwire
  covers it.
- Tests.
  - jit::this_tests (7 tests, both builds, also in the stress child)
    drives every kind of `this` through every site: an object; a
    primitive into a strict and into a sloppy callee; undefined and
    null into both. The sites are LoadThis, GetPropThis and
    SetPropThis (with length), numeric chains, region own reads,
    direct calls and constructors. An arrow's and a derived
    constructor's environment `this` are covered too.
  - Each runs at all three tiers against Node's results. At
    --tier=jit, counters check the native paths:
    - direct returns per round, exactly 30, 41 and 26;
    - property paths;
    - no set-prop helper call for an object `this`, 20 for a
      primitive;
    - borrowed reads;
    - the test-only TEST_THIS_CHAIN_OPS.
  - A call of a free name compiles to LoadNameForCall + CallWithThis,
    which passes Undefined itself. So the receiver-less direct-call
    check calls through a local binding, then checks that every
    pooled record holds Undefined.
  - Rooting (gc-heap): a synthetic context's `this` word is marked by
    a root and is collectable after clear_this. An object reachable
    only as a jit::run entry's `this` is marked R and survives
    stress 1, for a sloppy and a strict callee.
  - Updated: the context literals, the ABI test, jit_return_tests'
    pooled-record read, and the traced emitters test. The last pins
    the packed reset to exactly str x5, [x10, #48].
- Mutations (a separate source copy; 12 tests, each in its own

  - A finish stub resetting a zero word fails 3 tests: the

  - A trace that skips the word fails 2: the objects are marked S,
    not R.

    any one alone, makes the relevant test die with SIGSEGV.

Step 9e, string constants are immediates under gc-heap (5fa62d13):
- Under traced ownership both string-constant templates push the
  constant's word, PACK_STR | cell, as an immediate: mov_imm64 (3
  words in its MOVN form, else 4), the store and the add, with no
  load. The templates are the baseline's (jit_operations.rs) and the
  region copy (jit_regions.rs emit_effect). At 9d each was 8 words,
  with a dependent load of the cell from the constant's pool slot.
  The counted template is unchanged byte for byte (11 words in the
  default build, with its count increment).
- The region copy got the same change rather than being retired.
  Retiring it would change the default build's code under
  TRUST_JS_SHARED_NATIVE_OPERATIONS=0, or send gc-heap's constants to
  H_EXEC there. Neither new arm checks rc_ok: like a scalar
  constant's, the word depends on no probed layout.
- Chunk::jit_const_str_word (gc-heap only) gives the word.
  jit_const_copyable and PackedValue::scalar_bits are unchanged.
- Soundness, re-verified at HEAD:
  - The pool (consts) is a RootedVec, built once and only read
    afterwards. It roots its cells until the chunk drops. Chunk has no
    custom Drop, so no safepoint runs between the pool's drop and its
    code's.
  - The chunk owns its code (Chunk::jit and its OSR sidecar). Each
    compile commits into its own chunk's slot, and Chunk is not Clone.
  - Cells never move: the heap is a non-moving mark-sweep.
  - No code is shared between chunks or Agents. The fragment cache is
    per Interp, and a Plan owns its Rc<Chunk>. Static snapshots decode
    per Agent, with empty code cells. An inlined callee compiles into
    the caller's own pool. No thread-local holds a chunk or an AST.
  - GLOBAL_STUBS is process-wide, and SharedStub::emit takes no chunk.
  - Word 0 of a gc-heap string cell is the frontier, not a count. A
    constant is allocated at exact capacity (LStr::from), so any
    non-empty append copies.
  - So the immediate is as valid as the pool's own Value, which the
    VM's Op::Const pushes.
- The VM's Op::Add retire (run_vm_inner) and its two helpers are
  default-only, as 9c made jit_add_strings'. gc-heap appends at the
  buffer's frontier and needs no uniqueness, so under gc-heap nothing
  retires the old string of `s += x` any more. 2M `s += 'x'` into a
  local at --tier=bytecode: gc-heap 3,160.7M -> 2,802.8M instructions
  (-11.3%); the default build is unchanged.
- Code, gc-heap dumps against 9d's:
  - String-constant sites 60/18/924 in vue/react/djot, 8 words each
    -> 5 or 6: vue 480 -> 337 words, react 144 -> 92, djot 7,392 ->
    5,432.
  - Dump words: vue 186,213 -> 186,070, react 25,330 -> 25,278, djot
    1,037,713 -> 1,035,753. In every chunk the word change equals its
    sites' change. Chunk summaries changed in exactly the 12/2/105
    chunks with sites.
  - The default build's sites (60/18/929, 11 words) are as at 9d. Its
    929 djot sites against gc-heap's 924 predate 9e: four chunks
    compile to different code in the two builds (e.g. 10,466 against
    3,301 words).
- Tests.
  - jit::string_constant_tests (3 tests, both builds, every tier, also
    in the stress child):
    - A literal evaluates to its chunk's constant, the same string at
      each evaluation, in a function and in a loop region. It is ===
      and == to a string built at run time, across collections. Under
      gc-heap the immediate equals the word the VM pushes.
    - Appending to a local or a captured binding that starts as a
      literal copies out of it. The literal reads the same on the next
      evaluation, the grown buffer is not the literal's, and the
      constant has no spare capacity.
    - An inlined callee's literal is the caller's own constant, a
      separate cell. After the callee is collected (its function
      object and AST dropped), that constant is intact and caller()
      returns the replacement's string.
  - jit::traced_tests (3 tests, gc-heap):
    - In real generated code each string literal's sites are exactly
      the immediate, the store and the add: 1 site before a loop, 2
      inside it (baseline and region). No site materializes the pool
      slot's address.
    - The same check in a child process, with the regions' own
      vocabulary.
    - All 2,690 stub variants a site can request are assembled. None
      materializes a string word, or the word, cell or pool slot of any
      of a chunk's string constants. The test lists the shared stub
      kinds, so a new kind must be added to it.
  - Deviation from the brief, which asked that the caller's spliced
    code still push its string after the callee became unreachable.
    That cannot happen: the inline guard compares the callee's
    identity, and a dead cell named by a weak pin stays reserved, so
    the spliced body never runs again. The test checks the constant
    instead.
- Mutations (a separate source copy; 6 tests, each in its own process,
  at stress 0 and 1; the control passes 6 of 6 both ways):
  - The Symbol tag instead of the string tag fails all 6.
  - The pool slot's address instead of the cell fails all 6 (some
    abort).
  - A wrong tag in the region copy alone fails the region-copy child
    test.
  - An unrooted copy of the string, standing in for a constant's cell
    freed while code embeds it, fails 5 at stress 0 and all 6 at
    stress 1.
  - With the direct comparison of the immediate against the VM's word
    disabled, the 3 both-builds tests still catch the tag, slot and
    unrooted mutations.

Step 9f, Binding.value measured and not packed in S5:
- The question was whether packing Binding.value under gc-heap pays.
  The value is a 16-byte Value today; packed, it would be one word.
  The bar, set before measuring: an estimated saving of at least 2% of
  instructions at --tier=jit or --tier=bytecode on some fixture, or
  at least 3% of peak heap bytes. An agent measured at 52aa7a74, with
  an instrumented source copy that nothing committed. I checked its
  numbers.
- 9a's smaller store wins are in. emit_store_name_value puts the
  incoming word's kind decode, the old-BigInt check and the release
  under `if own.counted()`. In a gc-heap dump of a vue
  StoreNameCached site, the generic arm after the Number-over-Number
  test is a reload, one decode and two stores. The default build's
  same site has the extra decode, the BigInt check and the release.
- --tier=jit, gc-heap, the three fixtures:
  - Executions are measured. Counters on every binding load, store,
    update and loop-chain path are bumped from generated code,
    including the shared NameLoad stub. The load counts match the
    bytecode VM's binding reads: vue 3.22M against 3.14M, react 458K
    against 458K.
  - Today's lengths are counted in the uninstrumented dumps. A
    binding load is 10 instructions for a Number, 12 for an Object
    and 19-31 for the other kinds; a global property's load is 5. A
    binding store is 13 (Number over Number) to 40.
  - The packed lengths are inferred from the existing primitives,
    and optimistic: 3 for any load, 7 for a binding store.
  - Saving: vue 31.6M of 3,223.7M instructions (0.98%), react 4.1M of
    606.8M (0.68%), djot 4.5M of 1,261.2M (0.36%). Most of it is the
    Object load: vue runs 1.86M of them, at 9 instructions each.
  - The Rust helper paths at this tier would add 0.02-0.12%.
- --tier=bytecode, inferred from measured counts:
  - The VM's binding reads onto its stack (vue 3.14M, react 458K,
    djot 495K) and writes from it (102K, 9K, 14K) are measured.
  - The per-access conversion cost comes from a microbenchmark. A
    read costs 19-35 instructions more than a word copy, and a write
    10-15 more than a word store. The range allows for the
    benchmark's out-of-line pack.
  - Saving: vue 0.79-1.10%, react 0.40-0.54%, djot 0.45-0.60%.
- Code size, gc-heap dumps:
  - Inline binding templates would shrink by about 66 words per load,
    47 per store, 4 per update and 11 per loop-chain read.
  - Most LoadName sites call the process-wide NameLoad stub and would
    not change.
  - Totals: vue 186,070 -> 177,035 words (-4.9%), react 25,278 ->
    25,146 (-0.5%), djot 1,035,753 -> 1,008,763 (-2.6%).
- Memory, gc-heap (the S3 census at every collection):
  - Binding is 32 bytes and would be 24. The environment cell would
    go from 128 to 120 bytes and stay in the 128-byte class.
  - Spilled binding buffers beside the cells hold at most 34,896 B
    (vue at --tier=interp). Packed, they would save 5,816 B, 1.39% of
    the heap's live bytes at that collection. The other fixtures and
    tiers: 0.04-0.92%.
  - At --tier=interp, records that die spill 148 MB (vue), 45 MB
    (react) and 54 MB (djot) over a run. Packing would cut those
    bytes by a sixth, but not the allocations.
- Change size at 52aa7a74, lib code outside tests. It was counted by
  renaming the field and the accessors and compiling both builds:
  - 66 reads and writes of Binding.value (65 and a pattern under
    gc-heap);
  - 56/57 constructions;
  - 121/123 VarMap accessor calls.
  Six JIT templates would need gc-heap-only forms behind a layout
  fact, and x86-64 would decline them. The tree-walker (eval.rs, 25
  uses) would pay a conversion per access instead; that cost was not
  measured.
- Decision: not taken. No fixture reaches the bar at either tier: the
  best is vue, at about 1%. Memory moves by at most 1.4% of live heap
  bytes. Revisit in S6, where one word-slot type replaces the cfg
  split once the counted build is gone. The code-size saving (-0.5 to
  -4.9% of gc-heap code words) comes with it.
- No commits. The code-neutral store-name mark would not have changed
  any number: a store is emitted at its own op, so map offsets place
  every store site.

S5 review fixes (outside review of 37a3e12f, 2026-10-18)
--------------------------------------------------------
- [P1] A proper tail call's argument list was unrooted in most of the
  trampolines that run it. Past the ordinary-call depth (Op::TailDeep)
  a tail call leaves pending_tail, which the collector traces, holding
  the callee, the receiver and a Vec<Value> of arguments. A trampoline
  takes it out, so the list sits in a Rust-heap buffer the stack scan
  cannot see. Interp::call rooted the list (root_slice) from the take
  through its GC check and the call. The six JIT trampolines did not:
  jit_direct_finish, call_jit_committed_packed, call_jit_env_committed_
  packed, call_native_committed, call_jit_fast and construct_jit_fast.
  Their GC check could collect the arguments, and so could a Proxy
  callee's `apply` getter, which runs before the trap's argument array
  exists.
  - Fix: every trampoline takes the call through one step,
    Interp::run_pending_tail. It roots the list from the take through
    the check (amortized or not, as each path checked before) and the
    call. Interp::call uses it too.
  - Reach in the default configuration: direct calls return to the
    committed path from depth 128 (MAX_DIRECT_CALL_DEPTH), and tail
    calls are staged only from depth 1024, so jit_direct_finish drains
    one only with TRUST_JS_TAIL_CALL_DEPTH below 128. The committed,
    env and fast paths drain them at any depth past 1024.
    construct_jit_fast never sees one: both construct paths refuse a
    body with tail calls. Only a test native leaves a pending call with
    arguments for call_native_committed.
  - A JavaScript getter can mask the gap in the JIT paths. The callee's
    frame record returns to the pool, the getter's activation pops it
    again, and an active record's words are scanned whole. Its stale
    operand words then hold the arguments.
  - Test jit::rooting_tests::tail_call_arguments_are_roots_in_the_
    trampoline (gc-heap, ARM64). At depth 1024, with direct calls let
    run that deep, strict compiled functions tail-call a Proxy with
    two fresh objects. A native `apply` getter collects, so no JIT
    record is active, and reports that roots marked both objects; the
    trap then reads them back.
    - The calls drain through Interp::call (a Rust entry and the JIT's
      generic call), call_jit_fast, call_jit_committed_packed, its env
      variant, and jit_direct_finish once the callees are settled.
    - Mutation runs, one at a time: with the root removed from the
      step, or any one of those five sites taking an unrooted copy of
      the step, the test dies with SIGSEGV when the trap reads a freed
      object.
    - Unrooting call_native_committed or construct_jit_fast changes
      nothing here (see reach above).
  - The tree-walker's try/finally also takes pending_tail, keeping it
    in a Box across its finalizer. HasCallInTailPosition leaves nothing
    to keep there: a try block runs with tco_ok false, and a catch
    block keeps it only when there is no finalizer.
- [P2] difftest: classify_crash convicted a compiled tier whenever the
  interp oracle's rerun "completed", and running out of budget (the
  wall clock, or the allocation cap's abort) counted as completing. A
  program the interpreter could not finish was therefore reported as a
  crash of the first compiled tier that died on it.
  - Fix: a rerun now ends Completed, Exhausted or Crashed (Run). Only a
    Completed oracle can convict a compiled tier, and only one that
    Crashed. An exhausted or crashed oracle leaves the program
    inconclusive, as does a compiled tier that only runs out of budget.
  - The decision is a pure function (crash_verdict) beside run_end, so
    tests cover it without children: an exhausted or crashed oracle
    gives Inconclusive whatever the compiled tiers did. The old
    decision fails the first test (it convicts bytecode under an
    exhausted oracle).
- Gates (the working tree on 37a3e12f; no-LTO release builds unless
  noted):
  - Lib suites (debug): embed 2184/0/3, embed,gc-heap 2234/0/55 (the
    new test). Clippy clean for trust-js and trust-js-difftest in both
    builds; rustfmt clean on the changed files.
  - GC stress 1 (debug, embed,gc-heap, one process): the tail, rooting
    and loop-fragment tests, 36 pass. 8c's four slow tail tests run at
    100: 4 pass. In the lib suite, rooting_holds_under_gc_stress
    reruns the rooting tests at stress 1, the new one included.
  - difftest: its own tests 3/0/0 in both builds. --seed 7 --count
    150: 138 agree, 12 skipped, no crash in both builds, as at the
    exit gate.
  - Full Test262 at --tier=jit, both builds: 53,575/53,575 at the
    default threshold, 53,573/53,575 at threshold 0. All four reports
    are byte-identical to the S5 exit gate's.

S6 flip, deletion and acceptance (started 2026-10-18)
-----------------------------------------------------
Stage boundary: main has no commits past the merge base e3310993.

Plan, recorded before the work. Two read-only surveys (the counted
model's deletion inventory; the embed API, x86-64 and GC policy)
found:
- Gates: 772 gc-heap mentions in 77 files, all in crates/trust-js/src
  (about 442 production, 330 test), and 10 heap-bridge mentions, all
  in value.rs. Downstream manifests only forward gc-heap (host,
  difftest, fs, timers, runtime, web, node).
- The counted model, to delete:
  - the cycle collector and its edge helpers (interpreter.rs, about
    1,200 lines), the counted nursery (gc_generational.rs), and the
    counted parts of gc_edges.rs and gc_diagnostics.rs;
  - six DirectGcEdges traces; the counted Gc, Env and LStr handles;
    the Rc payload aliases; PackedValue's counted Clone and Drop;
    the scope registry; the map_data table; the VM string-add
    retire; 8 production count observers;
  - the JIT's Refcount ownership: jit_ownership.rs's counted arms and
    52 counted() blocks; on x64, 7 rc_* sites;
  - heap.rs (heap-bridge);
  - 21 default-only tests, and 48 gc-heap-ignored tests of the
    counted model.
- Open beyond deletion:
  - Only Linux and macOS have a conservative stack scanner
    (gc_core.rs), so a gc-heap build never collects on Windows or
    wasm32.
  - x86-64 under gc-heap has never been compiled or run: neither the
    scanner's spill and scrub assembly nor the x64 JIT, which is
    refused under gc-heap.
  - The embed API hands out raw cell handles, with no rooted handle
    type. A Value kept in Rust-heap storage, or past its Engine,
    dangles. Cross-Engine use is unchecked, and Gc derefs to
    RefCell<Object>.
  - TRust (/big/Code/TRust) builds against main's embed API.
- Criteria unmet at S5's exit: peak RSS +9.5..+146% against +25%; GC
  share 29-38% on sustained allocation against 5% (main spends 25-28%
  there); Vue instructions 2,974.2M against 2,970M.

Owner decisions (2026-10-18):
- The plan's non-goal "generational collection" is lifted. With
  full-heap collection, lowering the budget floor for RSS means more
  full marks of the whole live heap, and raising it costs RSS. A
  nursery removes that trade. S0 measured high infant mortality: on
  Vue, 85k of 151k deaths within 16 allocations of birth.
- Order: flip and delete first, so a write barrier goes into one model
  only. Then two cheap levers, measured: the byte budget replaces the
  inherited 100k-object trigger, and marking finds a cell's page
  directly instead of hashing every edge. Then the nursery, as its own
  step.
- 64-bit targets only. wasm32 leaves P1's acceptance (its criterion
  retires), and trust-js-wasm's wasm32 build stops being a P1 target.
- zombie (ruby@zombie, x86-64 Linux) validates x86-64 natively. It
  also validates Windows: x86_64-pc-windows-msvc, cross-built there
  with cargo-xwin and run under Wine.

Steps (each leaves the workspace building, with its gates green):
- Step 0, baselines: gc-heap code dumps, Test262 reports, lib and
  downstream counts and fixture outputs, so that deletions can be
  shown to change nothing. Also a trust-js tree and toolchain on
  zombie.
- Step 1, portability before the flip:
  - x86-64 Linux gc-heap on zombie at interp and bytecode: lib suites
    in both builds, per-test GC stress with the verifier, the
    scanner's adversarial tests, and Test262.
  - A Windows scanner: stack bounds from GetCurrentThreadStackLimits,
    a Win64 register spill (xmm6-xmm15 included), and stacker's fiber
    stacks. Cross-built and run under Wine on zombie.
- Step 2, flip: gc-heap is the default model.
- Step 3, the counted model's tests and heap-bridge: delete them,
  porting any test whose property still holds.
- Step 4, the counted collectors and their knobs.
- Step 5, the counted handles, payloads and count observers.
- Step 6, the JIT's Refcount ownership on ARM64. gc-heap code dumps
  must stay identical to step 0's.
- Step 7, the x64 JIT under gc-heap on zombie: Traced arms, InlineVec
  fields, the packed `this`, string immediates and due-word polls.
  The clamp is lifted and the x64-ignored tests return.
- Step 8, the gc-heap and heap-bridge features removed from every
  manifest, and their cfgs unwrapped.
- Step 9, the two cheap collector levers, measured against the RSS
  and GC-time bounds.
- Step 10, the nursery:
  - non-moving, with sticky mark bits: young means not yet marked by
    a full collection;
  - a write barrier, chiefly at borrow_mut (a per-object dirty mark),
    plus environment raw writes and VM and JIT stores;
  - the verifier checks, at every minor collection, that no old->young
    edge goes unrecorded;
  - weak refs, WeakMap ephemerons, FinalizationRegistry and owner
    eviction, all correct in minor collections.
- Step 11, the embed API: an opaque strong handle registered as a
  root in its Agent's heap. It carries the heap id, rejects other
  Engines, and is inert rather than dangling once its Engine drops.
  Value stops being a storable raw handle, and the runtime's and
  N-API's workarounds migrate. TRust's port is decided with the
  owner.
- Step 12, criterion closure: the execution word literally Copy;
  Binding.value and the VM's `this` packed (9f's revisit); no
  PropertyLayout count traffic in generated code; Object's
  Rc<GcState> and the slot registry replaced. How far the Value enum
  recedes in Rust and the embed API is decided with the owner, and
  recorded.
- Exit gate: all Success Criteria (wasm32 retired), on aarch64 and on
  zombie. Merge review with the owner.

Step 0, baselines (0c66177e, no-LTO release binaries in fresh target
dirs):
- gc-heap code dumps of the fixtures at --tier=jit (ASLR off, every
  chunk normalized by codedump_norm2): vue 56 chunks (186,071 words),
  react 10 (25,278), djot 160 (1,048,035), identical run to run. Kept
  in target/s6-base for the deletion steps.
- Fixture outputs and native compiles as at S5's exit (55/9/160 in the
  default build, 56/10/160 under gc-heap). Test262: the S5 review
  fixes' jit reports and main's interp and bytecode reports. Lib
  suites: default 2184/0/3, gc-heap 2234/0/55.
- zombie: each revision is exported to ~/trust-js-p1/<rev> (git archive,
  sources touched, so a shared target dir cannot hand back an older
  build). ~/trust-js-p1/dev mirrors the working tree's tracked files for
  iterative work. rustc 1.99.1; Test262 d86b2294 copied.

Step 1, x86-64 Linux and Windows on zombie:
- x86-64 Linux, the gc-heap build's first compile there: both builds
  compile. Lib suites: default 2014/0/3, gc-heap 1862/18/84.
  - 12 failures are x64 JIT tests that need native code, which gc-heap
    refuses on x86-64 until step 7 (one also asks Gc::strong_count).
    They carry the existing x86-64 ignore and return with step 7.
  - 6 failures are tests that expect an object dead and find it held by
    a stale stack word ("outside the traced frames").
    - 5: an explicit collection zeroed the stack only from inside its
      own frames (gc_collect, gc_heap_collect_explicit). Those frames
      are built over the region a finished callee has just used, and
      keep their unwritten slots as they were. On x86-64 such slots
      held dead cells' addresses; aarch64's frame layout happened not
      to. The explicit entries are now inlined and zero the stack from
      their caller's frame: Interp::gc_collect, gc_heap_collect_
      explicit, collect_garbage_for_host, gc_collect_young (explicit
      requests), Engine::collect_garbage_at_idle and Engine::collect_
      pending_task_garbage.
    - 1: first_window_context_does_not_retain_realm_bootstrap_state read
      the host-settings table in its own frame, and a slot of that
      frame, which the scan covers legitimately, kept a word equal to
      the realm handle's address. The reads moved into in_frame, as the
      gc_test module's rule asks.
  - The change surfaced one more test of the same kind (native_
    captures_collect_cycles_and_count_repeated_handles_in_both_
    collectors, through gc_collect_young); its collect helper is
    inlined too.
  - After: default 2014/0/3, gc-heap 1868/0/96, in two runs.
  - Clippy with -Dwarnings had never run on x86-64 and failed in both
    builds on items only the ARM64 JIT or aarch64-only tests use. They
    carry the existing cfg_attr(not(target_arch = "aarch64"),
    allow(dead_code)), the JIT-support impl Chunk block as a whole.
    Clean on both architectures in both builds.
- Windows (x86_64-pc-windows-msvc, cross-built on zombie with
  cargo-xwin, run under Wine 11.19):
  - The scanner covers Windows. GetCurrentThreadStackLimits gives the
    thread's stack top, and inside a stacker segment (a fiber on
    Windows) the segment's exact bounds. The Win64 callee-saved
    registers are spilled, xmm6-xmm15 included (28 words).
  - zero_below_sp and the x86-64 test helpers are declared sysv64:
    their assembly reads the first argument from rdi, where Win64 would
    pass it in rcx.
  - New tests: reference_only_in_xmm6_is_found (a reference held only
    in xmm6 across a collection; it fails with xmm6 left out of the
    spill) and registered_segment_bounds_match_the_allocation (a fiber
    segment's registered bounds equal its VirtualQuery allocation, at
    64 KiB, 100,000 B and 1 MiB).
  - Lib suites under Wine: gc-heap 1862 pass, 3 fail, 96 ignored, with
    every scanner test passing. The 3 (snapshot codec tests that expect
    stack growth on a small native stack) fail the same way in the
    default build. The default build also fails creation_caches_do_
    not_trust_a_reused_prototype_address and aborts in execution_
    stack_resource_exhaustion_unwinds_and_recovers (a panic past a JIT
    frame that cannot unwind). Wine's stacks and the default build's
    x64 JIT, not the collector; recorded open.
- aarch64, this tree: lib suites default 2184/0/3, gc-heap 2234/0/55;
  clippy clean in both builds.
- Running when committed, recorded with their results: per-test GC
  stress with the verifier on x86-64, full Test262 on x86-64 at every
  tier, and the scanner tests in release, fat LTO and an opt-level
  matrix on x86-64 and Windows.

Step 2, the flip: gc-heap is in trust-js's default features. Every
workspace crate takes the defaults, so all of them build the GC heap.
The counted model builds only without default features
(--no-default-features --features intl,embed) until steps 3-6 delete
it; the flag goes in step 8. The small build's instructions keep the
GC heap (--features gc-heap), and DIAGNOSTICS.md follows.
- Gates (aarch64):
  - lib suites: default (+embed) 2234/0/55, gc-heap's counts; counted
    2184/0/3. Clippy clean for both.
  - The workspace builds with all targets, without a diagnostic.
  - Downstream suites with default features: host 36/0/0, timers
    5/0/0, fs (no tests), web 61/0/1, node 42/0/0, runtime 182/0/1, as
    at S5's exit under gc-heap.
  - Fixtures at --tier=jit from a default release binary: compiles
    56/10/160; outputs as at S0 (masked md5 65b1faf9, 05a89273,
    9cad1fec).
  - difftest: 138 agree, 12 skipped, no crash.
- trust-js-wasm takes the defaults too. Its wasm32 build is not a P1
  target (64-bit only) and was not built.

Step 3, the counted model's tests and heap-bridge (an implementation
agent's change, reviewed and extended):
- Removed: the 47 tests the GC heap ignored as the counted model's
  ("P1 S2:"), the 21 tests compiled only without gc-heap, the counted
  assertion blocks inside tests with both forms, and heap-bridge: the
  feature, heap.rs (with its 18 unit tests) and its 10 cfgs in
  value.rs. Outside tests, only heap-bridge's cfg'd fields and paths
  and cfg(test) helpers left unused are gone.
- Ported to the GC heap, where the property still holds once a
  collection has run (each port failed under a mutation of what it
  checks, then passed restored): 17 of the 47. Among them: unwind
  restoration of borrowed bindings; the call-overflow cache's epoch
  and Realm guards; a host edge from a suspended generator; preview
  bounds; the computed-key stub cache; Map value release; prepared-
  reference slot tracing; getter-cache growth under re-entrance;
  array storage boundaries; four weak-metadata tests (an already-dead
  subscription, end_job on an empty queue, kept-target retirement,
  Symbol churn shrinking once collected).
- Review additions:
  - Two properties of deleted tests that still hold, restored as
    tests in both builds: equal string keys allocated separately share
    one Map entry with one hash (fails with strings compared by
    identity, and with a second hash); a host GC registration outlives
    its slot being taken (fails when take drops the registration, and
    when the sweep visits an absent slot).
  - weak_metadata_safepoint_honors_suppression_and_validates_cleanup_
    owners lost its counted suppression block and is renamed
    weak_metadata_safepoint_validates_cleanup_owners. Under the GC
    heap deaths are found only by collections, and jit_poll_tests
    checks that none runs while collections are suppressed.
  - gc_core's header no longer calls the feature off by default or
    points at heap.rs.
- Deleted, with the property gone or checked elsewhere:
  - contracts S2 removed: edges between Agent heaps (for-in cache,
    property shapes, native captures), a host omitting its handles, a
    callable lease rooting captures, a typed array's teardown beside
    an external wrapper;
  - the counted cycle collector, its edge cache and its nursery
    (gc_edges, gc_generational, the mixed native nursery, registry
    slot reuse, task-pressure and transient-task collection);
  - reference counts and release timing (array binding, native graph
    cycles, cold import cells, ordinary data reads, dense shift);
  - eager death notification (empty safepoints, acyclic ephemerons,
    separate observer heaps, Symbol destruction during a borrow);
  - migrated_private_homes_release_overwritten_objects_before_
    returning: a port found the object, at both tiers and every run,
    only through a stale word in a still-live frame about 960 B below
    the test's. "Released before returning" is not a GC-heap
    guarantee; rooting across the loop is checked elsewhere.
  - Of the 21 default-only tests, 7 keep their GC-heap form; 10 go
    to the counterparts their comments name (jit::rooting_tests,
    jit::traced_tests, a callback test); 4 checked the cycle collector
    or count guards.
- Counted forms the counted build still compiles stay until steps 5-6
  (JitCtx's this_raw and this_val in four JIT test helpers, map_data
  in gc_edges' setup and the memory inventory, a stack_trace setup).
- Counts: GC heap 2234/0/55 -> 2235/0/8 (+17 ports, +2 restored,
  -18 heap.rs tests; the 8 still ignored are benchmarks); counted
  2184/0/3 -> 2116/0/3. 50 files, about 5,000 lines fewer.
- Gates:
  - aarch64: lib suites as counted above; clippy clean in both
    builds; rustfmt clean; the workspace builds with all targets,
    without a diagnostic.
  - Per-test GC stress with the verifier and poisoning, the 21 tests
    ported, restored or renamed: on x86-64 all 21 pass at interval 1;
    on aarch64 20 do, and the Symbol churn test (30,000 Symbols) times
    out there twice (900 s) and passes at interval 100. It joins the
    stress runs' slow list (interval 100).
  - x86-64 Linux (zombie): GC heap 1869/0/49, counted 1951/0/3.
  - Windows under Wine: GC heap 1863 pass, 3 fail, 49 ignored; the
    3 are step 1's snapshot codec tests.
- Open:
  - Two interruption tests can spin forever on a loaded machine
    (the agent saw them under full swap, with both suites running:
    tests::interruption_aborts_a_running_microtask_checkpoint in the
    GC heap, tests::job_callback_context_is_restored_on_host_
    interruption in the counted model). Each sets a 20 ms deadline and
    queues a while(true) job. From reading the code: if the deadline
    expires in the script body after the job is queued,
    eval_interruptible returns Interrupted and keeps the job (the
    checkpoint clears its queue on an interrupt; the body's path does
    not), and the test then clears the deadline and evaluates again,
    so the job spins with nothing to stop it. Test timing in both
    models, not the collector; to be reproduced, and the tests made
    deterministic, in a later commit. Whether an interrupted script's
    queued jobs should survive it is a question for the owner.
  - ClearKeptObjects clears only the root interpreter's kept list, so
    a WeakRef target kept inside a ShadowRealm may live as long as the
    realm. From reading the code; untested.
  - gc_core's S1 registration surface (Registration and its root,
    edge and sweep-hook callbacks) has no user; for step 8.

Step 1, the runs still going at its commit, and a follow-up (zombie,
x86-64, rustc 1.99.1):
- Per-test GC stress with the heap verifier and freed-cell poisoning
  (debug gc-heap lib tests from the step 1 tree, each test in its own
  process, as the S5 exit gate ran them on aarch64): of 1964 tests,
  1844 pass at interval 1, the 20 slow ones at interval 100 and 4 at
  interval 1000, and 96 are the suite's x86-64 gc-heap ignores. None
  fail or time out.
- Full Test262 (annexB, built-ins, harness, intl402, language,
  staging; no-LTO release runners from the step 1 tree): 53,575/53,575
  excluding skipped, in both builds at interp, bytecode and jit. All
  six summaries are byte-identical to the aarch64 references: main's
  at interp and bytecode, the S5 exit gate's at jit. On x86-64 the GC
  heap's jit tier still runs bytecode until step 7; the default
  build's runs the x64 JIT.
- The scanner tests (gc_core) in optimizing builds, on x86-64 Linux
  and on Windows under Wine, as S2 step 2 ran them on aarch64:
  - in-tree at f32b1fd6, the lib tests filtered to gc_core:: in the
    no-LTO release build and the repo's release profile (fat LTO):
    Linux 39 pass and Windows 40, with 5 ignored timing probes each;
  - gc_core.rs and fasthash.rs unchanged in a crate of their own, in
    debug and in release at opt-level 0/1/2/3/s/z, target-cpu=native
    and fat LTO: all pass on both (41 and 42 in debug, 39 and 40 in
    release). The Windows register and segment tests ran in each.
- Follow-up: x86-64 Linux had no register-only test (aarch64 hides a
  reference in d8, Windows in xmm6). reference_only_in_r15_is_found
  hides one in r15, callee-saved under System V. It passes in-tree in
  debug and, standalone, in every configuration above. With r15 left
  out of the spill it fails in debug only: in release builds a frame
  on the collection path saves r15 itself, where the stack scan finds
  it. System V's callee-saved registers are all general-purpose,
  which optimized frames commonly save.

Interruption tests made deterministic (2026-10-18). tests::
interruption_aborts_a_running_microtask_checkpoint and tests::job_
callback_context_is_restored_on_host_interruption (step 3's open item)
armed a 20 ms deadline before evaluating a script that queues a
spinning job. Reproduced with an experiment test (not kept): a script
body interrupted after queuing a job leaves the job to the next
evaluation's checkpoint, at bytecode and at jit. In these tests that
job spins, and the test clears the deadline before evaluating again.
Their spinning jobs now arm the deadline themselves (armDeadline(), a
test native), so it can expire only while a job runs. Both tests pass
in both builds on aarch64, x86-64 and Windows under Wine. Open for
the owner: whether an interrupted script's queued jobs should survive
it (the checkpoint clears its queue on an interrupt; the script body's
path does not).

Step 4 plan (2026-10-18), from a read-only survey of the counted
collectors at f106392f:
- The counted collectors are compiled into both builds. Under gc-heap
  the cycle collector (interpreter.rs, about 1,270 lines with its edge
  helpers) and the nursery (gc_generational.rs and its bookkeeping in
  value.rs, about 600) are kept alive by allow(dead_code). Only they
  use the edge cache (gc_edges.rs), gc_diagnostics.rs, NativeCapture-
  Snapshot, the host's counted trace arm and the six DirectGcEdges
  traces.
- Shared, and kept: the safepoint, task-boundary, host and explicit
  collection entries; next_threshold, whose default formula moves
  beside GC_TRIGGER (TRUST_JS_GC_GENERATIONAL=0 also changes the GC
  heap's trigger today); gc_finish_generation, slimmed.
- Knobs that go: TRUST_JS_GC_DUMP, TRUST_JS_GC_DUMP_MIN_OBJECTS,
  TRUST_JS_GC_DUMP_ROOT_LIMIT and TRUST_JS_GC_RETAINING_PATHS
  (documented in DIAGNOSTICS.md), TRUST_JS_GC_GENERATIONAL, the minor
  and scope counters, and the scope trigger (a no-op).
- The counted build ends with this step, fields whose offsets
  generated code embeds stay until step 8, and the host API keeps
  is_minor (see the plan's recorded deviations for step 4).
- Check: the GC heap's fixture code dumps identical to step 0's. At
  0d8e1d75 they are (vue, react, djot), by codedump_norm2.
- Open, outside step 4: under the GC heap, TRUST_JS_PERF_METRICS'
  managed-memory record has said "unavailable" since S2.
  record_post_gc runs only from the counted collectors, over their
  object lists.

Step 4, the counted collectors and their knobs (an implementation
agent's change, reviewed and amended):
- Deleted:
  - the cycle collector with its edge helpers (interpreter.rs, about
    1,300 lines), the nursery (gc_generational.rs), the edge cache
    with DirectGcEdges (gc_edges.rs) and gc_diagnostics.rs; the six
    DirectGcEdges traces, NativeCaptureSnapshot, the host's counted
    trace arm, the native graph's old-generation prefixes, and the GC
    heap's panicking Gc and Env strong_count;
  - work the GC heap did for them that nothing read: the registry's
    nursery membership (kept on every allocation and death),
    GcState's minor-collection fields, writes to Interp's
    gc_task_major;
  - knobs: TRUST_JS_GC_DUMP (with _MIN_OBJECTS and _ROOT_LIMIT),
    TRUST_JS_GC_RETAINING_PATHS, TRUST_JS_GC_GENERATIONAL, the scope
    registry's trigger (a no-op), and seven PERF_METRICS fields that
    the GC heap left at zero (gc_scopes_seen, gc_scopes_reclaimed,
    gc_peak_scopes_before, gc_last_scopes_after, gc_minor_collections,
    gc_minor_objects_seen, gc_minor_scopes_seen). Nothing tracked
    parses them; dated benchmark records keep them as data.
- next_threshold moved beside GC_TRIGGER with its default formula,
  checked equal to the old one on about 20 million inputs.
- Kept:
  - The public API's signatures. is_minor and native_old_generation
    return false and their docs say there is no nursery;
    unstable_collect_young_for_host_tests is still a full collection
    after an entry scrub, as under the GC heap before.
  - For layout, unused until step 8 (allow(dead_code)): Interp's
    gc_task_major and scope_gc_next, Object's gc_mark and gc_scratch.
    Removing the two Interp fields changed all three fixtures' dumps.
- Tests: 3 deleted (two for the withdrawn sparse-mask experiment, one
  for native old-generation prefixes). gc_edges' six continuation
  tests moved to coroutine::tests and gc_generational's three to
  gc_collect::tests (without "nursery" in their names), running the
  explicit collection where they ran counted ones; apart from the
  edge-budget loop, their sources and expected values are unchanged.
  Tests that called gc_collect_young (under the GC heap a full
  collection) call Interp::gc_collect: native_captures (now a
  test-only module), weak_metadata, jit_numeric_array_tests,
  iterator_result_tests, array_shift_tests, property_read_tests, and
  object_alloc_bench, whose minor timing went.
- Review changes:
  - With the counted collectors gone, record_post_gc has no caller
    outside tests. The agent made the retained-memory accounting
    test-only (about 100 cfg(test) gates in 23 files); the review put
    it back as it was, with one allow(dead_code) on record_post_gc,
    until the record is wired to the GC heap or replaced. Props::clear
    and two clear helpers it calls, which only the counted sweep used,
    are cfg(test): two tests use them.
  - The docs of HostGcVisitor::internal (no effect) and register_gc
    (no cycle collection), and nine comments that described the
    deleted collectors, the crate doc among them.
  - An x86-64-only test, own_data_reads_warm_after_native_compilation_
    and_clone_live_value_kinds (ignored until step 7), called the
    deleted strong_count; zombie's build found it. Its reference-count
    check went; its GC-heap check stays (the result survives a
    collection once its owner is cleared).
- Code dumps (dumps-vs-base.sh): vue, react and djot identical to
  step 0's. The agent's tree differed in djot (9 of 160 chunks), and
  so does HEAD (492d88ad) built with 15 codegen units instead of 16,
  from a fresh export: its djot dumps are byte-identical to the
  agent's. Under TRUST_JS_GC_LOG, djot's first collection (at 33,785
  objects) keeps 6,643 in step 0's binary, 5,285 in the agent's and
  5,286 in HEAD's at 15 codegen units; later collections and djot's
  inline planning follow, and the last keeps 1,305 in each. A codegen
  change alone moves retention, so this is the conservative stack
  scan's (a build's stack contents), not a layout change. The plan's
  dump check for steps 4-6 now allows this form (recorded deviation).
- Gates:
  - aarch64: lib 2232/0/8 (2235 less the 3 deleted); clippy clean
    for embed, embed with architecture-diagnostics, default features,
    the small build and embed with bench; rustfmt clean; the workspace
    builds with all targets without a diagnostic. Building without
    gc-heap fails with the compile_error (and two unused-parameter
    warnings, from bodies that are gc-heap-only until step 8).
  - Downstream suites as at step 2: host 36/0/0, timers 5/0/0, fs (no
    tests), web 61/0/1, node 42/0/0, runtime 182/0/1.
  - Fixtures at --tier=jit (no-LTO release): compiles 56/10/160,
    outputs as at S0 (masked md5 65b1faf9, 05a89273, 9cad1fec);
    difftest 138 agree, 12 skipped, no crash.
  - Per-test GC stress with the verifier and poisoning, the 24 moved,
    retargeted or touched tests. aarch64: 23 pass at interval 1; weak_
    metadata_minor_work_depends_on_dead_subscribers_not_stable_old_
    targets, on the slow list since S5, times out there (900 s) and
    passes at its interval 100. x86-64: the 22 it compiles pass at
    interval 1 (two numeric-array JIT tests are compiled out there
    until step 7).
  - x86-64 Linux (zombie): lib 1867/0/49 (step 3's 1869, plus the r15
    test, less the 3 deleted); clippy clean.
  - Windows under Wine: 1860 pass, 3 fail, 49 ignored; the 3 are step
    1's snapshot codec tests.
- Open:
  - The managed-memory record (open since S2): its accounting now has
    no production caller. Wiring record_post_gc to the GC heap's
    collections is not free: engine-matrix.json runs the JIT with
    TRUST_JS_PERF_METRICS=1, and the walk runs at every collection.
  - Public items the agent found removable, for step 11: is_minor,
    native_old_generation, unstable_collect_young_for_host_tests and
    HostGcVisitor::internal.
  - trust-js-difftest's header still says a dropped Engine leaks its
    realm through the Rc heap; for step 8's documentation pass.

Step 5, the counted handles, payloads and count observers (an
implementation agent's change, reviewed):
- Deleted, all cfg(not(gc-heap)) unless noted:
  - the counted Gc and Env handles (both `mod rc`), and the GC heap's
    stubs that served only counted code (compiled there): Gc's
    outlives_release, clone_from_raw and release (a no-op; its five
    JIT call-path callers now just pop the callee slot), and Env's
    weak_count, outlives_release and clone_from_raw;
  - the counted payloads: LStr's buffer and uniqueness-based append,
    jstr's thread-local interned strings, BigInt's, Symbol's
    (SymbolRef, WeakSymbol), UserFn, LazyProto, TemplateRoots and the
    lazy prototype's weak owner;
  - PackedValue's counted Clone, Drop, drop_word and clone_raw, and
    replace_raw (compiled in the GC heap for one test);
  - the VM's string-add retire (in Op::Add, two release helpers, and
    jit_add_strings);
  - map_data, and the scope registry with its counted snapshots and
    teardown;
  - count observers: the JitOwnership probe, the CALLSTAT "callee
    refcount" gate, and array destructuring's old-slot checks, which
    the GC heap compiled and which always passed there;
  - the counted representations (Fields and BindingEntries as Vec, the
    u32 slot memo, LiveCount's and rooting's and execution storage's
    forms) and counted test code.
- Kept: LStr::strong_count (public; it panics) and WeakGc::weak_count
  (public; no caller), for step 11. LStr's empty Drop impl, which
  keeps Value a type with drop glue; removing it changes codegen, and
  step 12 decides how far values become Copy. The JIT's cfg(not)
  group and Refcount ownership (step 6), and every positive cfg (step
  8).
- No test deleted; the suite's test list is unchanged.
  packed_raw_frame_moves_and_replacements_preserve_ownership now calls
  replace_value through the raw word, as replace_raw did.
- Docs: about 40 sites described counted handles, payloads or cycle
  collection as current (the module docs of gc_handle, env_handle,
  lstr and bigint; Engine's collection docs; HostTrace, edge and
  supports_native; the pins, the task trigger, string append). The
  review checked them against the code, and also dropped HostTrace's
  instruction to report handles with `internal` (no effect since step
  4), with the two test hosts' calls.
- The build without gc-heap now fails with the compile_error and 85
  unresolved names from the deleted counted halves, until step 8.
- Dump check, extended: the shared stubs (direct call, finish stub,
  intrinsics, name and property ways) are assembled once per process
  and appear in no per-chunk dump, so a change to them would pass
  unseen. The dumps also run with TRUST_JS_JIT_CHUNK_STUBS=1 (each
  chunk carries the stubs) and TRUST_JS_JIT_INLINE_STUBS=1 (stub code
  at every site). Step 0's and step 4's binaries agree in both modes,
  for all three fixtures.
- Gates:
  - aarch64: lib 2232/0/8, its test list identical to step 4's;
    clippy clean for embed, embed with architecture-diagnostics,
    default features, the small build and embed with bench; rustfmt
    clean; the workspace builds with all targets without a
    diagnostic.
  - Downstream suites: host 36/0/0, timers 5/0/0, fs (no tests), web
    61/0/1, node 42/0/0; the runtime's below.
  - Fixtures at --tier=jit (no-LTO release): compiles 56/10/160,
    outputs as at S0 (masked md5 65b1faf9, 05a89273, 9cad1fec);
    difftest 138 agree, 12 skipped, no crash.

Step 7 plan (2026-10-19), from a read-only survey of the x64 JIT at
492d88ad, a survey of Windows unwinding at e930404a and one Wine run:
- After step 6 the x64 JIT emits no count, and the tier clamp alone
  keeps it off under the GC heap: Tier::admitted runs Jit as Bytecode
  on x86-64, 44 tests are ignored there with the clamp's reason, and
  16 test files and 4 inline test modules compile out.
- Missing on x64, by the plan's list:
  - polls: the entry and loop polls compare only the live count with
    gc_next, so x64 loops miss the byte budget, the stress interval
    and deferred requests (the heap's due word); entry polls pass 0,
    not POLL_ENTRY; no call to an allocating helper ends in a poll;
  - InlineVec: x64 reads named fields and scope entries only from a
    Vec, so under the GC heap every property, name and Reference
    template declines to its helper. Ported, a length is a 32-bit
    load: a 64-bit one reads len | cap << 32, which passes a spilled
    object's bounds check;
  - the packed `this` at [r12+0x30], which x64's property templates
    decline (they read it as a pointer to a wide `this`);
  - string constants, which x64 sends to the helper in every build.
- Windows: no unwind information is registered for JIT code and
  every helper is extern "C", so a panic under a helper aborts there,
  on any platform; errors leave JIT code as return values. One panic
  does reach a helper under Wine: the segment assertion (the plan's
  recorded deviation), seen in step 1's run of the counted build and
  reproduced with --nocapture.
- References: no counted x64 build is left. Step 1's x86-64 Test262
  reports (the counted build's jit tier ran the x64 JIT, byte-
  identical to aarch64's) and the aarch64 suite are the comparison.
- JitCtx's offsets are asserted on aarch64 only, while x64 hard-codes
  0x30 and 0x48: the asserts extend to x86-64.
- Correction to S5 step 7's entry ("x64 uses no records"): x64's
  moved entries do pop FramePool records. The records are traced, so
  the conclusion stands.

Step 7a, the x64 JIT's polls, the segment fix and the clamp lifted
(an implementation agent's change, reviewed):
- Polls (x64, gc-heap), as ARM64's: the entry and loop polls take
  their slow path while the heap's due word is set, before the
  count's comparison with gc_next, and the entry poll passes
  POLL_ENTRY. Every call of an allocating helper ends in the explicit
  poll (three instructions while the word is clear, else H_INTERRUPT
  with POLL_HELPER_END): helper_ends_in_poll is now shared by both
  backends, and H_CALL, H_NEW and string accumulation's committed
  exit poll at their ends. x64 keeps no value in a caller-saved
  register across a helper call (r12-r15 hold the context, operand
  top, helper table and slot base; [rsp] the strict byte), so a
  poll's collection finds every live word in place.
- The segment fix (the recorded deviation): at the segment cap the
  exhaustion check fires within EXECUTION_STACK_CHECK_MARGIN (256
  KiB) above the red zone, and a growth checkpoint at the cap runs
  the call on the current stack instead of adding a segment. The
  debug assertion is gone; tests count the checkpoints that reach
  the cap. A new test positions the stack exactly, in a thread of
  its own, and sets the segment count without allocating segments;
  the exhaustion test asserts that no checkpoint reached the cap, in
  every tier.
- The clamp lifted: Tier::admitted deleted with its callers (the
  engine binary's and difftest's notes, Engine::set_tier and the
  initial tier, now main's code, and 67 test sites), with the 44
  ignores and 25 x86-64 compile-out gates.
- Returning tests: 36 of the 44 ignored tests, the 115 tests of 19
  compiled-out modules, the long-callback and OSR-prefix tests pass
  unchanged. The poll, rooting and traced program tests now run on
  x86-64 too. x64 has no regions and no direct call sequence, so
  their region counts, direct-return counts and checks of ARM64
  code stay aarch64-only, and on x86-64 the poll tests assert the
  baseline forms (both of test 1's loops run in baseline code; under
  TRUST_JS_GC_STRESS the call helper's own safepoint collects before
  the callee's entry poll can). 8 tests are ignored until step 7b:
  x64's names (4) and property (4) template tests, which need the
  templates x64 still declines under the GC heap.
- layout_asserts covers x86-64: the JitCtx offsets 0-72 that both
  backends embed; CallIc, FnFrame and JitCode stay ARM64's.
- Comments that step 6 left speaking of counts now say what holds:
  no word owns anything, and the collector traces it.
- Review: every census helper reaches its poll through helper_spflag.
  x64's other direct helper calls are H_STRING_ACCUMULATION (it
  polls explicitly) and, for the census's own reasons, H_COND,
  H_RETURN, H_PUSH_HANDLER and H_POP_HANDLER (they allocate no cell)
  and H_UNWIND and H_COMPLETE (they route a completion). A poll
  clobbers rcx and the flags; through H_INTERRUPT it also sets rax to
  the canonical sp, which r13 holds, and leaves rdx clear, as the
  helper's own return did. The clamp's sites now equal main's.
- Mutation checks on x86-64, each restored exactly: without the
  due-word test 4 of the 6 poll tests fail, without the end polls 5;
  with the segment fix undone the new test fails at the margin, and
  with only the checkpoint's cap undone, at the old assertion.
- Gates:
  - aarch64: lib 2230/0/8, its test list step 6's plus the new test;
    clippy clean in the five configurations; rustfmt clean; the
    workspace builds with all targets without a diagnostic; without
    gc-heap the build fails with the same errors as at step 6.
  - Downstream suites: host 36/0/0, timers 5/0/0, fs (no tests), web
    61/0/1, node 42/0/0. The runtime suite ran while the machine's
    load average was 137 and failed two tests:
    worker_terminate_aborts_current_synchronous_script (25.4 s
    against its 5 s bound) and a Postgres adapter test (its client
    did not connect). The Postgres binary passes on rerun. Of four
    reruns of the worker test, two took 8.8 and 6.6 s and two 1.7 s.
    Alternated with a fresh build of 8edc86a0, it passed 8 times in
    each build (1.6-1.7 s): the bimodal timing step 5's A/B found
    at step 4. A full rerun (load average 12) gave 182/0/1, as at
    step 6.
  - Fixtures at --tier=jit (no-LTO release): compiles 56/10/160,
    outputs as at S0 (masked md5 65b1faf9, 05a89273, 9cad1fec);

    the shared stubs in both modes. djot's differ from step 0's (18,

  - Per-test GC stress with the verifier and poisoning:

Step 7b, the x64 JIT's GC-heap templates (an implementation agent's
change, reviewed):
- InlineVec: a new storage module (jit_x64_storage.rs) mirrors
  ARM64's readers in x64's encodings. vec_len loads an InlineVec's
  length as its 32-bit word, zero-extended (a Vec's as 8 bytes);
  vec_data addresses the elements in the cell while the capacity
  word is 0, else behind the heap pointer; named fields and binding
  entries wrap them, each gated by a disp32 *_fits predicate. The
  own-data and Number property templates, the name templates'
  layout check and the global and deep targets use them, so
  vec_fields and vec_scope_entries are gone. emit_prop_own's bounds
  check is now a 32-bit load and a register compare. The register-
  generic encoders (nr_*) moved unchanged from the names module into
  jit_x64.rs, which the storage module shares.
- The packed `this`: one receiver_cell serves both property
  templates. The This form checks the tag of [r12+0x30]
  (JitCtx::this_word) and untags it, as the Slot form reads its slot;
  this_form_ok is gone.
- String constants: Op::Const of a string pushes its
  jit_const_str_word with movabs. Only code the chunk owns embeds it:
  x64 has no shared stubs, a compile's buffer is committed to its
  chunk's jit slot or OSR sidecar, a spliced callee's constants are
  the caller's own (the splice is bytecode in the caller's chunk),
  and a script body's one-shot code runs while it borrows its chunk.
- Map and Set (the recorded deviation): Asm::guard_exotic_ordinary
  takes ARM64's range test where exotic_collection_admitted holds
  (its cfg now covers both backends). It guards emit_prop_own's
  receiver, emit_prop_num's receiver, hops and holder, and the
  Reference template's object base, as ARM64's
  emit_reference_target_ptr does; the name templates' global-object
  checks stay exact, as on ARM64.
- No other GC-heap layout difference came up: Binding.value (wide),
  the scope, parent and name words, Property meta and the lazy-
  prototype tags are read as ARM64 reads them. Kept: x64's count-
  motivated declines (step 12), the embedded offsets and the helper
  table (step 8); ARM64 code unchanged.
- Returning tests: the 8 ignored x64 template tests pass unchanged.
  The this and string-constant tests run on x86-64; ARM64's counters
  (direct returns, regions, chains, template paths) stay aarch64-
  only. On x86-64 they count x64's own-data reads by receiver form
  (TEST_OWN_PROPERTY_READS: [slot, this]): an object `this` 20 reads
  per 20 calls, a primitive's none; the chains' `this` 80 for
  orders(20), none for controls(20); the region test [20, 20, 0].
  The string-constant tests find each constant's movabs immediate in
  its chunk's code. The Map and Set program counts 100 own-data
  reads per labels(50) on x86-64.
- New tests (x86-64): the storage emitters as machine code against
  synthetic sequences of both layouts (a spilled one with a nonzero
  capacity word), live objects of 1, 2, 4, 5 and 9 fields and live
  scopes, Small and Indexed, in place and spilled; the own-data
  template in place and spilled from a slot and from `this`; a fresh
  holder's binding in place and spilled, counting both the resolved
  target and the native read (the target counter alone also counts a
  read that falls back to the helper).
- Mutation checks on x86-64, each restored exactly: a 64-bit length
  load fails the 3 storage tests (a length reads 6 | 9 << 32); the
  in-place/spilled choice inverted fails 3 tests and crashes 2; the
  This form read as a pointer again crashes 7; without the string
  arm 2 tests fail; without the Collection admission the Map and Set
  program counts 50 reads, not 100. On zombie a restored file synced
  with its older mtime let cargo reuse a mutated test binary; the
  agent cleaned that target dir and reran its suites.
- Gates (the review's, on the reviewed tree):
  - aarch64: lib 2230/0/8, its test list step 7a's; clippy clean in
    the five configurations; rustfmt clean; the workspace builds with
    all targets without a diagnostic; without gc-heap the build fails
    with the same errors as at step 7a.
  - Downstream suites: host 36/0/0, timers 5/0/0, fs (no tests), web
    61/0/1, node 42/0/0, runtime 182/0/1.
  - Fixtures at --tier=jit (no-LTO release): compiles 56/10/160,
    outputs as at S0 (masked md5 65b1faf9, 05a89273, 9cad1fec);
    difftest 138 agree, 12 skipped, no crash.
  - Code dumps: vue and react identical to step 0's, plain and with
    the shared stubs in both modes. djot's differ from step 0's (18,
    18 and 8 lines) and are identical in all three forms to the
    parent's (ef2ffe1c) built with 15 codegen units.
  - Per-test GC stress with the verifier and poisoning:
    - aarch64, the 841 tests whose names match the JIT's, the
      stack's, the tiers' and the templates' areas: 827 pass at
      interval 1 (one bench is ignored), the slow lists' 10 at 100
      and 3 at 1000.
    - x86-64 (zombie), the whole lib suite, 2082 tests: 2048 pass
      and 8 are ignored at interval 1 (3,762 s); the slow lists' 22
      pass at 100 and their 4 at 1000.
  - x86-64 (zombie): clippy clean; lib 2074/0/8 (aarch64's 8
    ignores). The 8 template tests run, and the list gains 15: the
    string-constant (3) and this (7) tests, now built for x86-64,
    and the 5 new tests. Windows: clippy as its baseline (23
    diagnostics); under Wine 2067 pass, 3 fail (step 1's snapshot
    codec tests) and 8 are ignored.
  - Test262 at --tier=jit on x86-64: 53575/53575, the summary
    identical to step 1's x86-64 report and to the S5 exit gate's
    aarch64 report.

Step 8 plan (2026-10-19), from a read-only survey of the committed
tree at ef2ffe1c:
- The flag: gc-heap is named on 10 manifest lines in 8 crates
  (trust-js's default list and feature; host, fs, timers, web, node,
  runtime and difftest each have it as their only feature) and on
  638 lines in 84 engine files: 323 cfg attributes and 9 cfg! folds
  that become unconditional, 31 compound conditions that simplify,
  the compile_error and POLL_ENTRY's else arm (the only dead code
  left), 270 comment lines and 4 strings. Elsewhere only dated
  records name it; heap-bridge survives only in dated records and
  the djot fixture's input.
- At step 8's base (4a0b83c5): 647 lines in 87 files, with 324
  attributes (step 7b's string-constant arm) and 44 lines of other
  conditions (7b's three x86-64 test gates); the manifests as at
  ef2ffe1c.
- Risk: once the manifests drop the feature, a missed cfg compiles
  its item out with at most a warning, and several gated statements
  are root guards and polls whose loss still compiles. So the cfgs
  are unwrapped to zero before the manifests change, clippy runs
  with -Dwarnings on aarch64, x86-64 and Windows, and each test list
  must equal its parent's. Unwrapped, 14 gated tail returns need
  rewriting for clippy, and 19 gated blocks flatten without changing
  a drop order.
- Users: TRust depends on trust-js with features = ["embed"] and
  never names gc-heap. No public item is gated; one public doc
  (NativeCallableRetained) names the feature. The small build
  becomes --no-default-features.
- Deferred items found: gc_core's registration surface (about 220
  lines) has no engine user but roots gc_core's test fixture (41
  Fixture::new() calls in its 51 tests). Interp's gc_task_major and
  scope_gc_next and Object's gc_mark and gc_scratch move probed
  offsets that generated code embeds (Object likely 184 to 176
  bytes, the same size class). H_DROP_AT and H_DROP_PACKED_AT are
  unused (N_HELPERS 33). gc_data_off and scope_data_off measure 0
  yet emit 11 adds on ARM64 (two of them moves) and 4 leas on x64.
  obj_from_rc is a real offset (8) under a counted-model name;
  LStr's `strong` holds a frontier.
- Method: 8a's code is checked as the release engine's .text, byte-
  identical to the parent's when both builds enable the same
  features. Cargo hashes a unit's enabled features into its symbol
  names, and a changed feature list alone reorders the code:
  ef2ffe1c built with a dummy default feature differs in 1,277,254
  of its 5,230,488 .text bytes, every function's size the same. So
  the child's comparison build re-declares gc-heap, as an empty
  default feature, in a copy of the tree. Checked by deleting every
  standalone gc-heap attribute line and rewording a manifest
  comment: the .text stays identical (no-LTO; aarch64 at ef2ffe1c,
  x86-64 at 4a0b83c5).
- Stays, for later steps: the PropertyLayout count emitters and the
  count-motivated guards (step 12), the host API's nursery-shaped
  items (steps 10 and 11).

Step 8a, the gc-heap feature gone, its cfgs unwrapped (an
implementation agent's change, reviewed):
- The feature: trust-js's default list is ["intl"] and its
  gc-heap = [] is gone with its comment; host, fs, timers, web,
  node, runtime and difftest lost the [features] tables that only
  forwarded it. The intl comment gives the small build as
  --no-default-features; --features gc-heap is now a cargo error.
- The engine: 324 #[cfg(feature = "gc-heap")] attributes and 9 cfg!
  folds gone; 34 compound conditions lost their feature term (the
  survey's 31 and step 7b's three x86-64 test gates); the crate's
  cfg_attr is a plain #![allow] of the same four lints. The
  compile_error is gone, and POLL_ENTRY is 1.
- Unwrapped, 14 gated tail returns became tail expressions, and 19
  gated blocks and 7 `if cfg!` bodies were flattened: each binds
  nothing or ends its function or closure, so no drop order, borrow
  or root guard changed. Comments naming the flag or the counted
  build stay for step 8b (278 lines in 74 files), except POLL_ENTRY's

- The check (the plan's method): the no-LTO release engine's .text,
  built with the parent's feature list, is byte-identical to step
  7b's on aarch64 (5,230,488 bytes) and x86-64 (5,300,576 bytes).
- Found, not changed: move_collection's non-Collection arm borrows
  `from` mutably while the match's scrutinee still holds its first
  mutable borrow, a panic if reached. Its only caller is the native

  built-ins at --tier=interp, and at every tier subclasses of bound
  Error, Array, Map, Promise and 8 other natives, and
  Reflect.construct of a bound Error. Open (binding the replaced
  value before the match fixes it).

Step 8b, what still spoke of the flag, the counted build or Rc

The move_collection fix (step 8's recorded deviation, after 8b):
- move_collection took `from`'s exotic state in the scrutinee of the
  match whose non-Collection arm put it back, so that arm borrowed
  `from` mutably while the scrutinee's borrow was still live: a
  "RefCell already borrowed" panic for any source that is not a Map
  or Set. The state is now taken in a statement of its own.

  Map's data; without the fix it panics at the second borrow.

Step 8c, gc_core's registration surface gone, its tests re-rooted
(an implementation agent's change, reviewed):
- Deleted: the S1 surface for registered Rust-heap roots, which
  nothing outside gc_core used (Registry, Registration and its Drop,
  the RootRange, TraceRoot, EdgeSource and SweepHook entries, the
  four add_* methods, the heap's four registry fields, the marking
  of root ranges and trace roots, the edge-source loop, the sweep
  hooks), with the docs that described it; Heap::is_live and
  MarkSource::Conditional's key, which nothing else read.
- The module-wide allow(dead_code) went with it (the recorded
  deviation): 39 items and statements only tests use are now
  cfg(test), among them collect_at_safepoint, nogc, borrow_scope,
  enter_native_stack and retention tracing. configure_heap reads
  TRUST_JS_GC_WHY in test builds only, and other builds' marking
  keeps no record of what marked each cell. The debug verifier is
  called under `if cfg!(debug_assertions)`, so release builds
  compile it too, and WeakSink owns the marker's two scratch vectors
  while a weak callback runs.
- Tests: the fixture's 64 root words reach the heap through a test
  MarkClient, and its collections run start_marking and
  finish_collection, as the engine's do. Five tests of the deleted
  surface went (root ranges re-pointed and dropped, trace roots and
  sources dropped, a hook dropping another, sweep-hook liveness, the
  ignored sweep-hook perf test). Three side-table tests report
  through the client's EdgeSink instead, pruning between the phases
  as the engine does, with their assertions unchanged. Four tests
  whose collections the heap drives (stress, the budget, retained
  large objects, perf churn) hold their roots in their own frames,
  where the conservative scan finds them.
- Mutation check: with the fixture's client reporting no roots, 16
  of the 38 gc_core tests that run in debug fail, survival tests
  among them.
- Review: TRUST_JS_GC_WHY's read moved under cfg(test) in
  configure_heap, replacing a set_retention_tracing that did nothing
  outside tests; DIAGNOSTICS.md says it is a test-build diagnostic;
  frame_return_address's two doc comments merged into one.
- Open, for step 10's plan: the in-cell weak callbacks have no
  engine user either. Every engine descriptor has `weak: None`, and
  only gc_core's tests report weak slots or ephemerons through a
  WeakSink. The nursery's plan decides whether they stay.
