The scan-bound fix (step 8's recorded deviation, before 8d):
- Found by 8d's implementation agent: with 8d, five x86-64 lib
  tests that check that cells die failed every run (two
  named-expression entry tests and the dead-code-owner, expired
  global-env-pin and group-by callee tests). Under gdb on zombie the
  retaining word was in thread-local storage, 16 bytes into
  jit_cache's REGISTRY: the unused payload of `pressure_miss: None`,
  a stale cell address copied from the stack temporary that the
  lazily built RefCell<Registry> was moved from. 8d's smaller
  JitLayout only moved which stale word lands there.
- Why a thread-local is scanned: on Linux a thread registers its
  stack up to the top of the block pthread_getattr_np reports, and
  for a spawned thread glibc (musl too) puts the thread descriptor
  and static TLS at the top of that block, above the outermost
  frame. The main thread's live elsewhere, so the CLI's scripts
  never had this; tests, workers and Test262's workers did.
- The fix: the registered top is now the lowest of the mapping's
  top and, between the current frame and it, the thread descriptor
  (pthread_self) and this thread's instance of every loaded
  module's TLS block (dl_iterate_phdr's dlpi_tls_data). No engine
  thread-local holds a cell inline as a root: the rooting table,
  frame lists and caches keep cells in heap-allocated vectors, which
  the scan does not read.
- New test, gc_core::tests::a_thread_local_is_not_a_root (Linux): on
  a spawned thread the registered top lies between a local of the
  thread's closure and a thread-local, and a cell that only a
  thread-local holds dies at a conservative collection. Without the
  bound the first assertion fails; with that assertion relaxed too,
  the cell survives (3 of 3 runs). With the fix it passes 10 of 10
  runs in debug and in release. Its helper native::entry_top is
  Linux-only, with a stub in the module for targets without a
  scanner, as innermost_region has.
- Gates (on the fix's tree):
  - aarch64: lib 2228/0/7, its test list 8c's and the new test;
    clippy clean in the five configurations; rustfmt clean; the
    workspace builds with all targets without a diagnostic. gc_core's
    tests in release, no-LTO and fat LTO: 37 pass, 4 ignored.
    gc_core.rs and fasthash.rs in a crate of their own: 39 pass in
    debug and 37 in release, 4 ignored each.
  - Downstream suites: host 36/0/0, timers 5/0/0, fs (no tests), web
    61/0/1, node 42/0/0, runtime 182/0/1 (load average up to 43).
  - Fixtures at --tier=jit (no-LTO release): compiles 56/10/160,
    outputs as at S0; difftest 138 agree, 12 skipped, no crash. The
    fixtures' code dumps are 8c's, plain and with the shared stubs
    in both modes: the CLI runs on the main thread.
  - Test262 at --tier=jit (worker threads), threshold 8: 53575 of
    53575, the report identical to the S5 review fixes'.
  - Per-test GC stress with the verifier and poisoning, aarch64, the
    964 tests whose names match the JIT's, the stack's, the
    collector's and threads' areas: 941 pass at interval 1 (5 are
    ignored), the slow lists' 15 at 100 and 3 at 1000.
  - x86-64 (zombie): clippy clean; lib 2072/0/7. Windows: clippy
    had one diagnostic past its baseline's 23 at first, entry_top
    unused there; with it Linux-only the diagnostics are the
    baseline's, and those checks and both lib suites ran again.
    Under Wine 2064 pass, 3 fail (step 1's snapshot codec tests) and
    7 are ignored. The x86-64 list is 8c's and the new test;
    Windows's is 8c's.
  - The scanner tests on x86-64 Linux and on Windows under Wine:
    in-tree in the no-LTO release build and with fat LTO, 37 pass on
    Linux and 36 on Windows (the new test is Linux-only), 4 ignored
    on each; gc_core.rs and fasthash.rs in a crate of their own pass
    on both in debug (39 and 38) and in release at opt-level
    0/1/2/3/s/z, target-cpu=native and fat LTO (37 and 36).
  - Per-test GC stress with the verifier and poisoning, x86-64
    (zombie), the whole lib suite, 2079 tests: 2046 pass and 7 are
    ignored at interval 1 (3,740 s); the slow lists' 22 pass at 100
    and their 4 at 1000.

Step 8d, the always-zero data offsets gone (an implementation
agent's change, reviewed):
- Deleted: JitLayout's gc_data_off and scope_data_off, the offsets
  from a stored object or environment handle word to its Gc::as_ptr
  or Env::as_ptr identity, both 0 since a handle's word became its
  cell's address. The probe now requires each stored word to be its
  identity (object_identity_ok, scope_identity_ok, and a closure's
  env word its Env::as_ptr); if one is not, the layout is not valid,
  as for the probe's other checks.
- Generated code: ARM64 loses nine no-op adds (`add xN, xN, #0`),
  its two register moves spelled that way are `mov` (x13 from x10
  in emit_call_inline_marked, x4 from x10 in emit_direct_call), and
  the accessor landing subtracts obj_off alone. x64 loses three
  no-op `lea r9, [r9+0]`, and its move spelled `lea rax, [r9-0]` is
  `mov rax, r9`. Range checks, the x64 names module's `valid` and
  the docs no longer name the fields; jit_layout_tests assert the
  identities instead.
- Review: two ARM64 null tests only skipped a deleted add, so each
  branched to the next instruction (emit_direct_call's parent cache
  and emit_prop_create_probe's proto); they went too, and the
  comments say what the word is (an identity, or null).
- Found on the way: the scan-bound fix's defect (the previous
  entry). With 8d on 8c, five x86-64 tests that check that cells
  die failed; on the fix they pass.
- The dumps check is new, dumpcmp-8d.py: the brief's
  dumpcmp-zero.py allows neither moved Interp offsets nor dropped
  null tests. Interp holds its JitLayout inline (552 bytes, now
  536), so the 60 members after it moved 16 bytes down (Interp 5832
  bytes, now 5816). Per chunk, with the no-op adds dropped, then
  each CBZ/CBNZ to the next kept instruction, and the moves' two
  encodings one token: the parent's and the child's instruction
  streams are equal but for load, store and add immediates that
  address a member of Interp at its parent offset and its child
  offset (the layouts read from both release engines' DWARF,
  dwarf-layout.py); the child drops only no-op instructions that the
  parent has at the same place (by kind and the number of kept
  instructions before it); and the counts differ by what the child
  lost.
- Kept: two other offsets that are 0 in this build, a binding's
  value (binding_value, at two sites) and a property entry's
  (entry_value), still emit no-op adds; every one left in the
  dumps is theirs, `add x14, x14, #0`. They are measured layout
  offsets, not identities, and 8d leaves them.

Step 8e, the layout-only fields gone (an implementation agent's
change, reviewed without changes):
- Deleted: Interp's gc_task_major and scope_gc_next and Object's
  gc_mark and gc_scratch, unused since step 4 removed the counted
  collectors and kept only so that the offsets generated code embeds

  test now counts 167) and the arms of the three exhaustive patterns
  (Interp's two and Object's GcTrace).

  168, so RefCell<Object> is 176 in the same 192-byte size class;
  ic_plain moved from 168 to 164 and gc_weak_observed, extensible
  and is_constructor from 170-172 to 165-167; gc_internal stays at
  160. Interp 5816 bytes, now 5808: the 30 members after the gaps
  moved, 22 by 8 bytes (module_async_seq to gc_task_pending) and 8
  by 9 (constructing to inline_ic_safe).
- is_constructor's doc described a construct-time prototype cache.
  It now says what the field records: IsConstructor, set when an
  object gets its [[Call]] (user functions other than arrows,
  methods, generators and async functions; natives marked as
  constructors; bound functions and callable proxies whose target
  was one), and value_is_constructor's rule for natives with an own
  data `prototype`. The size-class assert's comment gives the new
  size.

  fixtures, plain and with the shared stubs in both modes, is equal
  up to load, store and add immediates that a moved member of Object
  or Interp explains. It cannot see three moves: no fixture's code
  reads is_constructor, and Interp's strict and constructing lie
  past a byte load's reach, so their offsets sit in mov sequences
  (byte_field_address), which the tokens mask. All three offsets
  come from the probes. call_site and gc_tick moved too; the
  emitters only check their range.
- Memory snapshots (memory.rs) count size_of::<RefCell<Object>>()
  per object body, so they now report 176 bytes an object; the cell
  is still 192.

Step 8f, the helper table's two drop slots gone (an implementation
agent's change, reviewed):
- Deleted: H_DROP_AT and H_DROP_PACKED_AT (slots 9 and 19, which no
  generated code called: no word owns anything), their
  helper_table() and HELPER_NAMES entries, jit_drop_at,
  jit_drop_packed_at with its debug panic, and
  PackedValue::drop_raw, whose only caller it was.
- Renumbered: the last two helpers take the freed slots, H_ITERATION
  31 to 9 and H_STRING_ACCUMULATION 32 to 19, so no other index
  moves (H_INTERRUPT stays 22, H_GET_METHOD_ELEM 24). N_HELPERS 33,
  now 31.
- Interp holds the table inline (jit_helpers at 4112, 264 bytes, now
  248), so the 59 members after it, jit_layout to inline_ic_safe,
  moved 16 bytes down, in the same order: Interp 5808 bytes, now
  5792. Object is unchanged (168).
- Tests: traced_property_stores_call_no_drop_helper (ARM64 only),
  which scanned the traced stores for loads of the two slots, is
  gone, and the traced tests' header no longer lists it. The packed
  native ABI test in bytecode.rs loses its jit_drop_at call and the
  doc sentences about the drop helpers. Docs that said no generated
  code calls a drop helper now say the table has none (jit_ownership's
  header) or drop the clause (two word-routing tests), and the poll
  table's "not polled" list no longer names the two.
- Review: the helper identity test also checks the two renumbered
  helpers' names.
- The dumps check (layout-dumps.sh, with the helper map 31:9 and
  32:19): every chunk is equal up to the two helpers' table loads
  and immediates that a moved member of Interp explains. Plain dumps
  renumber only djot's six string-accumulation calls (plain mode's
  helper stubs are shared by the process, not in the dumps); with the
  stubs per chunk or inline, iteration's calls renumber too. As at
  8e, the moves of strict and constructing sit in mov sequences, and
  the emitters only check gc_tick's and call_site's range.

The dump reference for steps 9-12 (02d7f81d; ref-8f.sh):
- A no-LTO release engine of 8f's commit, built in a fresh target
  dir, is byte-identical to the 8f review gate's. Its fixture code
  dumps, plain and with the shared stubs per chunk and inline, each
  made twice, are identical run to run once normalized
  (codedump_norm2) and identical to the review gate's: vue 56
  chunks, react 10, djot 160. They are kept in target/s6-ref, and
  dumps-vs-ref.sh compares a tree's dumps with them chunk by chunk.
- Correction to 8f's entry: the raw word totals vary from run to run
  with one binary (vue with the stubs inline: 447,516 and 447,513
  words in the reference's two runs; with the stubs per chunk,
  250,499 in both, against the review gate's 250,498). The one- and
  three-word differences from 8e's totals are embedded heap
  addresses that vary between runs, not the smaller binary.

Step 9 plan (2026-10-20), from a read-only survey of 02d7f81d and
profiles of its fat-LTO engine:
- Triggers. A collection is due when:
  - the live object count passes Interp::gc_next: 100,000 at first,
    then the live count plus an eighth or plus 100,000, whichever is
    more, clamped to the live-object limit;
  - the heap's byte budget is spent: after a sweep, twice the live
    bytes, at least 8 MiB (TRUST_JS_GC_MIN_HEAP_BYTES);
  - GC stress asks, or a deferred request waits.
  ArrayBuffer data (64 MiB), task boundaries (object growth and
  churn since the last one) and explicit and idle requests collect
  too. The byte budget reaches generated code through the heap's due
  word; the JIT's polls (ARM64 and x64, at function entry and loop
  headers) also compare the live count with gc_next.
- Which fires (TRUST_JS_GC_LOG, and reruns with the byte budget
  raised out of reach): on vue, react and djot every collection that
  allocation triggers comes from the 8 MiB floor, and none reaches
  100,000 objects. On benchmarks/sustained-allocation.js the object
  count fires the 12 steady-state collections of 18 (38.6 MB and
  201k objects live after each).
- Marking resolves every traced edge in Heap::locate: an FxHash map
  from address >> 16 to the page's slot, then a divide. Small cells
  sit in 64 KiB-aligned pages without a header; cells over 4 KiB
  (long strings) are separate allocations, kept in a BTreeMap.
- The sweep collects every dead object's address into a hash set,
  which the engine's table sweeps and weak processing consult, and
  probes the set of weakly pinned cells for every dead cell.
- Profiles (perf record, CPU 5, --tier=jit, self time as a share of
  the process's cycles):
  - sustained allocation: locate 9.2%, the dead set's inserts 6.9%,
    trace_object_cell 7.7%, mark_word 4.1%, mark 2.7%, sweep_phase
    2.5%;
  - djot: the pinned probes 2.2%, the dead set's inserts 1.8%,
    locate 0.9%, the rest of marking 1.1%.
- Step 9 runs as three commits, each gated like 8a-8f and measured
  against its parent:
  - 9a: the byte budget replaces the object-count trigger. gc_next
    becomes the live-object ceiling: the limit, or after the limit's
    RangeError the room its handler gets. The JIT's polls keep
    reading it, so generated code does not change. The performance
    record also splits GC pause time by cause, so that measurements
    can leave out the shell's exit collection.
  - 9b: locate finds a small cell's page through a two-level table
    indexed by address bits (the 48-bit payload >> 16) instead of
    the hash map, for every word it resolves, conservative ones
    included. Large cells keep their map.
  - 9c: the sweep tells dead objects from live ones without a hash
    set of every dead object, and skips the pinned probes when no
    cell is pinned.
- The task-boundary trigger stays. It serves embedders' quiet points
  and FinalizationRegistry cleanup; its end-of-eval collection
  re-marks the whole heap (on the sustained workload it reclaims
  nothing). The nursery (step 10) revisits it.
- Measurement (gcmeasure.py): fat-LTO binaries of each commit and of
  main (e3310993), each from a fresh export in a fresh target dir,
  run as fresh copies made together; vue, react and djot at
  --tier=jit and the sustained workload at --tier=jit and bytecode,
  with TRUST_JS_PERF_METRICS; perf stat on CPU 5; 15 rounds in
  rotating order; medians. GC share is pause time over wall time.
- Dumps: none of the three changes emitted code or when the
  fixtures collect, so their dumps must be the reference's (dumps-
  vs-ref.sh), djot's GC-timing-sensitive chunks excepted under step
  4's rule.

Step 9a, the byte budget the only allocation trigger (an
implementation agent's change, reviewed):
- Interp::gc_next is no longer a growth trigger but the live-object
  ceiling: the live-object limit, or after the limit's RangeError
  the live count plus LIMIT_ERROR_HANDLER_ROOM (100,000) for the
  handler. Every writer sets it so: the initializer, the collector,
  gc_check, gc_task_boundary, collect_garbage_for_host and
  Engine::set_live_object_limit, whose floor stays 100,000
  (MIN_CONFIGURED_LIVE_OBJECT_LIMIT). next_threshold and GC_TRIGGER
  are gone. gc_next's readers are unchanged, generated code among
  them.
- The performance record reports GC pause time by cause as well
  (gc_pause_seconds_by_cause, keyed like gc_causes), and
  DIAGNOSTICS.md says that its counts and pauses include the shell's
  exit collection.
- Test object_count_alone_does_not_collect: with the heap's budget
  floor out of reach, 300,000 objects, half of them kept, allocate
  without a collection at each tier; with the engine's floor back,
  the next safepoint collects once. The tests that set gc_next to
  force a collection are unchanged.

Step 9b, marking finds a cell's page without hashing (an
implementation agent's change, reviewed):
- Heap::page_map, an FxHash map from page number to the page's slot,
  is now a PageTable indexed directly by the page number of the
  48-bit payload: its high 16 bits select a leaf of 65,536 entries
  (4 GiB of addresses), its low 16 bits the entry, which holds the
  slot plus one, or 0. new_page inserts, release_page removes, the
  heap's release clears it and unmaps the leaves, and locate reads
  it for every word the collector resolves; the large-cell fallback
  and the divide are unchanged.
- Both levels are zeroed memory mapped from the OS (mmap on Linux
  and macOS, VirtualAlloc on Windows, a zeroed allocation elsewhere):
  512 KiB of addresses for the top level and 256 KiB per leaf, of
  which only the pages holding entries in use are written. A probe
  of /proc/self/pagemap found one or two leaves per run of the
  fixtures and the sustained workload, and one or two pages written
  in the top level and one to three in a leaf; pages that the
  conservative scan only reads map the shared zero page. The brief
  suggested a zeroed Vec for the top level, but glibc served it from
  its heap and wrote every page in three of four runs, once its
  dynamic mmap threshold had risen.
- Debug builds check the table after each sweep and at the heap's
  release: every live page maps to its slot, and the table holds as
  many entries as there are live pages. A page above the 48-bit
  payload panics at insertion (the map took it, but locate could
  never find it).
- Tests: the table's operations (pages in the first, a middle and
  the last leaf and at a leaf's ends, pages and slots reused); the
  heap's table through release, reuse and the heap's release; a
  precise edge from a small cell to a large one and on to a small
  one (gc_core) and a long string held only by a property (engine,
  Interp and Bytecode tiers), which no test covered; conservative
  words in a spare page, between pages and in a 4 GiB range without
  pages, on the stack and as roots, find nothing (interior addresses
  were covered already). The three tests that do not use the table
  pass at b0d97ce1 too.
- Collections (TRUST_JS_PERF_METRICS, --tier=jit): 7, 3, 6 and 15,
  as at 9a, outputs the same.
- Gates (the review's, on the agent's tree):
  - aarch64: lib 2233/0/7, its test list the parent's plus the five
    new tests; clippy clean in the five configurations; rustfmt

    diagnostic; rustdoc's 40 warnings are 9a's.
  - Downstream suites: host 36/0/0, timers 5/0/0, fs (no tests), web
    61/0/1, node 42/0/0, runtime 182/0/1.
  - Fixtures at --tier=jit (no-LTO release): compiles 56/10/160,

  - The fixtures' code dumps: against the reference made at 8f,

  - Test262 at --tier=jit: 53575/53575 at threshold 8 and 53573 of

Step 9c's plan, amended before the step (2026-10-20), from a
read-only survey of b0d97ce1 with counters (an export with counters
added, nothing timed; vue, react and djot and the sustained workload
at --tier=jit, their 31 collections):
- The set of weakly pinned cells is never empty: it held 1 to 614
  cells in every collection (dead objects that inline-cache, name-
  lookup and call pins still name), so skipping the probes when it
  is empty would skip none. Instead the pinned cells are marked in
  their pages' metadata (a bit per small cell, a flag on a large
  one) before the sweep, which tests the bit where it called the
  probe. The probe hashed once per dead cell and zombie: up to
  283,000 times in a collection, 262,000 in the sustained
  workload's steady state.
- The dead set goes as planned. Between the phases, an object is
  dead when its cell, found by its exact start through 9b's table,
  is allocated, unmarked and not a zombie. That answers the engine's
  membership tests (912 to about 6,500 per collection, against
  26,000 to 156,000 inserts into the set). Walks use the list that
  dead_cells returns, or the table when it is the smaller: the weak
  targets (at most 155 entries) are no longer probed once per dead
  object.
- FinalizationRegistry cleanup callbacks may run in another order:
  they followed the hash set's order, and the specification leaves
  the order to the implementation.
- Chunk::call_pins keeps every callee it ever cached until the chunk
  recompiles, so dead callees stay reserved as zombies (djot's grow
  from 0 to 585 objects over its six collections). An open item, not
  9c's.

Step 9c, the sweep stops hashing every dead cell (an implementation
agent's change, reviewed):
- Between a collection's phases the engine's table sweeps asked a
  hash set of every dead object, built from the list Heap::dead_cells
  returns (26,000 to 156,000 inserts per collection on the gate
  workloads). They now take gc_sweep::DeadObjects, which keeps the
  list for walks and answers membership from the heap's bits:
  Heap::is_dead_small_cell finds the page through 9b's table and
  requires the exact start of a cell of the object descriptor that
  is allocated, unmarked and not a zombie. The map of large cells is
  not consulted: an object's cell is never large (a const assertion
  beside the view). Debug builds assert on every query that the heap
  is between the phases of the view's collection (the collecting
  flag and the collection's number), and that the answer is the
  list's.
- The consumers: the host sweep, the template cache, the native
  functions' realms, the ShadowRealm eviction and the finalization
  scheduling before the sweep ask the view. gc_clear_weak_targets
  walks the weak-target table, asking the view about each object
  key, when the table has fewer entries than there are dead objects,
  and otherwise the list (clearing a key the table does not hold
  does nothing). gc_forget_weak_owners chooses its side by the table's
  length, as before. gc_sweep::map and set keep their rule: each dead
  key is removed unless the dead outnumber the table's capacity, and
  then the table is scanned, each key asking the view. The brief had
  asked them to compare the table's length instead, which would scan
  a sparse, large table's buckets for a handful of dead objects; the
  review restored the capacity.
- The scheduling after the sweep drops its dead check, which the
  bits can no longer answer and which was redundant: before the
  sweep gc_forget_weak_owners removed every dead registry from both
  of its tables, after which only a registry still in
  finalization_registries can be readied and no script runs (the
  comment says so). The reclaimed count is the list's length.
- Pins: clear_dead_weak_cells returns the dead targets that weak
  handles still name as a list (a target named by several cells is
  listed as often), and finish_collection records each in its page's
  metadata, a new bitmap PageMeta::pins by exact start (a flag on a
  large cell). The sweep tests the bit where it called the probe,
  keeping a pinned dead cell as a zombie, and clears the bits with
  the marks. An address that does not start an allocated cell
  records nothing: the probe, asked only about allocated cells'
  starts, matched nothing for one either. The bitmap adds a bit per
  cell to a page's metadata, at most 512 bytes (16-byte cells).
- FinalizationRegistry cleanup callbacks may now run in another
  order (they followed the hash set's). No test depends on it: the
  registry tests count or sort their callbacks.
- Left out, as the brief said: Heap::dead_cells still tests three
  bits for each cell (about 356,000 cells in each of the sustained
  workload's collections, the survey counted), where it could test a
  word of each bitmap at a time.
- Tests: between the phases, is_dead_small_cell answers as the list
  for every byte of three pages (live, dead, zombie, free and black-
  allocated cells of two size classes, a page's tail, another
  descriptor's cells), around a dead and a live large cell, for the
  dead cells' tagged words and for addresses outside the heap; pins
  keep a dead small cell and a dead large cell reserved, finalized
  once, through each collection that pins them, and the first one
  after their pins go frees them, while an interior address, a
  tagged word, a free cell's start and a live cell's pin nothing; in
  debug builds a query after the sweep panics. gc_sweep's tests and
  one weak_metadata test build views from lists (from_list, no
  heap). weakly_pinned_dead_cells_stay_reserved_until_unpinned
  passes with only its two finish_collection calls changed (a slice
  of addresses for the closure), as do six other calls in gc_core's
  tests. Nine mutations each made a target test fail.
- Collections (TRUST_JS_PERF_METRICS, --tier=jit): 7, 3, 6 and 15,
  as at 9b, outputs the same.
- Gates (the review's, on the reviewed tree):
  - aarch64: lib 2236/0/7, its test list the parent's plus the three
    new tests; clippy clean in the five configurations; rustfmt
    clean; the workspace builds with all targets without a
    diagnostic; rustdoc's 40 warnings are 9b's. git grep finds no
    dead or pinned set left in gc_collect, gc_handle and
    weak_metadata; gc_sweep's FastSets are the debug builds' copy of
    the list and the test views'.
  - Downstream suites: host 36/0/0, timers 5/0/0, fs (no tests), web
    61/0/1, node 42/0/0, runtime 182/0/1.
  - Fixtures at --tier=jit (no-LTO release): compiles 56/10/160,
    outputs as at S0; difftest 138 agree, 12 skipped, no crash.
  - The fixtures' code dumps: against the reference made at 8f,
    vue's and react's chunks are identical in the plain dumps and
    with the stubs per chunk and inline. djot's differ in 9 of its
    160 chunks (4 inline) and are identical in all three forms to
    the parent's (61ee2a4e) built with 15 codegen units: djot's first
    collection, at 33,785 objects, keeps 6,643 in both and 5,285 in
    the parent's at 16, the two states step 4 recorded, and the last
    keeps 1,305 in each.
  - Test262 at --tier=jit: 53575/53575 at threshold 8 and 53573 of
    53575 at 0, the reports identical to the S5 review fixes'.
  - Per-test GC stress with the verifier and poisoning, aarch64:
    the 1034 tests whose names match the JIT's, the stack's, the
    collector's and the layout probe's areas, the three new among
    them: 1010 pass at interval 1 (6 are ignored), the slow lists' 15
    at 100 and 3 at 1000.
  - x86-64 (zombie): clippy clean; lib 2081/0/7. Windows: clippy as
    its baseline (23 diagnostics); under Wine 2073 pass, 3 fail (step
    1's snapshot codec tests) and 7 are ignored. Both lists are the
    parent's plus the three new tests. Test262 at --tier=jit:
    53575/53575, the report identical to step 1's x86-64 one and the
    S5 exit gate's aarch64 one. Fixtures: compiles 56/10/160 and 7, 3
    and 6 collections, outputs as on aarch64; difftest 138 agree, 12
    skipped, no crash.
  - Per-test GC stress with the verifier and poisoning, x86-64
    (zombie), the whole lib suite, 2088 tests: 2055 pass and 7 are
    ignored at interval 1 (about 3,100 s); the slow lists' 22 pass
    at 100 and their 4 at 1000.
- Measured (gcmeasure.py, as at 9b: fat-LTO engines from fresh
  exports, 8f d95cd4a8e052, 9b 71cf32ad4f59, 9c 16498e4df839 from
  the gated tree, main 311ae9ae5658; copies made together; CPU 5, 15
  interleaved rounds, medians; started once a local inference server
  had used under 1% of a core for a minute, the other cores 0.3 busy
  on average, 1.25 at most; outputs equal everywhere). Idle, the
  server still uses about 0.3% of a core, so the test used at 9b, no
  CPU at all for a minute, never passed.
  - Against 9b: the sustained workload at --tier=jit cycles -8.1%
    (wall -8.1%, instructions -8.4%), GC pause 360 to 281 ms; at
    bytecode cycles -5.8% (wall -5.9%), GC pause 358 to 281 ms. vue,
    react and djot at --tier=jit: cycles -1.3%, -1.7% and -3.6%, GC
    pause -32%, -21% and -34%.
  - Peak RSS against 9b: -0.3% to -1.2% on the fixtures, +1.3% (jit)
    and +1.1% (bytecode) on the sustained workload, 3.0 MB. With
    glibc's mmap threshold fixed (MALLOC_MMAP_THRESHOLD_=131072, 7
    runs each) the difference is 0.5 MB (134.0 against 134.5 MB),
    about what the pin bitmaps take.
  - Against main, 9c's cycles: vue -12.9%, react -14.2%, djot
    -10.4%, the sustained workload -38.6% (jit) and -31.8%
    (bytecode). Peak RSS: vue +36%, react +125%, djot +7%, the
    sustained workload +22% (jit and bytecode). GC pause over wall
    time without the exit collection: vue 3.4%, react 4.6%, djot
    6.3%, the sustained workload 30.3% (jit) and 22.5% (bytecode);
    main's, its exit collection included: 0.8%, 0.3%, 2.8%, 34.7%
    and 28.0%.
  - A lead for peak RSS: the heap takes its 64 KiB pages and its
    large cells from the system allocator (the shell's fastalloc
    passes blocks over 1 KiB through), here glibc's malloc. With its
    mmap threshold fixed as above, which also keeps glibc from
    raising the threshold as large blocks are freed, the sustained
    workload's peak RSS at --tier=jit is 133 MB for 9c against 143
    MB for main (-7%), where by default they are 182 and 150 MB (5
    runs each, medians). vue's is 37.7 against 29.9 MB (+26%, +33%
    by default); react's and djot's do not change.

Step 9 closed (2026-10-20): 9a b0d97ce1, 9b d498e826, the amended
9c plan 61ee2a4e, 9c b72987ab.
- Measured, 8f against 9c (9c's run): the sustained workload at

- S0's bounds on the fixtures against main, at 9c: GC time at most

- Open items carried past step 9, each recorded where it arose:

    8 MiB floor sets the fixtures' heap. Step 10, the nursery, is the
    next lever. A lead (9c): the heap's pages and large cells come
    from glibc's malloc, whose dynamic mmap threshold holds about 49
    MB of the sustained workload's 182 MB peak and 4 MB of vue's.
  - The task boundary's end-of-eval collection marks the whole heap
    (on the sustained workload it reclaims nothing); step 10.
  - Chunk::call_pins keeps every callee it cached until the chunk
    recompiles, so dead callees stay reserved as zombies (9c's
    amended plan).
  - Heap::dead_cells tests three bits for each cell where it could
    test a word of each bitmap at a time (9c).

Step 10 plan (2026-10-20), from two read-only surveys of 443fc5d6
(their reports and briefs in target/s6-step10/surveys, their files
in target/s6-survey10a and s6-survey10b):
the write paths a barrier must cover (compiler passes over scratch
trees, DWARF layouts, the JITs' store emitters, the fixtures'
dumps), and what a nursery would face (no-LTO exports with counters
and phase timers, and memory experiments; 9 interleaved rounds on
CPU 5, the other cores 1.8 busy at the median).
- Where a collection's time goes (default floor, per run):
  - vue 28.9 ms: finalizers and emptied pages 40%, fixed work 31%
    (0.49-1.70 ms per collection), page bit work 15%, marking 12%;
  - react 5.9 ms: 54%, 19%, 14%, 13%;
  - djot 23.3 ms: 42%, 19%, 15%, 22%;
  - the sustained workload 305 ms (jit): marking 61%, finalizers
    25%, page bit work 12%, fixed work 2%.
  Finalizers cost about 32 ns per dead cell (react 61-67 ns), and a
  nursery does not avoid them. The fixed work includes the 912
  builtins' [[Realm]] edges, re-registered at every collection
  (0.13-0.22 ms).
- The full collector at byte floors of 8, 4, 2 and 1 MiB: only vue
  at 4 MiB meets both of S0's bounds. React meets the RSS bound
  only at 1 MiB (19.7 MB against main's 16.2), where its GC share
  is 10.3%; djot meets the RSS bound at every floor and the GC
  bound at none. At 8 MiB react's 21.7 MB over main are 8.8 MB of
  cell pages (120 of its 135 pages spare after the first
  collection), about 12.4 MB of dead objects' Rust-heap payloads,
  freed at that collection and kept by glibc, and 0.7 MB of fixed
  structures (the empty script's peak). The first two scale with
  the budget.
- Survival: at 1 MiB a collection promotes about 30 KB (vue), 50-90
  KB (react) or 150-300 KB (djot), and old cells die at about the
  promotion rate. Old cells holding young cells that die with them
  (nepotism) are negligible.
- Memory: glibc serves each 64 KiB page by posix_memalign from its
  arena, and the alignment slack beside it returns to its free
  lists, which payload churn then spreads into. Pages mapped from
  the OS (an export, one mapping per page) took peak RSS from 183.7
  to 133.8 MB on the sustained workload (main 150.0), from 42.4 to
  37.4 on vue (main 31.0) and from 44.3 to 42.8 on djot (main
  41.8); react's barely moved (37.9 to 37.0). GC pause unchanged;
  wall +4.9% on the sustained workload from 18,000 more page
  faults: a fresh 64 KiB mapping faults 16 times, where glibc's
  heap here faults in 2 MiB huge pages. Mapping large cells too
  changed nothing measurable. A microbenchmark (CPU 5) writing
  1,186 fresh 64 KiB pages, the sustained workload's count: a
  mapping per page 43 ms of faults, 2 MiB chunks 35 ms, the chunks
  advised for huge pages 8 ms and 2 MiB more RSS (transparent huge
  pages are madvise-only here, always on zombie).
- Barrier sites (survey A):
  - 476 production borrow_mut and try_borrow_mut calls whose
    receiver is a Gc or an Env; inherent methods on the handles
    take every one (the downstream crates and TRust have none);
  - three raw writes: the captured-binding store's cache hit
    (store_cap_ic), and a deferred prototype's materialization,
    which during a read writes its lazy cell's state and promotes
    its function's prototype word;
  - generated code: on ARM64 five stores in three emitters (a named
    property's update and creation IC, the name store's global
    property and wide binding, an element append), on x64 one
    emitter (store_value); 916 sites in the fixtures' dumps;
  - host reporters whose values change after creation (napi's
    promise executor).
  Survey B's census: every old cell that held an edge to a young
  cell had been mutably borrowed through its handle since the
  previous collection, at every tier; its probes reach the three
  raw paths. Remembered sets are at most 111 objects and 5
  environments per interval, and most edges sit in array elements.
- What a minor collection traces in full whatever the nursery's
  size: Interp roots, host graphs, interned strings and template
  roots, thread roots, every side-slot owner and Realm member, the
  ephemerons and [[Realm]] edges. An old owner is marked, so its
  conditions fire at once: none of them needs a barrier.
- Step 10 runs as four commits, each measured against its parent
  and main (fat LTO, gcmeasure.py):
  - 10a: the heap maps its pages from the OS. On Linux it maps 2
    MiB-aligned chunks, advises them for huge pages and carves 64
    KiB pages from them; a page trimmed from the spare pool returns
    its memory with MADV_DONTNEED and stays in its chunk for reuse.
    Elsewhere a page is a mapping of its own (Windows' allocation
    granularity is 64 KiB; macOS maps twice the size and unmaps the
    ends). Large cells stay with the allocator.
  - 10b: marks persist and a write barrier records old cells as
    they are written; every collection is still major, and clears
    the marks before it marks. Pages of objects, environments and
    deferred prototypes end in a 512-byte card table, a byte per
    128 bytes of the page (338, 508 and 1354 cells a page instead
    of 341, 512 and 1365); the barrier stores to the card of a
    cell's start, computed from its address alone, with no load or
    branch. Its sites: inherent Gc::borrow_mut, Gc::try_borrow_mut
    and Env::borrow_mut, the three raw writes, the generated stores
    (the owner reloaded where no register holds it), and a
    reporter that declares changing values, whose function is then
    traced at every collection as a side-slot owner. Debug builds
    check when a collection starts, before the marks clear, that no
    marked cell with a clean card holds an edge to an unmarked
    cell; GC stress runs the check at every safepoint. Generated
    code changes by the barrier's instructions alone.
  - 10c: minor collections. A minor collection marks from the
    roots, the side slots, the ephemerons, the native stack and the
    marked cells whose cards are set, traces no other old cell,
    sweeps only the pages that received cells since the last
    collection, lists the dead objects from those pages, and leaves
    its marks set: a cell is old once any collection has marked it.
    A minor collection is due when the bytes allocated since the
    last collection pass the nursery budget
    (TRUST_JS_GC_NURSERY_BYTES; the default chosen by measuring 1,
    2 and 4 MiB); a major one when old bytes (live after the last
    major collection plus those promoted since) pass today's byte
    budget, max(8 MiB, twice the live bytes after the last major
    collection). Explicit, idle and host requests, ArrayBuffer
    pressure and the live-object limit (before its RangeError)
    collect majorly; the task boundary's collection becomes minor,
    on its growth and churn conditions. GC stress makes every Nth
    stress collection major. The spare pool keeps what the nursery
    can fill, at least 16 pages (64 today). The performance record
    and TRUST_JS_GC_LOG tell minor collections from major ones. The
    in-cell weak callbacks (WeakFn, WeakSink) go: no engine
    descriptor has one, and a minor collection would never visit
    an old holder's weak slots (8c's open item).
  - 10d: a minor collection's fixed work is restricted to what
    changed since the last collection: the [[Realm]] edges of the
    functions registered since then, and the [[Realm]] table swept
    from the dead list. What else 10c's measurement shows is
    recorded before the work.
- Weak handles, weak_metadata, FinalizationRegistry cleanup and the
  table sweeps keep their rule, unmarked is dead; in a minor
  collection only young cells can be unmarked, so an old dead cell
  waits for a major collection.
- Not in step 10:
  - Element-range dirtiness: a dirty old array is rescanned whole.
    The sustained workload writes its 200,000-element array only
    in each round's replacement loop, so about once per round.
  - Cheaper finalization: step 12's replacement of Object's
    Rc<GcState> and the slot registry, which every object's
    finalizer updates.
  - The host API: HostGcVisitor::is_minor stays false, since host
    graphs are reported in full at every collection; step 11
    decides (TRust traces only young state when it is true).
- Dumps: 10a and 10d change no emitted code, and 10c none beyond
  10b's. Their dumps must be the parent's, djot's GC-timing-
  sensitive chunks excepted as at step 4. 10b's must be the
  parent's with exactly the barrier's instructions added.

Step 10a, the heap maps its pages from the OS (an implementation
agent's change, reviewed):
- A page source owned by the heap (gc_core::page_source) gives the
  heap its 64 KiB pages and takes back those the spare pool trims.
  On Linux it maps 2 MiB chunks at 2 MiB boundaries (4 MiB mapped,
  the ends unmapped), advises them for transparent huge pages and
  carves its pages from them: the current chunk's next page, else
  the page given back last, else a new chunk's first (a chunk a
  huge page backs is resident whole from its first touch, so its
  later pages take no fault). A page given back is discarded
  (MADV_DONTNEED; by hand if the system refuses) and stays in its
  chunk. Chunks are unmapped with the heap; debug builds check that
  its release accounts for every page. On macOS a page is a mapping
  of its own (128 KiB mapped, the ends unmapped), on Windows a
  VirtualAlloc of 64 KiB, its alignment asserted; other targets
  keep the allocator. The spare pool and large cells are unchanged.
- Test pages_are_aligned_and_zeroed_when_new_reused_or_taken_again:
  100 pages filled, emptied (the pool keeps 64 and gives 36 back)
  and refilled, every page aligned and zeroed; on Linux four chunks
  throughout, a fifth only once no page given back is left; nothing
  mapped after the heap's release. Five mutations each made it
  fail. The macOS and allocator paths, forced onto Linux in a
  scratch export, pass gc_core's 46 tests.
- Collections (TRUST_JS_PERF_METRICS, --tier=jit): 7, 3, 6 and 15,
  outputs the same. TRUST_JS_GC_LOG shows retention moving by a few
  objects, the same on every run of a binary (the sustained workload
  keeps 201,027 or 201,029 objects where 9c kept 201,030 or 201,032;
  djot up to 241 fewer cells): stale stack words now resolve against
  other addresses.
- Gates (the review's, on the agent's tree):
  - aarch64: lib 2237/0/7, its test list the parent's plus the new
    test; clippy clean in the five configurations; rustfmt clean;
    the workspace builds with all targets without a diagnostic;
    rustdoc's 40 warnings are 9c's. git grep finds no call of
    page_layout left; the allocator serves pages only on other
    targets, and large cells.
  - Downstream suites: host 36/0/0, timers 5/0/0, fs (no tests), web
    61/0/1, node 42/0/0, runtime 182/0/1.
  - Fixtures at --tier=jit (no-LTO release): compiles 56/10/160,
    outputs as at S0; difftest 138 agree, 12 skipped, no crash. The
    sustained workload at --tier=jit and bytecode: 15 collections
    (13 by allocation, the task boundary's, the exit's), its output
    9c's.
  - The fixtures' code dumps: against the reference made at 8f,
    vue's and react's chunks are identical in the plain dumps and
    with the stubs per chunk and inline; djot's differ in the 9
    chunks (4 inline) that differed at 9c, and in all three forms
    its chunks are 9c's review dumps'.
  - Test262 at --tier=jit: 53575/53575 at threshold 8 and 53573 of
    53575 at 0, the reports identical to the S5 review fixes'.
  - Per-test GC stress with the verifier and poisoning, aarch64:
    the 1035 tests whose names match the JIT's, the stack's, the
    collector's and the layout probe's areas, the new one among
    them: 1011 pass at interval 1 (6 are ignored), the slow lists'
    15 at 100 and 3 at 1000.
  - x86-64 (zombie): clippy clean; lib 2082/0/7. Windows: clippy as
    its baseline (23 diagnostics); under Wine 2074 pass, 3 fail (step
    1's snapshot codec tests) and 7 are ignored. Both lists are the
    parent's plus the new test. Test262 at --tier=jit: 53575/53575,
    the report identical to step 1's x86-64 one and the S5 exit
    gate's aarch64 one. Fixtures: compiles 56/10/160 and 7, 3 and 6
    collections, outputs as on aarch64; difftest 138 agree, 12
    skipped, no crash.
  - Per-test GC stress with the verifier and poisoning, x86-64
    (zombie), the whole lib suite, 2089 tests: 2056 pass and 7 are
    ignored at interval 1 (about 3,100 s); the slow lists' 22 pass
    at 100 and their 4 at 1000.
- Measured (gcmeasure.py; fat-LTO engines from fresh exports, 9c
  16498e4df839, 10a a9efdaf98d41 from the gated tree, main
  311ae9ae5658; copies made together; CPU 5, 15 interleaved rounds,
  medians; the empty script as a control; started once a local
  inference server had used under 1% of a core for a minute, the
  other cores 0.48 busy (median), 2.59 at most; outputs equal
  everywhere):
  - Against 9c: peak RSS on the sustained workload 183.5 to 136.9
    MB at --tier=jit (-25.4%) and 181.9 to 136.4 at bytecode
    (-25.0%), vue 43.1 to 40.3 (-6.6%), djot 44.7 to 44.8 (+0.3%),
    react 37.6 to 39.3 (+4.4%), the empty script 16.0 to 17.5
    (+9.4%). Minor faults -31% and -35% on the sustained workload,
    -12% to -22% on the fixtures. Cycles -0.1% to -0.9% (wall -0.3%
    to -1.2%), GC pause 0.3% to 2.9% shorter.
  - The small heaps' 1.5 MB: idle (a script that spins for 1.5 s,
    smaps_rollup read at 0.8 s, 5 runs each) the engine holds 5.8
    MB of anonymous memory against 9c's 4.3 and main's 4.0. The
    heap's first chunk is backed by a huge page, so all 2 MiB of it
    is resident from its first touch, whatever the heap uses.
  - Against main, 10a's cycles: vue -13.0%, react -14.5%, djot
    -10.9%, the sustained workload -38.8% (jit) and -32.0%
    (bytecode). Peak RSS: vue +27%, react +141%, djot +9%, the
    sustained workload -8.7% (jit) and -8.5% (bytecode), the empty
    script +17%. GC pause over wall time without the exit
    collection: vue 3.4%, react 4.6%, djot 6.4%, the sustained
    workload 30.5% (jit) and 22.7% (bytecode); main's, its exit
    collection included: 0.8%, 0.3%, 2.8%, 34.9% and 28.2%.
- Open, for 10c: the huge page that backs the heap's newest chunk
  holds the pages not taken yet, up to 2 MiB, which a small heap
  pays in full. Whether a chunk is advised only once the heap holds
  several is decided with 10c's measurement of the nursery, and
  recorded before the work.
- Plan amendment for 10b, before its work: a host reporter whose
  values may change after its function's creation has the
  function's card set when each collection starts, so the function
  is rescanned at every collection, rather than being traced as a
  side-slot owner: the same effect, without exempting it from the
  barrier's check. The reporter trait's new provided method says
  whether the values are fixed; its default, for embedders, says
  they may change.

Step 10b, marks persist and a write barrier records old cells as
they are written (an implementation agent's change, reviewed):
- Marks persist: the sweep keeps small and large cells' marks and
  clears only waiters and pins, so between collections a marked
  cell is old (it survived the last collection) and an allocated
  unmarked one young. A collection starts in Heap::begin_marking,
  out of line: debug builds check the barrier, then every mark and
  every card is cleared. dead_cells, is_dead_small_cell and
  is_dead_target assert in debug builds that a collection is in
  progress; only the check reads the last collection's marks.
- Card tables: LayoutDesc::carded is set for objects, environments
  and deferred prototypes. Their pages end in a 512-byte table, a
  byte per 128 bytes of the page, and hold 338, 508 and 1,354 cells
  of 192, 128 and 48 bytes; locate and is_dead_small_cell already
  bounded the cell index, so no address in a page's tail or table
  resolves to a cell. gc_core::card_mark sets the card of a cell's
  start from its address alone, at base + 65,024 + ((addr >> 7) &
  511): no lookup, load or branch.
- The barrier's sites: the handles' own borrow_mut and
  try_borrow_mut (Gc, Env) set the card before borrowing (no
  collection runs while a borrow is outstanding); store_cap_ic, the
  deferred prototype's promotion in PackedValue::unpack (the
  function's card) and its materialization (its own cell's) set it
  just before their stores. ARM64, one emitter of four
  instructions (ubfx, orr #0xffff, sub #511, strb with a register
  offset, storing 0xff): sites A, B and E have the owner in x10; C
  reloads the realm's global object (a name cache) or the prepared
  Reference's object; D reloads the binding's environment, by the
  name cache's mode the running one, its parent or the deep holder
  len - 1 parents up (20 words with the card), or the Reference's
  scope. x64's store_value computes the card's address from R9
  before decode_rhs overwrites it, keeps it in the frame's spare
  word and sets the card after the store (7 instructions).
- After begin_marking a collection that scans the native stack
  zeroes 16 KiB below its frame (256 KiB in debug builds) with the
  existing scrub_stack: begin_marking's frames held cells' and
  pages' addresses, which the scan's frames would reuse.
- Host reporters: NativeCallableRetained::reports_fixed_values,
  false by default, its contract documented with the trait.
  RandomStream and napi's callback and constructor closures
  declare their values fixed, and so, a review change, does
  Error.captureStackTrace's getter: its frames' names are captured
  before it is made, and it only builds the formatted lines, a Rust
  string, later. The plan's amendment named it among the changing
  reporters; napi's promise executor is the workspace's only one
  left. Functions with a changing reporter are listed in
  GcState::rescanned_natives (an Interp field would change the
  memory inventory's count), registered by make_native_callable and
  the super() graft, evicted between the phases of the collection
  that finds them dead, and carded as each collection starts,
  before the check.
- The check (debug builds): every marked small cell with a clean
  card, and every marked large cell, is traced; an edge to an
  allocated, unmarked cell panics, naming both cells. A failed
  check in a collection called from compiled code aborts the
  process, since nothing unwinds out of compiled frames, so the
  tests make their last collection from Rust.
- Tests: 9 in gc_core (the tables' geometry, no cell in a table,
  card_mark's one byte, cards clear as a collection starts, a spare
  page reused for another kind, survivors marked until the next
  collection, the readers' assertion, the check's panic and its
  passing side); 19 engine tests in write_barrier_tests.rs, 12 of
  them ARM64's (sites A-E, each name-cache mode of D, each kind of
  Reference) and the review's captured_stack_getter_is_not_rescanned
  among the 7 that run everywhere; on x86-64,
  native_name_stores_record_their_owners_in_cards (seven kinds of
  store, each counted as native). No existing test changed. 28
  mutations in an export each made a target test fail: every
  site's card removed, C's and D's owners reloaded wrongly, the
  reporters' registration, eviction and cards, the check, the
  sweep keeping the marks, the cards' clearing, the tables (D's
  Reference mutation after one test change). The review's mutation
  of x64's store_value, its card store removed, built on zombie in
  a target dir of its own: the x64 test failed, the check naming an
  old object with a clean card.
- Collections (TRUST_JS_PERF_METRICS, --tier=jit): 7, 3, 6 and 15,
  outputs the same.
- Gates (the review's, on the reviewed tree):
  - aarch64: lib 2265/0/7, its test list the parent's plus the 28
    new tests; clippy clean in the five configurations; rustfmt
    clean; the workspace builds with all targets without a
    diagnostic; rustdoc's 40 warnings are 10a's.
  - Downstream suites: host 36/0/0, timers 5/0/0, fs (no tests), web
    61/0/1, node 42/0/0, runtime 182/0/1.
  - Fixtures at --tier=jit (no-LTO release): compiles 56/10/160,
    outputs as at S0; difftest 138 agree, 12 skipped, no crash. The
    sustained workload at --tier=jit and bytecode: 15 collections,
    its output 10a's.
  - The fixtures' code dumps (dumpcmp-10b.py): with the card
    sequences dropped, every chunk is 10a's in the plain dumps and
    with the stubs per chunk and inline, but djot's 9 GC-timing-
    sensitive chunks (4 inline), which are the 8f reference's, the
    other retention state. Sequences dropped: vue A 44, B 40, C 19,
    D 19, E 4; react E 10; djot A 366, B 366, C 24, D 24.
  - Test262 at --tier=jit: 53575/53575 at threshold 8 and 53573 of
    53575 at 0, the reports identical to the S5 review fixes'.

    live; also in one of the agent's four runs) is a retention flake
    that 10a made frequent, not 10b. Under Wine the realm tests'
    module, run 15 times each, fails in 1 run at 9c (a sibling
    test's dropped Realm live), in 6 at 10a and in 6 at 10b, and in
    none with 10b's tree taking Windows pages from the allocator as
    before 10a; on x86-64 Linux 30 of 30 runs pass. Probably address
    reuse: with a VirtualAlloc for each page, a released heap's
    page addresses go straight to the next heap, so stale stack
    words from earlier work resolve to new cells (not traced
    further). The next commit gives Windows its pages from the
    allocator again.

Step 10a on Windows, amended after 10b (the review's change): Windows
takes its GC pages from the allocator again, as before 10a, instead
of a VirtualAlloc of each page.
- Why: the dropped-Realm retention flake under Wine (10b's entry)
  became frequent with 10a's VirtualAlloc of each page, and nothing
  measured on Windows asked for pages from the OS: 10a's lever was
  glibc's alignment slack. macOS keeps its mapping of each page
  (no macOS test host).
- Gates: zombie-check.sh: x86-64 clippy clean, Windows clippy
  as its baseline (23 diagnostics); lib suites: x86-64 2099/0/7;
  Windows under Wine 2091 pass, 3 fail (step 1's snapshot codec
  tests) and 7 are ignored; the realm tests' module under Wine
  passes in 15 runs of 15 (10b: 9). Linux builds do not compile
  the change; rustfmt clean.

Gates tiered (2026-10-21, the owner's decision, for the rest of the
program): a commit's full gates took about two and a half hours,
four fifths of it per-test GC stress (10b: aarch64 2 h 18 min, 113
of them stress, 38 of those for the 30 tests of the slow lists;
zombie about 1 h 50 min, 75 of them stress), and 10b's agent 8.2
hours, mostly its own stress runs and mutation rebuilds. From 10c's
review on (the plan's working agreements):
- Each commit's gate, about half an hour with the two machines in
  parallel: aarch64 as before, but Test262 at threshold 8 only and
  per-test GC stress at interval 1 only for a targeted list: the
  commit's new tests, the tests of the modules it changes and the
  tests named for the collector (gc_, weak, finaliz, rooting: 178
  at 10b); zombie its compile checks, its x86-64 and Windows lib
  suites, its test lists and its release checks (fixtures and
  difftest).
- At each step's close and the stage's exit gate: the whole lib
  suite under GC stress on both machines, the slow lists at their
  intervals, Test262 at threshold 0 and Test262 on x86-64.
- Implementation agents run targeted stress only and mutation-check
  only the key sites; the review's gate covers the rest.
- The risk accepted: a rooting bug outside a commit's targeted
  tests shows at the next milestone, to be found among the step's
  commits, rather than before the commit. The debug builds' barrier
  check and verifier still run in every lib test's collections.

Step 10c, minor collections (an implementation agent's change,
reviewed):
- A minor collection keeps the marks. As it starts (begin_marking),
  after debug builds' checks of the barrier and of the young pages'
  bookkeeping, it lists every marked, non-zombie cell that starts in
  a set card (a card can hold the starts of three 48-byte cells) and
  clears those cards; marking traces them first, then the roots,
  side slots, ephemerons and native stack as a major collection
  does, and no other old cell. It sweeps only the pages that
  received cells since the last collection (a page's young flag and
  the heap's list, set where a cell is taken from a free list or a
  page is made) and the large cells, puts the young pages with free
  cells back on their classes' lists, and leaves the marks set.
  dead_cells walks the same pages; pins are recorded only where
  the sweep goes. Every sweep clears the young flags, and the heap's
  bytes after it are what was allocated less what it freed (checked
  against a page walk in debug builds). A major collection works as
  before and clears the flags too.
- Triggers: a minor collection is due once the bytes allocated since
  the last collection pass the nursery budget,
  TRUST_JS_GC_NURSERY_BYTES (2 MiB by default; 0 turns minor
  collections off); a major one once the heap holds more than the
  byte budget after a minor collection (max(the 8 MiB floor, twice
  what the last major collection left), its old cells counted live
  or not), or when a major request was deferred. The due word stays
  the one flag generated code polls; Heap::due_kind chooses at the
  safepoint. Under GC stress every Mth collection is major
  (TRUST_JS_GC_STRESS_MAJOR, 8 by default). gc_core's own heaps have
  no nursery unless asked, so its earlier tests run as before.
- By cause: allocation safepoints, the JIT's polls and the task
  boundary collect minorly unless a major collection is due; explicit
  requests (gc_collect, $262.gc, collect_garbage_for_host, the idle
  and exit collections, unstable_collect_young_for_host_tests),
  ArrayBuffer pressure and the live-object ceiling majorly. The
  ArrayBuffer pressure counts data since the last major collection,
  since minor ones leave old dead buffers. Interp::gc_collect_minor
  exists for tests.
- A review change: release_host_job_context, which asks for a
  collection at the next task boundary to reclaim a destroyed
  Document's graph without waiting for allocation pressure, now also
  asks the heap for a major collection (Heap::request_major: the
  next collection of any cause is major, none is made due). The
  agent's tree collected minorly there, so the retired Realm, old,
  stayed until a major collection, and had changed the test to
  expect that; the test is back to its property, the boundary
  reclaiming the Realm, and asserts the collection was major.
- The spare pool keeps the nursery's pages (32 at 2 MiB), at least
  16.
- The in-cell weak callbacks are gone: WeakFn, WeakSink,
  LayoutDesc::weak, CondEdge::slots, visit_weak, clear_dead_weak,
  the marker's three fields, every descriptor's weak field and
  gc_core's four tests of them. Weak handles, weak_metadata and the
  table sweeps keep their rule (unmarked is dead); in a minor
  collection only young cells can be unmarked.
- The performance record adds gc_kinds and
  gc_pause_seconds_by_kind; TRUST_JS_GC_LOG names the kind, and a
  minor collection's line adds the young pages it swept, the cells
  it traced from cards and the bytes it marked (promoted).
  DIAGNOSTICS.md documents both variables and the floor's new
  meaning.
- HostGcVisitor::is_minor stays false; host graphs are reported in
  full at every collection.
- Tests: 11 in gc_core (minor collections free young garbage and
  keep old cells they never trace, a card's old cell keeps a young
  one and the check panics without the card, only young pages are
  swept, only young cells are found dead, zombies on swept pages,
  promotion, the due logic, the stress period, the spare cap, the
  review's request_major), 7 engine tests in
  minor_collection_tests.rs (allocation safepoints minor until a
  major collection is due, young garbage freed and old objects
  kept, weak targets dying young at minor collections and old at
  major ones, the task boundary, explicit majors, the live-object
  limit's major collection, GC stress's major period). Changed, the
  property kept: five direct start_marking calls name the kind;
  a_spent_byte_budget_collects_within_one_poll spends a nursery;
  operand_temporaries_survive_helper_end_polls makes every stress
  collection major (a minor one would not mark an operand that is
  old already); object_count_alone_does_not_collect raises and
  restores the nursery with the floor; two task-boundary tests use
  a 16 MiB nursery so the task's objects are young at the boundary.
  Ten mutations at the key sites (the card scan, the young pages,
  promotion, the due logic) each made a target test fail.
- The agent's measurement (no-LTO, on a machine an unrelated build
  kept busy, 19 cores on average: counts and peak RSS hold, times
  are inflated): at a 2 MiB nursery vue makes 20 minor and 1 major
  collections, react 5 and 1, djot 15 and 1 (each fixture's major
  is the exit collection), the sustained workload 190 and 4; peak
  RSS against the parent's vue 38.0 to 30.4 MB, react 37.5 to 22.1,
  djot 43.8 to 41.2, the sustained workload 135.5 to 83.3, while GC
  pause grows on every workload (vue 28.5 to 38.2 ms, djot 22.8 to
  35.7, the sustained workload 286 to 385); at 1 MiB pause grows
  more, at 4 MiB it is near the parent's with peak RSS 34.0, 27.7,
  42.5 and 84.0 MB. Each minor collection still pays the
  collection's fixed work (10d) and its finalizers, and on the
  sustained workload each steady-state minor collection rescans the
  carded 200,000-element array (Heap::mark 8% of cycles, the card
  scan 2.6%). djot never runs a major collection before its exit
  collection: its old cells stay under the 8 MiB floor.
- 10a's open item, answered: without the huge-page advice (a
  scratch export) peak RSS stays within the runs' spread at the 2
  MiB nursery (the empty script 15.6 against 16.1 MB), while minor
  faults rise 24% on the empty script, 50-57% on the fixtures and
  4.9 times on the sustained workload. The advice stays.
- Dumps: vue's and react's are 10b's in all three forms; djot's
  differ from 10b's in the 9 GC-timing-sensitive chunks (5 inline),

  of djot's 160 chunks are 10b's.

Step 10d plan (2026-10-21), from 10c's measurement:
- Where minor collections cost time. 10c's added GC pause per added
  collection, against 10b (fat LTO): vue about 0.4 ms (14 more
  collections, +6.0 ms), djot about 0.8 ms (10 more, +7.7 ms),
  react about 0.1 ms; survey B2 had put a collection's fixed work
  at 0.3 to 1.7 ms (vue's side-slot owners up to 0.8 ms for 2,883
  owners, the 912 builtins' [[Realm]] ephemerons 0.13 to 0.22 ms,
  client roots, table sweeps). A flat profile of 10c (no-LTO,
  user cycles, call graphs not permitted here) puts the fixtures'
  GC-named functions at 2.5 to 5% of cycles, the client's own
  functions at 0.1 to 0.2%: the fixed work is spread through
  marking, edge registration and hash lookups. On the sustained
  workload marking is 16% of cycles, tracing 4% and the card scan
  2%: each minor collection rescans the carded 200,000-element
  array, every one, not about once per round as the step 10 plan
  had it.
- 10d restricts a minor collection's fixed work to what changed
  since the last collection, each restriction checked in debug
  builds as each collection starts, as the barrier is:
  - the [[Realm]] ephemerons of the native functions: in a minor
    collection only those registered since the last collection;
    the [[Realm]] table swept from the dead list;
  - side-slot owners (the 11 tables of gc_side_slot_tables! and
    the generators): in a minor collection only owners that are
    young or whose entries were written since the last collection,
    which the tables record as they are written (an old, clean
    owner's values are old: the last collection traced them);
  - WeakMap and WeakSet entries (weak_collection_data): in a minor
    collection only entries written since the last collection or
    whose keys are young;
  - the nursery's default, chosen by 10d's measurement at 1, 2 and
    4 MiB.
- Not in 10d: element-range dirtiness (an open item now, its
  premise corrected: the sustained workload's minor collections
  each rescan the array; its elements live outside the cell, so a
  card cannot name a range of them); a per-page summary of the
  cards (the scan reads every carded page's 512-byte table, 2% of
  the sustained workload's cycles); cheaper finalization (step
  12); the client's roots, which every collection reports.

Step 10d, a minor collection's fixed work restricted (an
implementation agent's change, reviewed):
- The records: ClientRecords in the heap's GcState (one per
  ShadowRealm tree) holds what the tables wrote since the last
  collection: side-slot owners, WeakMap and WeakSet entries by
  (collection, key), and the native functions registered in the
  [[Realm]] table. The tables are #[repr(transparent)] newtypes
  over their maps, so Interp's layout, whose field offsets
  generated code embeds, is unchanged (the dumps are 10c's): reads
  go through Deref; every insert or mutable access takes the heap
  and records, and with no DerefMut an unrecorded write does not
  compile; removals record nothing. A collection consumes the
  records once its marking has run, so writes between the phases
  and after the sweep are kept for the next one; a deferred
  collection consumes nothing.
- A minor collection reports only what was recorded: the side-slot
  owners of the 11 tables and the generators (Realm members,
  ShadowRealm owners and host owners as before), the recorded
  WeakMap and WeakSet entries as ephemerons, and the [[Realm]]
  edges of the functions registered since the last collection;
  its [[Realm]] table sweep asks the dead list about those
  functions, or walks the dead list when that is shorter. A major
  collection reports everything, as before.
- A deviation from the brief, kept: the brief said "young or
  recorded"; a young owner, key or built-in was necessarily written
  after it was allocated, so it is recorded, and finding young ones
  otherwise would walk the tables, the cost removed. The debug
  check is the stronger for it: MarkClient::check_records runs in
  begin_marking after the barrier's and the young pages' checks,
  reads the last collection's marks (Heap::cell_age), and asserts
  that every young owner, key and built-in is recorded, that an
  unrecorded old owner's side slots hold only marked cells, that an
  unrecorded entry under an old key has a marked value, and that an
  old built-in not registered since has a marked [[Realm]]; a panic
  names the table, the owner and the value. A write through a
  table value's interior mutability would escape the types; the
  review found none (the values are plain data, Values and handles
  whose cells have their own cards), and generators leave their
  table while they run and are inserted, recorded, as they
  suspend.
- Tests: 12 in table_record_tests.rs: the client's report (a
  test-only ClientReport) names the written and young entries and
  none of the old, clean ones, for each restriction; young values
  survive minor collections through old entries alone (an old
  promise's reaction, an old generator's sent value, an old
  registry's held value, at every tier; each of the 12 tables
  written from Rust; an old WeakMap key's value; a young built-in's
  [[Realm]]); dead young built-ins' [[Realm]] entries leave at a
  minor collection, dead old ones at the next major; five
  should_panic tests bypass a record. Five call sites pass the heap
  (memory.rs, prepared_reference_own_tests.rs), their assertions
  unchanged. Eight mutations (each record removed, each check
  disabled, the record never consumed, the minor [[Realm]] sweep
  removed) each made a target test fail.
- The agent's measurement (no-LTO, 5 rounds, the other cores 0.46
  busy at the median): collections and outputs the parent's; GC
  pause at the 2 MiB nursery vue 31.6 to 28.9 ms, react 5.7 to 5.4,
  djot 27.4 to 26.2, while the sustained workload, which writes no
  side table, took 6% more GC pause in that build: code generation
  in the 16-unit no-LTO build (a property lookup's closure no longer
  inlined, the trace callbacks dearer per call, the same cells
  traced). Built with fat LTO the tree ran fewer instructions on
  every workload.
- Gates (the review's: step 10's close, the tiered gates'
  milestone, on the reviewed tree):
  - aarch64: lib 2291/0/7, its test list the parent's plus the 12
    new tests; clippy clean in the five configurations; rustfmt
    clean; rustdoc's 40 warnings are 10c's; the workspace builds
    with all targets without a diagnostic.
  - Downstream suites: host 36/0/0, timers 5/0/0, fs (no tests), web
    61/0/1, node 42/0/0, runtime 182/0/1.
  - Fixtures at --tier=jit (no-LTO release): compiles 56/10/160,
    outputs as at S0, 21, 6 and 16 collections; difftest 138 agree,
    12 skipped, no crash. The sustained workload at --tier=jit and
    bytecode: 194 collections, its output 10c's.
  - Dumps: identical to 10c's in all three forms, djot's included.
  - Test262 at --tier=jit: 53575/53575 at threshold 8 and 53573 of
    53575 at 0, the reports identical to the S5 review fixes'.
  - Per-test GC stress with the verifier, poisoning and the
    barrier's and the records' checks, aarch64, the whole lib suite
    (2298 tests): 2261 pass at interval 1 (7 are ignored), the slow
    lists' 25 at 100 and 5 at 1000.
  - x86-64 (zombie): clippy clean; lib 2125/0/7. Windows: clippy as
    its baseline (23 diagnostics); under Wine 2117 pass, 3 fail
    (step 1's snapshot codec tests) and 7 are ignored. Both lists
    are the parent's plus the 12 new tests. Test262 at --tier=jit:
    53575/53575, the report identical to step 1's x86-64 one and the
    S5 exit gate's aarch64 one. Fixtures: compiles 56/10/160 and 21,
    6 and 16 collections, outputs as on aarch64; difftest 138 agree,
    12 skipped, no crash.
  - Per-test GC stress, x86-64 (zombie), the whole lib suite, 2132
    tests: 2099 pass and 7 are ignored at interval 1 (2,352 s; 3,792
    at 10b, when every collection was major); the slow lists' 22
    pass at 100 and their 4 at 1000.
- Measured (gcmeasure.py; fat-LTO engines from fresh exports, 10c
  2a4c5ed657a2, 10d 878d81371e7e from the gated tree at 1, 2 and 4 MiB
  nurseries, main 311ae9ae5658; copies made together; CPU 5, 15
  interleaved rounds, medians; the empty script as a control;
  started once a local inference server had used under 1% of a core
  and all CPUs under one core for a minute, the other cores 0.37

  - Against 10c at the 2 MiB nursery: GC pause vue -9.3%, react
    -6.9%, djot -5.5%, the sustained workload -3.5% (jit and
    bytecode); cycles -0.6% to -1.7% (react +0.3%); peak RSS within

  - The nursery's size, against 10c at 2 MiB: at 1 MiB peak RSS

  - Against main, at 2 MiB: cycles vue -12.1%, react -14.6%, djot
    -8.3%, the sustained workload -33.3% (jit) and -27.4%
    (bytecode); peak RSS vue +3.8%, react +48%, djot +3.4%, the
    sustained workload -43%; GC pause over wall time without the
    exit collection vue 3.8%, react 4.6%, djot 7.8%, the sustained
    workload 35.6% (jit) and 27.0% (bytecode); main's, its exit
    collection included, 0.8%, 0.3%, 2.9%, 34.8% and 28.3%.
