    task boundary's collection is minor, and the in-cell weak
    callbacks are gone.

Step 11 plan (2026-10-21), from a read-only survey of 5a4a1560
(target/s6-survey11/report.md) and the owner's decisions:
- The owner: step 11 replaces the embed API's storable Value outright,
  with no deprecated compatibility layer, and migrates this
  repository's users. TRust builds from an old trust-js commit
  (e3310993 plus a patch, written against the pre-S2 host API) and
  is ported after the merge review; it constrains nothing here.
- What the survey found: the embed API hands out raw cell handles.
  Value is a public Clone enum whose cell variants carry Gc, which is
  pub, Copy and derefs to RefCell<Object>; a Value kept past its
  Engine crashes, another Engine accepts it unchecked, and one kept
  in a Vec across a major collection names another object at the
  reused address. Rust storage is kept alive only through HostTrace
  reports (OpState::put and ResourceTable::add require it), reporters
  and the stack scan. This repository's 13 HostTrace impls only root
  values; N-API's references (a weak-strong flip), its wraps (weak
  identity) and wasm's host functions need weak handles. Six Vec
  builders in node and web hold values across [[Set]], which can
  collect; N-API references at count 0 stay strong, so wrapped
  objects never die, and N-API finalizers never run.
- Step 11 runs as two commits:
  - 11a, rooted handles: a handle table in each heap's GcState
    (shared by a ShadowRealm tree): counted strong and weak slots,
    a free list, the heap's id, never reused, and a flag the heap's
    release clears, after which every handle is inert. Handle and
    WeakHandle hold the table and a slot, !Send and !Sync; using
    one with another Engine is an error. A major collection traces
    every strong slot; a minor one only the slots written since the
    last collection, recorded as 10d's tables are, checked in debug
    builds; weak slots are cleared between a collection's phases.
    Every value this repository stores moves to handles (the 13
    HostTrace impls, the runtime's duplicate and batch rooting,
    N-API's references and wraps with Node-API's count semantics),
    OpState and ResourceTable stop requiring HostTrace, and the six
    builders root their values. Value itself does not change yet.
  - 11b, the scoped call API: values in the embed API carry a
    generative lifetime (Value<'s>, Ctx<'s>, engine.scope(|cx| ...),
    natives over for<'s>), so none can be stored or cross a scope or
    an Engine; strings and BigInts are made through the scope; Gc,
    WeakGc, Object and Abrupt leave the public API. The host traits
    lose what nothing uses (internal, supports_native, is_minor,
    native_old_generation, unstable_collect_young_for_host_tests,
    HostGc::sweep_gc); HostTrace's endpoints take weak handles;
    NativeCallableRetained no longer reports values (closures keep
    them as engine captures), which retires 10b's rescanned
    natives; RealmEntry becomes a token. About 420 signatures in
    this repository's crates move with it.
- Not in step 11: TRust's port; minor-aware host tracing (hosts
  report in full at every collection); a Vec<Value<'s>> held across
  a safepoint stays a hole that rooted lists and GC stress guard.

Step 11a, rooted handles (an implementation agent's change,
reviewed):
- The table (handles.rs): one per heap's GcState, shared by a
  ShadowRealm tree: entries of a value, a handle count, a strength
  and a recorded flag, a free list, the heap's id (a global counter,
  never reused), an alive flag and a collecting flag. An entry made,
  given a new value or made strong again is recorded; the records
  are consumed right after 10d's, once the marking has run, and
  kept for that collection's weak clearing.
- The API (embed): Handle, WeakHandle and CountedHandle (N-API's
  references: strong while the count is above 0, weak at 0, flipped
  in place), each the table and an entry, so !Send and !Sync; made
  and read through a HeapOwner (Ctx or Engine, sealed). A value
  whose cell is not allocated in the owner's heap is refused
  (Foreign), a handle read through another heap's owner too, one
  whose heap was released answers Released, and a write while a
  collection runs is refused (Collecting; debug builds panic).
- Collections: a major collection reports every strong entry as a
  root, a minor one the recorded strong entries; weak entries whose
  targets died are cleared between the phases, right after the dead
  list is built and before the host sweeps and any cell's freeing
  (a minor collection examines the recorded ones). Debug builds
  check, as each collection starts beside 10d's records, that every
  unrecorded entry names an old cell or, if weak, nothing. The
  heap's release empties the table and clears its alive flag first,
  so remaining handles are inert.
- This repository's stored values moved to handles: host's callback
  queue and task registry, timers' handlers and arguments, web's
  server, websocket and SSE dispatch functions and wasm's memory
  buffer (its host functions as weak handles), node's FFI callbacks
  and N-API's call-scope arena, the runtime's error and rejection
  hooks (the duplicate ReportingShims rooting gone), its running
  callbacks and rejections and its workers' dispatch. OpState and
  ResourceTable no longer require HostTrace (a type registers its
  tracing if it reports a graph), and value_free! is gone; until
  11b makes values unstorable, nothing in the type system stops a
  host type from storing a raw Value unreported. The two dns
  decoders and four wasm builders keep their values in a rooted
  list across [[Set]].
- N-API: references are counted handles, so napi_get_reference_value
  answers NULL once the referent died, as Node-API does; the
  reference napi_wrap returns is weak, so wrapped objects can die;
  wraps check identity by upgrading a weak handle; deferreds hold
  strong handles. Finalizers run now: NapiState's sweep, after the
  weak clearing, moves dead wraps' finalizers to a pending list run
  at the next call into an addon, in an environment of their own;
  none runs at teardown or if no addon is called again, and an
  exception a finalizer throws is dropped (open).
- The memory snapshot reports the live handles, strong and weak.
- Along the way: three ShadowRealm retention tests failed on the
  agent's tree from a latent false root: the client's roots() left
  each idle realm owner's address in a stack slot that the native
  stack scan's frame reuses (written only with TRUST_JS_GC_WHY, which
  is why probes hid it), and the larger frame moved it. Marking::new
  now lists the entered realms, so roots() touches only real roots.
- Tests: 15 in handle_tests.rs and an unregistered host type that
  reports nothing (survival across minor and major collections and
  promotion, release, inertness after the Engine drops, another
  heap's handle and value refused, weak handles cleared young at a
  minor collection and old at a major one, the flip, no stale
  upgrade at a reused address, the minor collection's report of
  recorded entries only, should_panic on a bypassed record);
  downstream: host's callback queue and task registry, armed timers,
  web's builders under a collecting setter, N-API's references, a
  dying wrapped object's finalizer and deferreds, a worker kept only
  by its dispatch and a server, across collections. Changed: host.rs
  tests register their trace types, memory.rs test types lose
  value_free!, the timers' inert handler is Code, the task-registry
  test makes handles, the wraps test checks that the sweep removes
  dead entries, wasm's test_stats takes a Ctx. Four mutations (the
  minor collection's record, the weak clearing, the heap id check,
  the release's clearing) each made tests fail.
- Gates (the review's, tiered, on the reviewed tree):
  - aarch64: lib 2307/0/7, its test list the parent's plus the 16
    new tests; clippy clean in the five configurations; rustfmt
    clean; rustdoc's 40 warnings are 10d's; the workspace builds
    with all targets without a diagnostic; git grep finds no
    value_free! and six HostTrace impls, N-API's (empty, for its
    sweep) and five test types.
  - Downstream suites: host 37/0/0, timers 6/0/0, fs (no tests), web
    62/0/1, node 45/0/0, runtime 184/0/1 (the agent also ran host,
    timers, web and N-API's at GC stress 1 and the runtime's at
    100).
  - Fixtures at --tier=jit (no-LTO release): compiles 56/10/160,
    outputs as at S0, 21, 6 and 16 collections; difftest 138 agree,
    12 skipped, no crash; the sustained workload's 194 collections
    and output as 10d's. The dumps are 10d's in all three forms.
  - Test262 at --tier=jit threshold 8: 53575/53575, the report
    identical to the S5 review fixes'.
  - Per-test GC stress at interval 1 of the targeted list (1177
    tests): 1172 pass, 5 are ignored.
  - x86-64 (zombie): clippy clean; lib 2141/0/7. Windows: clippy as
    its baseline (23 diagnostics); under Wine 2133 pass, 3 fail
    (step 1's snapshot codec tests) and 7 are ignored. Both lists
    are the parent's plus the 16 new tests. Fixtures: compiles
    56/10/160 and 21, 6 and 16 collections, outputs as on aarch64;
    difftest 138 agree, 12 skipped, no crash.
- Measured (gcmeasure.py; fat-LTO engines from fresh exports, 10d
  878d81371e7e, 11a 03612778a13c from the gated tree, main
  311ae9ae5658; copies made together; CPU 5, 15 interleaved rounds,
  medians; started once a local inference server had used
  under 1% of a core and all CPUs under two cores for a minute (an
  owner's process kept one core busy throughout), the other cores
  1.40 busy (median), 3.02 at most; outputs equal everywhere):
  - Against 10d: cycles within 0.5% and instructions within 0.05%
    on every workload, GC pause within 1.4%, peak RSS within 2%:
    the engine's own paths are unchanged.

Step 11b, the scoped embed API (an implementation agent's change,
reviewed):
- Values: the embed API's Value<'s> is an enum of Undefined, Null,
  Bool, Num and opaque BigInt, Str, Sym and Obj wrappers, each
  repr(transparent) over its cell's address with an invariant, !Send
  brand (PhantomData<*mut &'s ()>) and no deref; Object has get, set,
  call, is_callable and addr and compares by identity. The types are
  the engine's own in layout (repr(u64) with the same discriminants,
  the internal EMPTY marker read as undefined; static assertions and
  a test), so a native written against them is the same machine
  function: the engine stores and calls it unchanged, and generated
  code is 11a's.
- Scopes: Ctx<'s>, a repr(transparent) facade over the interpreter
  with 127 methods, each first switching to its own Engine's heap;
  Engine::scope takes a for<'s> closure, and Engine's 56 other
  methods take no values. NativeFn and NativeCaptureFn are for<'s>
  over (&mut Ctx<'s>, Value<'s>, &[Value<'s>]); native closures keep
  their JavaScript values as engine captures and may carry Rust
  state. Handle::new(cx, &v) and handle.get(cx) cross a scope's edge;
  ValueList<'s>, over the engine's rooted vector, replaces 11a's
  RootedValues at the six builders.
- Internals out of the public API: Gc, WeakGc, Object, Abrupt and the
  internal value are pub(crate) (git grep finds them in no public
  signature, 44 at 11a), the bytecode module private with Tier
  re-exported; the public items (rustdoc's JSON, every reachable
  item's signature and fields) are listed in target/s6-step11b.
- Host traits: HostGcVisitor's edge, root, js_to_native and
  native_to_js take weak handles (native_edge and native_root stay);
  internal, supports_native, is_minor, native_old_generation,
  HostGc::sweep_gc and unstable_collect_young_for_host_tests are
  gone; sweep_gc_with_native runs after dead values' weak handles are
  cleared and before any cell is freed. NativeCallableRetained and the
  retained-memory visitors report no values, which retires 10b's
  rescanned natives and reports_fixed_values (Error.captureStackTrace's
  getter keeps its frame names as engine captures).
- RealmEntry is a token of (heap, depth): the Engine keeps the saved
  Realm states on a stack traced as roots, and leaving checks the
  Engine and that the entry is the innermost.
- The migration (functions generic over 's, natives among them):
  engine glue and tests 37 (19), host 11 (3), timers 5 (4), fs 30
  (22), web 87 (55), node 240 (154), runtime 68 (54), repl 1; the cli
  unchanged, difftest one path. Not mechanical: each napi_value points
  to a boxed handle read into the current call's scope; the runtime's
  event loop runs in scopes, with values kept across checkpoints in
  handles and the rejection APIs returning handles; the REPL keeps
  each completion in a handle.
- Found on the way: deleting an apparently dead Env::try_borrow_mut
  broke a write-barrier test, because the call fell through Env's
  Deref to RefCell's method, which bypasses the barrier; restored.
  Gc's and Env's Deref to RefCell, crate-private now, stays a way
  around the barrier (open, step 12).
- Tests: removed the three rescanned-native tests (the table is
  gone); added captured_stack_getter_keeps_its_frame_names and nine
  scope tests (scoped values are the engine's, natives receive,
  return and throw every kind, two ValueList tests, a scope's heap
  after another Engine's, closure state with captures, a replaced
  Realm kept until left, two RealmEntry panics). Six compile_fail
  doctests, each checked for its error: a value stored in OpState
  (E0521), returned out of scope (a lifetime error), pushed into an
  outer Vec (E0521), two Engines' scopes mixed (E0521), and a Handle
  or a Value sent to another thread (E0277). Changed beyond porting:
  explicit_collections_are_major drops the removed entry; the host
  graph and trace tests and the HostGc impls in handle_tests and
  weak_metadata report through weak handles (embed-only now); the
  two cross-Engine mutation tests became internal tests; the foreign
  value of handles_and_values_of_another_engine_are_refused is built
  from the internal form, as no scoped path makes one.
- Gates (the review's: step 11's close, the tiered gates'
  milestone, on the reviewed tree):
  - aarch64: lib 2314/0/7, its test list the parent's less the three
    rescanned-native tests plus the 10 new; the doctests 9 pass (1
    ignored), the six compile_fail ones among them; clippy clean in
    the five configurations; rustfmt clean; rustdoc's warnings 8,
    none new and 32 gone; the workspace builds with all targets
    without a diagnostic; git grep finds Gc, WeakGc, Object or
    Abrupt in no public signature.
  - Downstream suites: host 37/0/0, timers 6/0/0, fs (no tests), web
    62/0/1, node 45/0/0, runtime 184/0/1.
  - Fixtures at --tier=jit (no-LTO release): compiles 56/10/160,
    outputs as at S0, 21, 6 and 16 collections; difftest 138 agree,
    12 skipped, no crash; the sustained workload's 194 collections
    and output as 11a's. The dumps are 11a's in all three forms.
  - Test262 at --tier=jit: 53575/53575 at threshold 8 and 53573 of
    53575 at 0, the reports identical to the S5 review fixes'.
  - Per-test GC stress with the verifier, poisoning and the debug
    checks, aarch64, the whole lib suite (2321 tests): 2284 pass at
    interval 1 (7 are ignored), the slow lists' 25 at 100 and 5 at
    1000.
  - x86-64 (zombie): clippy clean; lib 2148/0/7. Windows: clippy as
    its baseline (23 diagnostics); under Wine 2140 pass, 3 fail

Step 11 closed (2026-10-22): the plan f43c3136, 11a d8651ca5, 11b
(this commit).
- The embed API's values are scoped (Value<'s>, Ctx<'s>,
  Engine::scope) and cannot be stored, returned out of their scope,
  mixed between Engines or sent to another thread (six compile_fail
  doctests); what Rust keeps lives in rooted handles, checked
  against their heap and inert once it is released; no public item
  exposes the engine's cells. This repository's crates are migrated;
  TRust is ported after the merge review (the owner's decision).
- Performance and the fixtures' code are 10d's: the engine's own
  paths did not change.
- Open items carried past step 11, each recorded where it arose:
  - N-API finalizers run at the next call into an addon, none at
    teardown, and an exception a finalizer throws is dropped (11a).
  - Gc's and Env's Deref to RefCell, crate-private now, is a way
    around the write barrier (11b).
  - In release builds an internal EMPTY marker reaching a native's
    arguments would be undefined behaviour; debug builds assert it
    never does (11b).
  - Step 10's list stands.

Step 12 plan (2026-10-22), from a read-only survey of df5da39d
(target/s6-survey12/report.md, its census, experiments and
disassembly beside it) and the owner's decision:
- The owner: the packed 8-byte word replaces the 16-byte Value enum
  everywhere, in the engine's Rust code and in the embed API (11b's
  Value<'s> becomes 8 bytes too). TRust's port follows the merge
  review, against the API this step leaves.
- What the survey found: the packed word already encodes every value
  (NaN-boxed: every f64 inline, NaNs canonicalized, eight tags plus
  the property-only deferred prototype), and both of the collector's
  scans resolve its cell words by mask. The 16-byte Value is stored
  in 154 declarations (Binding.value with 94 sites, Scope.with_obj,
  the VM's this, builtins' state), passed or returned by 1,312
  functions, matched by 2,048 variant patterns (341 more in tests,
  111 in the embed API) and constructed at 2,427 sites. Generated
  code reads a wide Value only in seven binding templates and the
  with-object guards (which rely on Option<Value>'s niche) on ARM64
  and six emitters on x64; no helper's ABI carries one. The wide
  Value can derive Copy today (a clean check of an export); the
  packed word cannot, for 28 writes through shared references and
  the deferred prototype's promotion. Object holds 16 bytes for its
  heap's Rc, a weak-observed flag and a registry slot: allocation
  takes two Rc increments and a decrement and reserves a slot, and
  each dead object's finalizer spends about 40 instructions on that
  bookkeeping before its payload's drops (in 10c's profile 0.34%,
  1.25% and 1.81% of cycles on vue, djot and the sustained workload,
  an estimated 8%, 15% and 5% of their GC pause). One ARM64 template
  (SetProp's creation commit) still counts PropertyLayout
  references, and guards that the counted model motivated remain.
- The design: Value becomes a Copy, !Send word (repr(transparent)
  over u64) whose associated constants and functions are named like
  today's variants, so the constructions compile unchanged; matches
  go through a view, today's enum, made only by an inline view() and
  never stored. A property word stays the only place a deferred
  prototype lives; frame slots become cells of words. Bindings and
  this hold words, so the binding templates take the packed path
  globals take, and with_obj is a word with EMPTY for none. The
  embed API's Value<'s> becomes the word with its zero-sized brand
  and a kind() view; EMPTY reads as undefined, which retires 11b's
  open item on undefined behaviour in release builds.
- Step 12 runs as six commits, each built, gated (tiered) and
  measured on its own:
  - 12a, heap-level object accounting: objects drop their heap's Rc
    and registry slot; the live count rises at allocation and falls
    by each swept object page's dead; weak observers are keyed by
    address and evicted from the dead list; objects with nothing to
    free skip finalization where the sweep can tell. Its review
    also measures the byte budget's floor below 8 MiB with the
    nursery, the lever left for react's peak RSS (a change of
    default is recorded before it is made).
  - 12b, PropertyLayout as payload cells (traced from properties,
    the shape cache, deferred prototypes and templates, copy on
    write by a sticky shared bit, checked in debug builds); the
    creation commit's count traffic, jit_ownership.rs and the guards
    the counted model motivated go.
  - 12c, Copy and views, neutral: the wide Value Copy, the packed
    word Copy with cells for its 28 shared writes, view() and kind()
    as identities, every pattern ported (by a codemod, compiler-
    checked), and Gc's and Env's Deref to RefCell removed so that no
    borrow can pass the barrier (11b's open item). The dumps must be
    11b's.
  - 12d, packed storage the JIT reads: Binding.value, with_obj, the
    VM's this and JitThisView as words; the binding templates' wide
    branches and x64's wide emitters deleted.
  - 12e, the flip, engine and embed API together (their layouts are
    tied): Value and Value<'s> become words, the natives' arguments
    slices of words (DecodedArgs and its kin gone), every number
    construction canonicalizing NaN, raw bits unsafe and checked in
    debug builds. The dumps must be 12d's.
  - 12f, one representation: the packed word becomes Value, and the
    conversions, StoredValue and the wide emitters' remains go.
  Step 12 closes with the milestone gate after 12f; the S6 exit gate
  and the merge review with the owner follow.
- Risks, each with its check: words in Rust frames for the
  conservative scan (resolved by mask, as JIT frames' words are;
  the step's whole-suite stress); NaN payloads (canonicalized at
  construction, debug-checked raw bits); the lost niche (the
  with-object probe would refuse every name template without a
  failing test: a test of a name template under with); a native's
  argument slice moving during its call (asserted); decoding at hot
  matches (predicates where the measurement shows it); copy on
  write without counts (12b's debug check).
- Not expected of step 12: react's peak RSS (+48% against S0's
  +25%), which comes from cell pages and dead payloads; djot's GC
  time (7.8% against 5%) is expected to fall with 12a, not
  necessarily under 5%. The exit gate reports both against the
  bounds.
- Not in step 12: element-range dirtiness and a per-page card
  summary (step 10's open items); N-API finalizers at teardown
  (11a's).

Step 12a review amendment (2026-10-22), before the fix:
- Allocating in the active heap makes host reentry restoration an
  object-safety requirement. A native can enter another Engine,
  return without another outer Ctx operation, and leave the other
  heap active for the caller's next object allocation. The old
  prototype-derived heap concealed that path for ordinary objects.
- Restore the owning Agent at native and other host callback
  boundaries before allocation resumes, including error paths.
  The existing enter_agent fast path alone cannot do this: when
  already active it saves no restoration state. Tests exercise
  nested Engine execution and post-callback allocation at all tiers.
- Weak owner lookup must skip unrelated heaps currently borrowed
  for sweeping: dropping native Rust state can drop a captured
  Engine whose weak metadata unsubscribes from its own live heap.
  Cover collection-triggered nested destruction and teardown.

Step 12b review amendment (2026-10-22), before implementation:
- The layout-edge audit found that heap-local layout rebuilding
  alone cannot make foreign parsed-code reuse safe. Function's
  global code/code2 slots also cache heap-local constants and JIT
  immediates; thread-rooted chunk constants can outlive their heap.
- Runtime caches are selected by never-reused heap identity, with
  a cheap primary entry, bounded foreign entries and weak lifetime
  tokens cleared before heap release. Realm-compatibility checks
  stay inside the selected heap. Active frames retain immutable
  chunks. A foreign entry is rebuilt from neutral AST/compiler
  recipes without reading old Props, layouts or constant values.
- Heap-local weak registrations trace the actual FunctionMaps and
  Chunk owners, including late literal/initializer publication.
  Compilation roots temporary constants until the chunk takes
  them and registers its roots, with no intervening safepoint.
  Constructor hints use the existing conditional side-slot table
  and minor-write records. Private-layout validation deduplicates
  actual owning-slot addresses, not repeated trace visits.

Step 12a, heap-level object accounting (an implementation agent's
change, reviewed; 2026-10-22):
- Object no longer holds its heap's Rc, registry slot or weak-watch
  flag. Allocation uses the active Agent heap. The core increments
  its attached object count at allocation and subtracts newly dead
  cells while sweeping each page or large cell, including pinned
  zombies exactly once. Release resets it; debug builds compare it
  with live_cells. Snapshots use that same live-cell enumeration.
- Weak observers are keyed by object address and removed before
  freeing, with foreign watches routed to the target's actual heap.
  The sparse thread-local heap directory keeps weak entries only.
  Unsubscription tolerates a heap already borrowed for sweeping;
  nested captured Engine destruction cannot borrow that core again.
  Notifications enqueue identities; cleanup does not run inside GC.
- Finalization skips objects without Rust-owned payloads. The check
  covers layouts, spills, sidecars, callables, exotic state, native
  data and accessors; the object census still sees every death.
- The creation IC reads the fixed shape directory from JitCtx,
  replacing its receiver-to-heap load and address calculation.
  Release DWARF: Object 168 to 160 bytes (RefCell<Object> remains in
  the 192-byte cell class), Interp 5792 to 5776, JitCtx 320 to 328
  and JitFrame 2368 to 2376. The fixed JitCtx prefix is unchanged.
- The recorded reentry amendment: native dispatch, committed native
  calls, clocks, module loaders, settings hooks and live collection
  queries restore the owning Agent before engine execution resumes,
  including exceptions and unwinding. Scoped input conversions run
  before activation. Cross-heap prototypes are rejected in debug
  builds; allocation does not borrow a same-heap prototype.
- Tests cover count timing, snapshots, limits, minor/major death,
  weak address reuse, native payload drops, creation commits and
  nested host reentry at all tiers. Three independent release
  mutations fail their exact tests: missing count subtraction
  (4102 versus 2), observer eviction (table not empty), and native
  owner restoration (new object in the wrong heap). Focused checks:
  66 debug tests, 28 at stress 1 and five diagnostics tests pass.
- Commit gate, source frozen throughout (start/end hashes equal):
  - aarch64 lib 2329/0/7, 15 new tests and none removed; workspace
    all-target build without diagnostics; downstream host 37/0/0,
    timers 6/0/0, fs no tests, web 62/0/1, node 45/0/0 and runtime
    184/0/1. Rustdoc retains its eight baseline warnings.
  - Test262 at JIT threshold 8: 53575/53575, report identical to the
    accepted baseline. Targeted per-test GC stress: 1642 pass and
    seven ignored at interval 1, no failures or timeout retries.
  - Fixtures: compiles 56/10/160, collections 21/6/16, outputs as
    before; sustained allocation at JIT and bytecode keeps its
    output and 194 collections. Difftest: 138 agree, 12 budget
    skips, no crashes.
  - zombie: x64 lib 2162/0/7, Wine 2154/3/7 (only the three known
    snapshot codec failures), lists +14/-0. x64 clippy clean;
    Windows retains its 23 baseline diagnostics. Fixtures and
    difftest agree with the local gate.
- Two test-only feature corrections followed the frozen gate:
  the new clock test uses the internal clock slot, and an existing
  x64 property test calls the internal collector wrapper. Production
  source is unchanged. All five local clippy configurations pass;
  exact clock tests pass locally with/without embed. Both corrected
  tests pass on default x64, embed x64 and Wine; x64 default/small
  clippy also pass. Formatting and diff checks are clean.
- After the usual address normalization, every fixture chunk in
  all three dump modes equals 11b's with only measured field moves
  and the exact directory rewrite (dumpcmp12a.py); no GC-timing
  exemptions. Deliberate wrong prologue and directory-base registers
  are both rejected by the
  comparator. Evidence is in target/s6-step12ar.
- Measured on crystal aarch64, CPU 5, rustc 1.99.1 for 11b and
  12a; the retained main reference uses rustc 1.99.0. Fat-LTO,
  one-codegen-unit release exports (11b adbe71c66a3e, reviewed 12a
  5ea1c66f0230, main 311ae9ae5658), copies made together, 15
  interleaved rounds of empty/vue/react/djot/sustained at JIT and
  sustained at bytecode. The 4/6 MiB trials use the same 12a binary
  with TRUST_JS_GC_MIN_HEAP_BYTES; the default remains 8 MiB.
  - Waited 360 seconds for quiet: inference used 41 ticks and all
    CPUs 6238 ticks in the last minute. During the 450 valid runs,
    other cores were 0.38 busy at median, 2.52 at most; no scaled
    counter, nonzero exit or output difference. Data and summaries:
    target/s6-step10/m12a.
  - Against 11b, vue/react/djot cycles +0.7/-0.3/-0.2%, instructions
    +0.29/-0.20/-0.39%, GC pauses -3.5/-4.2/-3.7%; peak RSS
    -1.0/+3.1/-1.7%. React's measured ranges overlap (22.7-25.2 MB
    before, 22.7-25.5 after). Empty-script cycles are unchanged.
  - Sustained allocation: JIT/bytecode cycles -0.5/-1.7%, GC pauses
    -1.7/-2.0%, peak RSS -2.9/-1.8%. Median longest pauses improve
    from 18.2/18.1 to 18.0/17.8 ms; the largest observed bytecode
    pause is 20.8 ms, versus 18.7 before.
  - The 4/6 MiB floors do not change React's collections or work;
    its median RSS moves -1.4/-1.1%, within the same ranges. For
    Djot, 4 MiB lowers peak RSS 5.5% but raises total GC pause 4.8%
    (6 MiB raises it 6.0% with only 0.4% lower RSS). Both worsen
    Djot's open GC-time bound, without closing React's memory bound,
    so no default change is made.
  - Against main, fixture JIT cycles are 12.1/14.7/8.3% lower.
    React's peak RSS remains +48.3% (bound +25%); Djot's GC share
    excluding exit remains 7.6% (bound 5%). These remain open for
    the S6 exit review; vue/react GC share is 3.6/4.4%.
  - Compilation costs, same binaries/tier and 15 paired rounds:
    vue/djot native compile time -0.8/+0.4%; generated code bytes
    -0.04/0/-0.07% on vue/react/djot. React's short timings had a
    noisy initial +6.4% median difference. A separate 100-pair
    capture gives 1.557 versus 1.556 ms (-0.04%), with a paired
    bootstrap 95% interval of -4.1 to +5.7%; no reproducible
    increase. Bytecode compile time there is unchanged. Both
    captures retain every sample under m12a-compile and
    m12a-compile-react; all exits, outputs and counters are valid.

Step 12c/12d review clarification (2026-10-22), before either step:
- The neutral Copy/view step compares generated code with 12b. The
  original 11b label predates the intended changes in 12a and 12b;
  it is not a requirement to restore their removed instructions.
- The storage audit found Interp.new_target still read as a wide
  value by ARM direct calls, including a second-word load, a wide
  save and a byte-only clear. Pack it in 12d with Binding.value,
  with_obj and VM this, including save/restore and root accounting.
  This preserves 12e's requirement for unchanged emitted code.

Step 12b full-gate review repair (2026-10-22), before the fix:
- The first lib gates exposed a debug verifier defect: layout-owner
  inventory called the collector's host-root traversal, consuming
  the native graph before actual marking. Inventory must use only
  the interpreter's non-consuming field trace; host graphs contain
  addresses, not layout-handle owner slots. The mixed native/JS
  ephemeron test is the integration regression for this path.
- Chunk root registration adds Weak owners, so Rc::get_mut is no
  longer a valid fixture-editing mechanism. Rewrite uniquely strong
  fixture chunks as owned values inside the existing test no-GC
  guard, then publish a new Rc and register that actual owner.
- Owner-death cache tests must expect runtime retirement's new epoch
  fence while retaining their live-sibling sharing proof. Native
  creation-counter proofs require process isolation from the global
  prototype epoch. A new runtime test also used an embed-only
  microtask wrapper in non-embed builds; preserve its internal work.
- The frozen first local gate was stopped after workspace/downstream
  success and the recorded lib failures; zombie completed. The
  corrected tree receives fresh routine gates on both machines.

Step 12b review tooling correction (2026-10-22):
- The retained DWARF helper treated pointer DIEs without an explicit
  byte size as their pointee type, creating oversized member ranges
  in the dump comparator. It now reads the containing unit's address
  size and rejects overlapping or out-of-bounds struct members.
- Re-extracted both 12a layouts from the original retained binaries
  and repeated all nine comparisons with the corrected ranges:
  every chunk still passes, with no exemptions. Original evidence
  is preserved; corrected evidence is under target/s6-step12ar,
  layouts-corrected and dumps-corrected-layouts.txt.

Step 12b measurement clarification (2026-10-22), before measuring:
- Binary .comment sections identify rustc 1.99.1 (58cf8f9c3) in
  11b and 12a, and 1.99.0 (b940084d7) in the retained S0 main
  binary. Corrected 12a's compiler description; its comparisons
  with 11b use the same compiler, while comparisons with the old
  main artifact include a compiler difference.
- Rebuild e3310993 from a fresh export with the current compiler
  and fat-LTO/one-codegen-unit settings. Measure both this matched

  four binaries together. Keep both references and report the
  compiler effect separately; no historical result is overwritten.

Step 12b stress fixture repair (2026-10-22), before the fix:
- Targeted stress found a fixture that passes an unbranded object
  from one Engine to another interpreter with the foreign heap
  active. This is unavailable through the scoped public API and
  violates I6 once property layouts become heap cells: the foreign
  transition cache installs its layout in the owner's object.
- Retain the shape-identity/collision regression using heap-neutral
  key prefixes and integer shape IDs to exercise the foreign
  transition cache. Keep each resulting layout in its own heap,
  mutate objects with their owner active, and preserve all-tier
  warmed-cache assertions. Explicit minor/major collections check
  both heaps. The collector verifier and ownership rules stay in
  force; no production cross-heap references are introduced.

Step 12b performance review (2026-10-22), before any repair:

All 360 samples in target/s6-step10/m12b are valid: 15 interleaved
rounds, six workloads, four retained fat-LTO engines, crystal CPU 5.
Quiet began after 60 seconds (inference 9 ticks, all CPUs 2437 ticks);
other cores busy median 0.40, maximum 3.30. Outputs agree, counters are
unscaled, and exits are zero.

Against 12a, fixture cycles rise 0.5-1.8%; sustained JIT/bytecode cycles
rise 2.0/3.1%. Peak RSS stays within 2.8% on every workload. Native
compile medians improve and fixture generated code shrinks 1.2-1.7%.
However, sustained allocation's median longest pause rises from 17.9 ms
to 21.1/21.0 ms, about 18/17% at JIT/bytecode, with paired 95% bootstrap
intervals wholly above 16%. This exceeds the component regression
policy: hold 12b for investigation and rework.

A diagnostic repeats 150 major collections over the sustained workload's
retained 200,000-object shape. Profiles of the exact retained engines
attribute most additional sampled cycles to Heap::locate, mark_word and
mark. The new per-object layout edge is the leading explanation; this is
profiling evidence, not an acceptance comparison. Data:
target/s6-step12br/pause-profile. User-space instruction samples work;
stack sampling is denied by the current perf settings, which remain
unchanged. No implementation repair is chosen yet.

Step 12e preflight clarifications (2026-10-22), before 12d/12e:

Shrinking Value can change JitCtx.error's Option<Abrupt> and
resume_step's Option<VmStep>, shifting later JitCtx fields and
JitFrame's word base. Interp is repr(Rust); pending_new_target and
import_meta can affect its probed member offsets too. No byte delta is
assumed before measurement. Keep the compact carriers: 12e's neutral
comparison to 12d may normalize only actual DWARF-measured JIT field
moves, proven at each instruction's base register and source emitter.
All other instructions, scalar constants and branch destinations remain
exact. Object, Property, Scope, Binding, Chunk and fixed cache records
must be measured as applicable; they receive no blanket exemption.

The argument-loan audit also found an existing Function.prototype.apply
rooting gap: its ordinary pooled Vec is unregistered while later indexed
getters may collect values returned by earlier getters. Keep that
evaluated prefix rooted during construction as part of 12e's
apply/argument boundary work. Both simple and forwarded constructor
initializer paths must stop destructively replacing words in borrowed
caller windows. Bare native tests must exercise opaque function-pointer
calls for Ok and Err, EMPTY forwarding and post-call polling/tail-drain
lifetimes on ARM, x64 and Wine.

Step 12b marking review amendment (2026-10-22), before the repair:

Evaluate a bounded positive-word cache local to one Marker to avoid
resolving and checking the same shared layout/prototype edge repeatedly.
Compare a tiny recent-word set with a small direct-mapped table; retain
the version supported by full-workload measurements, including misses
and churn. Every traced edge remains present.

Only an allocated, non-zombie cell proven marked may enter the cache,
after first-mark parent/statistics/worklist and waiter-wakeup
bookkeeping. Exact-word equality is required. A fresh cache starts each
collection before native-stack scanning and disappears before post-
marking allocations or sweeping. Minor card-owner worklist tracing,
private-layout owner verification and the independent heap verifier
remain unchanged. Tests cover collisions, repeated collections and
address reuse, ephemeron wakeups, old-to-young cards and invalid words;
key mutations must fail their intended assertions. Preserve the initial
measurements, then repeat routine gates and matched performance after
any retained production change.

Step 12b cache comparison (2026-10-22), before refinement:

Fresh fat-LTO recent4 (42d1bac6f3ed), direct32 (2b69819fa623) and
inlining-only (eaacf3a3d057) exports were compared with 12a, initial 12b
and matched main over 540 valid fixture samples. Direct32 restores the
sustained longest pause to about 17.7 ms; recent4 reaches 19.1 ms and
inlining alone 20.7 ms. However, direct32 adds minor-collection miss
traffic versus inlining alone. Across 88 additional shared/distinct-
layout diagnostic samples, it improves shared major-collection time
about 19%, but distinct layouts' cycles rise 6.7% versus initial 12b.
These are diagnostic comparisons, not replacements for the acceptance
fixtures; keep all samples.

Evaluate one contained refinement: use the cache for small trace batches
and the original locate/mark path for batches above 64 edges. Specialize
mark_word at compile time, select the loop once per batch, and keep all
marking/waiter/card behavior identical. This preserves small shared-edge
caching during both collection kinds and avoids filling it with long
unique streams. The threshold is an amortization heuristic, not a claim
that large batches are unique. Test uncached sole-held
targets/descendants and add large repeated-reference and shared-minor
diagnostics before selection. No candidate is accepted yet.

Step 12b marking selection (2026-10-22), before final routine gates:

Retain the 32-entry exact positive-word cache with the 64-edge batch
limit. The uncached specialization preserves every first-mark effect;
large batches still traverse all edges. The measured source is frozen
at core SHA 2eadf66c; a separately reviewed 167-line test insertion
adds real minor-first and old-carded large-batch cases. Production is
byte-identical. The final source core SHA is 179ef1d8.

Fresh fat-LTO binary 787cbefb0836 (20127584 bytes), current compiler,
was compared over 540 valid fixture samples and 176 diagnostic samples.
Against 12a, sustained longest-pause medians fall 17.98 to 15.61/15.55
ms at JIT/bytecode; total GC time falls 6.1/5.5%. Framework cycles
change +1.54/+0.23/+0.34% for Vue/React/Djot, with peak RSS within
2.5%. The distinct-layout diagnostic costs 2.33% cycles versus initial
12b (paired 95% CI 0.50..4.79), down from the broad cache's 6.7%.
Shared major and young shared churn improve 34.6% and 17.8%. Large
repeated arrays lose the broad cache's extra gain but remain 3.6%
faster than initial 12b. These probes supplement the acceptance
fixtures; none replaces them.

A 600-sample, 100-round follow-up investigated the noisy empty-exit
GC and React compilation components. React native compilation is
4.2% lower than 12a (95% CI -9.29..-1.13). Empty startup wall/cycles
change +0.86/+1.65%, RSS -0.02%. Its exit-GC median changes +0.26%,
but the interval remains wide (-3.38..17.96%); do not claim a precise
exit-GC speedup or equivalence. Retain the initial 15-round estimate
and all follow-up samples. Both have identical outputs and valid
counters. The first 12a empty run alone has 31 major faults; it is
retained. Other-core load medians/maxima are 0.595/3.175 for fixtures,
0.895/3.415 for diagnostics and 1.499/4.256 for the small follow-up.
All runs start after the established 60-second quiet check.

Artifacts: target/s6-step10/m12b-mark-batch64, m12b-batch64-probes
and m12b-batch64-small, including component-review.jsonl intervals.
Final local and zombie routine gates follow this selection. No
commit is made before those gates and the source review complete.

Step 12e native ABI clarification (2026-10-22), before implementation:

The existing bare/captured callback conversion changes the function
pointer's return type from Result<scoped Value, scoped Value> to
Result<internal Value, internal Value>. Equal size/alignment and
successful opaque calls do not establish this aggregate ABI contract.
Rust documents compatibility for transparent wrappers themselves, not
an automatic substitution through arbitrary aggregate types.

Keep callback calls at a signature with their actual declared Result
return type, then convert its Ok/Err value explicitly. Preserve bare
function storage without an allocated closure, native caching,
read-only argument loans, brands and EMPTY normalization. The read-
only preflight is selecting the smallest sound dispatch representation
before 12e starts; no implementation choice is made here. ABI tests
remain required regression evidence, rather than the safety argument.

References checked against Rust 1.99.1 documentation:
https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.fn.html#abi-compatibility
https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/type-layout.html

Step 12e ABI design selected (2026-10-22), before implementation:

Use a separate typed ScopedNative bare-function variant and a typed
ScopedCaptured body. The native IC keeps its existing fields and uses
unused direct flag 0x40 to select the declared callback signature inside
Rust helpers. Its compiled-entry bit stays clear and intrinsic ID stays
zero. All four native pointer reconstruction paths must honor the flag
before reentry; scoped callbacks never enter engine-intrinsic shortcuts.

Map Ok/Err explicitly after calling the original typed pointer. Share
native bookkeeping without allocating a closure adapter. Retain argument
loans, captures, host restoration, interruption, constructor state and
post-call tail lifetimes. Existing emitted probes still test direct bit
zero; verify unchanged code and measure the expected-stable callable,
cache and payload layouts. This changes no public callback ergonomics.
The detailed source inventory is retained locally for implementation;
these requirements and the safety rationale are tracked here.

Step 12d x64 fixed-name completion (2026-10-22), before the fix:

The new exact-mode tests found that x64 has no native FIXED_NAME_IC
branch: a valid fixed-layout current scope falls to the helper. Complete
that path in 12d, whose coverage explicitly includes packed native
binding commits in fixed environments. Reuse the validated slot-address
logic; preserve layout/slot bounds, full-word with absence, live
initialization/import/mutability flags and the actual environment's
write barrier. Exercise all word kinds, rejection and recovery, and a
discriminating isolated mutation. This is a deliberate x64 admission
addition, not a claimed neutral width-only rewrite. Unrelated packed
loop paths keep their existing admission and bailout behavior.

Step 12b, traced property layouts and generated count guards (code
change, reviewed; 2026-10-22):
- PropertyLayout is a non-Copy GC handle. Cloning permanently marks
  its payload shared; structural writes detach into a private cell.
  Keys and their optional index have a payload finalizer. Objects,
  Props, deferred prototypes, shape directories, array-length and
  for-in caches, constructor hints, function maps and chunks trace
  their layout edges. Debug verification counts owning slots, not
  repeated visits, before enforcing the private-layout invariant.
- Function runtime caches belong to a heap identified by a
  never-reused ID. Primary lookup borrows its stable entry; foreign
  entries are bounded to four and live closures keep evicted entries
  recoverable through a weak per-heap index. Major collections prune
  and shrink that index after finalization; minors do not scan it.
  Actual weak Chunk/FunctionMaps registrations trace late fields.
  Source cloning clears derived caches without touching old cells,
  and runtime retirement fences raw call-cache slots before freeing
  them, even when another owner retains the compiled chunk.
- Generated layout count traffic and jit_ownership.rs are gone.
  Stores no longer inspect displaced values for ownership; dense
  holes still take their checked path, and numeric reads retain
  their type guards. Exact Math/isNaN intrinsics ignore receiver
  kind. x64 word transfers admit BigInt while preserving TDZ,
  lazy-word, equality and HTMLDDA semantics. Native return paths
  publish their result unconditionally, including full Undefined
  for mode zero. Descriptor, capacity and receiver card checks stay.
- Sole-holder tests cover static and late code/template layouts,
  constants, private keys, weak constructor hints and cache eviction.
  Foreign parsed-code reuse, runtime leases and neutral clones run
  across heaps and after heap release. Native counter assertions
  distinguish real fast commits from helper fallbacks. Ten isolated
  mutations fail at their intended assertions: eight trace/shared/
  retirement cases, a native creation-card omission, and a faithful
  per-heap shape-ID allocator restoring the old collision.
- Final selected-source routine gates (fresh after the marking repair):
  - aarch64 lib 2367/0/7, list +40/-2 (two renamed tests); all five
    clippy configurations and formatting clean, rustdoc retains
    eight warnings, workspace all-target build has no diagnostics.
    Downstream host/timers/fs/web/node/runtime pass 37/6/0/62/45/184
    tests, with the existing web/runtime ignores.
  - Test262 JIT threshold 8: 53575/53575, identical baseline report.
    Targeted stress: 1822 pass, five ignored, no failures or
    timeouts across all 1827 selected tests; no retries needed.
  - zombie x64 lib 2197/0/7; Wine 2189/3/7, only the three baseline
    snapshot codec failures. Lists +37/-2. x64 clippy is clean and
    Windows retains 23 diagnostics. All release checks pass.
  - Fixtures keep outputs, 56/10/160 compiles and 21/6/16
    collections on both machines. Difftest: 138 agree, 12 budget
    skips, no crash. Sustained allocation keeps its output and
    194 collections at both tiers. All nine final dump pairs match
    initial 12b exactly apart from consistent address relocations.
  - Full root-owned local/remote logs and source maps are retained
    in target/s6-step12bmr; the remote source matches all 493
    selected paths. Earlier failed gates and diagnostic variants
    remain preserved separately; no failure is relabeled a pass.
- The per-Marker cache has 32 exact positive words, initialized
  afresh each collection. It records only allocated, non-zombie
  marks after every first-mark effect and waiter notification.
  Descriptor batches above 64 edges use the uncached specialized
  loop; card tracing and both verifiers remain independent.
  Thirteen tests cover hits, collisions, invalid words, native
  roots, reuse, cards, conditional edges and the real minor paths.
  Three cache mutants and two batch-path mutants fail their
  intended assertions in isolated successful builds. The final
  two minor tests are test-only additions to measured production;
  root review verifies the exact 167-line insertion.
- Release DWARF: Object 160, Interp 5776, JitCtx 328 and JitFrame
  2376 bytes remain unchanged; PropertyLayoutData grows 32 to 40
  while losing its external Rc header; Chunk grows 1320 to 1344.
- All nine fixture/stub dump comparisons are accounted for by exact
  source-constrained rewrites, four measured Vec-header loads and
  four measured Chunk loads/stores. The comparator verifies base
  register provenance, scalar MOV/MOVK constants, consistent tagged
  pointer relocations and surviving branch destinations. Cards and
  semantic checks remain exact. No unexplained chunk is exempted.
  - Djot's inline-stub constructor crosses the existing optional
    code budget: 15820 to 15040 bytes against a 15104-byte limit.
    Retained binaries' REGIONLOG confirms rejection then admission.
    A fresh isolated 12a diagnostic export raises only that budget
    allowance, admitting the old region for complete comparison.
    Its entire code matches after the planned rewrites; it is never
    used as a performance baseline or production candidate.
  - Four deliberate comparator mutations are rejected: wrong
    Undefined publication, wrong hole tag, wrong layout base
    register and an omitted card inside the newly admitted region.
    Each corresponding unmodified comparison passes.
- Performance selection and all component intervals are recorded
  above, including rejected variants and the small-component
  follow-up. Final fat-LTO binary 787cbefb0836 (20127584 bytes),
  parent 12a 5ea1c66f0230, matched main 4de8ccc1edd4, rustc 1.99.1,
  fat LTO/one codegen unit on Crystal aarch64 (CPU 5 pinned,
  Cortex-X925/A725, Linux 7.3.0, frequency boost disabled).
  Framework cycles versus 12a change +1.54/+0.23/+0.34%; sustained
  JIT/bytecode cycles -1.72/-0.48%. Median longest sustained pauses
  improve 13.2/13.5%, and total GC time 6.1/5.5%. Generated fixture
  code shrinks, while compile-cost and memory reviews are retained
  separately. The noisy empty exit-GC interval remains explicit.
  Against matched main, framework cycles improve 10.5/14.4/8.1%,
  sustained JIT/bytecode 34.8/28.2%; this does not close S0's
  outstanding React RSS or Djot GC-share bounds.

Step 12e/12f tagged-helper clarification (before implementation)

The tagged.rs module contains live numeric helpers as well as an
older representation experiment. Five bytecode numeric paths use
TaggedNumericFrame/TaggedNumberFrame; jit_ir imports neutral IDs,
RootMap and DeoptRecord, producing CopySlot recipes. Step 12e uses
canonical Value constructors directly in those five Number helpers,
preserving ToInt32, Number::exponentiate edge cases and the existing
tagged_arithmetic flag. Invalid-word and NaN tests move to the real
Value contract. This does not remove the live IR metadata or add
GC-bearing constants to metadata that does not trace them. Inventory
the unused HeapRef/root/materialization experiment before the 12f
single-representation cleanup; retain meaningful IR validation.

Step 12f scaffold cleanup clarification (before implementation)

The source inventory finds no live handle-table or relocation bridge
in tagged.rs. After 12e replaces the five numeric shadow helpers,
only jit_ir's neutral metadata imports and one side-exit validation
test use the module. Step 12f removes the obsolete second TaggedValue,
32-bit HeapRef, fake root set, shadow frames and toy materializer.
It preserves root-map validation and ordered CopySlot deopt metadata
in a neutral module, with no embedded GC values. Port meaningful
bitmap and side-exit checks; do not retain the unused toy heap just
for its tests. The real gc_core::NoGcScope remains unchanged.

The now-redundant tagged_arithmetic field, environment switch and
alternate Number path also retire in 12f. Preserve coercion ordering,
ToInt32, signed zero, NaN and exponentiation edge-case coverage on the
canonical path. Removing the flag may reorder Rust-layout Interp
fields. The neutral 12f/12e comparison admits only measured field
moves with source and base-register proof; it admits no semantic
emitted-code change, guessed offset range or blanket block exemption.

Step 12e callback argument roots (review finding, before the fix)

Readonly argument-window review found a pre-existing gap at the
safe callback boundary. Interp::call_callback/invoke can supply a
Rust-heap slice to CallbackCache::call_values. Both the compiled
fast and cached entries poll before copying parameters into their
callee frame, but this incoming heap window had no registered root.
The generic call and committed native call already root their input.

Two isolated pre-fix probes on zombie, from source fd209928..., each
force exactly one major pre-entry collection. The fresh argument is
held only by a heap Vec and a weak tracker; the compiled scalar body
performs no collection. Both fast and cached probes reach the actual
native callee and fail their first-mark assertion: Stack rather than
Root. Survival by a stale native-stack word does not establish the
required boundary root. The failing source and results are retained.

Root the borrowed slice in CallbackCache::call_values after its
non-JIT generic early return, through cached/fast/fallback dispatch,
post-call polling and tail draining. A root guard borrows the same
window; no copy or change to emitted instructions is needed. Its
other caller supplies a native-stack array. Prepared fixed-array
calls retain their established Decision 0 stack-root contract; raw
compiled entry contracts must explicitly require caller roots as
well as stable immutable storage. Verify both pre-entry probes and
that roots end with the call, then run the normal cross-tier gates.

Step 12c: Copy values, decoded views and explicit mutable slots

Value, LStr, JsBigInt and PackedValue now implement Copy. The engine
and scoped enums retain their 16-byte layout for this preparatory
step; distinct ValueView/ValueKind enums share variant inventories
and explicit discriminants. Patterns across the engine and host
crates use decoded views. Constructors retain their names; views
are not stored. The scoped invariant brand and thread affinity stay.

PackedValue is a plain 8-byte word. Property-only PropWord retains
Cell mutability and deferred-prototype materialization; frame slots
use Cell<PackedValue>, while operand windows use plain words. The
FRAME_BUF backing allocation uses UnsafeCell for actual mutation;
no mutable slice is manufactured from shared Cell storage. Binding
string accumulation decodes, appends and writes back while its
original binding remains rooted. Gc/Env expose inherent borrows,
with the card barrier before mutable borrows; their Deref-to-RefCell
escape is gone. Explicit raw payload pointers serve the few audited
unsafe sites. Borrowed string text is tied to the original Value,
with copied frontier append/growth and precise GC regressions.

Four new tests pin Copy/size/thread affinity, every decoded variant,
and internal/scoped borrowed text through append, growth and minor
and major collections. Existing branding compile-fail doctests,
barrier, alias, lazy-prototype and rooting tests remain. The source
and expanded-pattern census covers macros and forwarding patterns.
Key mutants remove Env::try_borrow_mut's barrier or bypass property
promotion; both fail at the intended verifier/execution boundary.

The first full zombie gate exposed one converted layout-test bug:
it compared an original Value address to a Bool field in a decoded
copy. The corrected test reads only the original initialized tag
and payload byte, preserving its layout check without reading
padding. An address-sensitive view census found no second instance.
The failed run and exact before/after snapshots remain preserved.
A focused ARM rerun passed; the final gates use the corrected source.

The full ARM run exposed a second test assumption: the strict Math
intrinsic hit count can lose a hit when another test invalidates the
process-global call-cache epoch. The original failure had no epoch
counter, so a specific concurrent invalidation cannot be identified.
The old test passes in its own GC-stress process. Isolate its exact
hit proof in a child that inherits stress; retain the 32-hit bound.
A one-call positive control must hit once, then the identical call
must miss after forced invalidation; warm again before measurement.
The repaired test passes normally and at stress 1; the full ARM lib
rerun is 2371/0/7 and all five clippy configurations remain clean.
Only this ARM test module changes after the original gate; production
builds and x64/Wine are unaffected. Source maps verify that boundary.

Step 12d: packed bindings, scope receivers and call state

Binding.value is a private execution word with explicit accessors.

The x64 FIXED_NAME_IC branch is completed as recorded beforehand:
a nonzero matching layout plus bounded Small/Indexed slot lookup.
Other non-mirror loop admission remains unchanged. Obsolete wide

reviewed; no collector, guard or fallback is exempted by this step.

Native proofs cover each cache mode, all execution kinds, six update

Step 12e: engine and scoped Values become words together

The engine Value and branded embed Value are transparent eight-byte
Copy words. Match views decode only for inspection. The scoped
invariant lifetime and thread restriction remain. Every language
Number constructor canonicalizes NaN, including reserved tag-shaped
payloads; finite values, signed zeros and subnormals retain their bits.
ArrayBuffer backing bytes and internal Wasm float payloads retain
their separate contracts. Raw reconstruction is unsafe, checks the
encoding in debug builds, and requires a live cell of the proper kind
and Agent. Pointer packing retains its release 48-bit fit assertion.

Native calls borrow the original immutable evaluated argument window.
DecodedArgs and redundant argument packing are removed. Mutable Cell
slots and property words are never reinterpreted as immutable argument
slices. Constructor initializers read and copy input words. Callers
retain the window until the call, post-call polls and pending tails
finish, then retire or recycle their own storage.

Bare and captured scoped callbacks keep their actual function types.
ScopedNative tag 13 preserves bare function storage; native IC bit
0x40 distinguishes the typed signature in Rust dispatch. The bare
pointer's low bit remains clear, its intrinsic is zero and chunk_raw
is null. Each dispatch funnel calls the declared Result type and
maps success/error explicitly; no aggregate function ABI transmute
is used. Constructor, realm, capability, super, GC trace, finalizer
and retained-memory paths include both typed callback forms.

EMPTY becomes Undefined at every public value boundary, including
bare callbacks, returns, throws, captures and forwarded arguments.
Ordinary slices retain their address. Only an EMPTY-containing slice
needs a sanitized copy. This closes step 11's release-boundary item.

As recorded before implementation, Function.prototype.apply roots
its evaluated prefix while later indexed getters can collect. The
root remains through the call and ends before buffer recycling.
CallbackCache roots borrowed heap arguments before its compiled
entry poll and through tail draining; controlled first-mark tests
proved the previous incidental native-stack retention. Prepared
fixed-array callers retain their stack-root contract, and raw JIT
entry documentation states the caller's root obligation.

The five live Number helpers formerly routed through tagged.rs now
use canonical Value constructors, retaining ToInt32, coercion order
and the NaN/infinite-exponent pow guards. The arithmetic switch is
kept in this part; the unused second representation is removed in
12f. Invalid-word tests now exercise production Value.

Source review covers every typed native funnel, immutable loan,
root/poll/tail boundary and representation ingress. Focused checks
include all value kinds and Ok/Err native results, native cache hits,
captured callbacks, reentrant and oversized argument windows, both
constructor forms, release EMPTY forwarding, NaN inputs and raw-byte
preservation. Linux focused checks pass 19 normal and 19 stress cases
plus 10 release cases; Wine 25 normal and 25 stress; ARM 28 normal
and 28 stress. Every counted case resolves in the actual inventory.
Fresh mutants omitting the apply prefix root, NaN canonicalization
or the original-window loan fail their intended assertions. Earlier
invalid-fixture and zero-match runs remain recorded as failures.

Production layouts: Interp 5776 to 5768 bytes, JitCtx 328 to 320,
JitFrame 2376 to 2368, with its word base moving from 328 to 320.
The other 20 measured carrier layouts retain every direct member's
offset and size. Exact layouts, runtime probes and source provenance
are retained for the neutral generated-code comparison.

The fresh x64 suite exposed three weak-metadata test fixtures whose
inactive WeakTarget payload word retained an old object address on
the native stack. Retention diagnostics locate the scanned word at
token+16. Box these weak tokens inside their existing separate frame
and borrow them afterward. Native scanning and all assertions stay.
Wine also exposed native-frame sensitivity in the synthetic this-word
root test; its original retained word was not localized. Box that
context in a separate helper frame. Both precise-only diagnostics
and boxed native-scanning variants pass normal and stress cases.
The final boxed context test still rejects an omitted precise root:
the first assertion sees D instead of R in both Wine modes.

Both repairs are confined to test modules. Preserve the original
source, layout and fat-LTO binary manifests; exact before/after
inventories and the reviewed cfg(test) boundaries establish identical
production input. This is source equivalence, not a claim that the
old binaries were rebuilt from the final test-file bytes.

prepared files retained old mtimes. That run is invalid and archived.

now copies bytes with current mtimes, and later zombie gates use

Step 12f: one execution representation

Value is directly the transparent u64 execution word and thread
marker. PackedValue, StoredValue, CallArgs, JitThisView and identity
conversion wrappers are removed. ValueStack stores Value directly.
Cell<Value> remains the mutable slot representation, and PropWord
alone may hold a deferred prototype. Its materialization records
the original owner's card before publishing the new object edge;
the lazy cell retains its separate barrier.

Remove tagged.rs's unused second TaggedValue, 32-bit HeapRef, mock
roots, shadow frames and toy materialization machinery. Neutral
compiler IDs, validated root maps and ordered CopySlot recipes
remain in deopt.rs. They contain no GC values or runtime addresses.
The real heap's test-only no-GC/borrow controls stay. Production keeps
safepoint-only collection and trace-time borrow checks. The arithmetic
flag, environment switch and duplicate Number paths are removed, keeping
coercion, IEEE edge cases and the exponentiation guards.

The clone_on_copy blanket allowance is removed. Compiler-typed
repairs remove redundant copies while retaining actual Rc, handle
and copy-on-write layout ownership. Native callbacks still use
their declared signatures; immutable argument loans, post-call
polls, tail drains and caller retirement remain intact. Final source
review found no new aliasing, rooting or premature retirement defect.
Stale counted-ownership comments now describe copied words and loans.

Focused root-owned checks pass 79 normal and 79 interval-1 stress
cases on each of ARM, x64 and Wine. Five strict configurations pass
on ARM and x64; Windows retains exactly its 23 baseline warnings.
Two fresh isolated mutants compile successfully and fail the intended
test: removing NaN canonicalization changes the asserted word bits;
removing the original owner's card triggers the old-to-young edge
verifier. Neither a build failure nor an empty filter is counted.

The compound rooting test now validates every exact test selector
and every applicable module prefix against the child binary's list,
then compares observed children with the entire expected inventory.
The first run caught a historical abbreviated deferred-prototype
filter; x64 caught an ARM-only module prefix requiring its existing
architecture cfg. Preserve those failures. Corrected fresh binaries
verify all 68 ARM and 54 x64 child tests under stress. Subsequent
comment corrections and the shared test-fixture repair described in
12e have exact retained diffs. The milestone tests use final source.

Final production DWARF and the original runtime probes agree for all
23 measured carriers in the diagnostic and ordinary builds. Interp
stays 5768 bytes; removing tagged_arithmetic moves thirteen following
byte fields by one byte, including constructing from 5759 to 5758.
JitCtx remains 320 bytes and JitFrame 2368. The other measured
carriers retain their sizes and all direct-member offsets.

The final source audit inventories roots, all eight cell descriptors,
ephemerons, weak clearing, native captures, heap identities, caches,
barriers, initialized native buffers and safepoint boundaries. It is
evidence for the inspected contracts, not a construction proof for
unexecuted paths. The known N-API finalizer teardown gap stays open.

I4's original wording forbids all cached interior pointers, whereas
the existing engine keeps non-owning raw Binding and code locations.
Live owner identity, weak address pins and non-recycled structural
generations or code epochs guard every inspected use. Runtime and
code retirement invalidate before freeing; execution leases protect
reentry. Fixed-layout bindings rederive slots. The final owner review
must address this wording explicitly; raw caches are not sole roots,
but the absolute statement that they are absent is not true.

The first full milestone run found a stale test expectation: removing
tagged_arithmetic correctly left 166 classified Interp fields, while
the inventory test still expected 167. The complete x64 suite was
2215/1/7 with only that failure. Cancel the ongoing ARM/Wine runs and
retain their partial logs; no partial run is counted as a pass. The
correction changes one byte inside the cfg(test) assertion. Source,
inventory and retained DWARF independently count 166. The exhaustive
struct pattern, duplicate checks and all classification checks stay.
Preserve the original layout/LTO manifests and add an exact source
equivalence link for this third test-only change. Full milestone
validation restarts on the corrected source with fresh zombie targets.

The strict comparison passes all nine fixture/mode pairs: 678 chunks
and 1356 verified companion images, with zero semantic rewrite rules
or body exemptions. All 26 positive and 141 negative neutral-proof
controls pass. The inventory-count correction required updating the
checker's exact interpreter source hash; the first stale-pin rejection
is retained. The new frozen bundle changes only that reviewed hash
and its certificate metadata. Every proof algorithm and control is
unchanged from the accepted 12e checker.

The corrected full milestone passes on both machines. On Crystal,
rustfmt checks 146 Rust files; all five strict clippy configurations
pass. Rustdoc retains eight warnings. The library is 2387/0/7 and
the doctests 11/0/1. The inventory has 32 removed and 21 added names,
