Implementation and correctness

Steps 12a–12f replace the engine and scoped API's Value enum with one
eight-byte, thread-bound Copy execution word. Mutable slots use
Cell<Value>. PropWord alone can retain a deferred prototype and
records the original owner's card when materializing its edge.
Native calls borrow immutable argument windows; typed callbacks keep
their actual signatures and roots. NaN ingress is canonicalized,
while raw buffer and guest Wasm payload bits keep their semantics.
The obsolete tagged experiment, arithmetic switch and transitional
value wrappers are gone. Neutral compiler-frame metadata remains.

The corrected full step-12 milestone passes on both machines. ARM
library/stress has 2387 passes and seven expected benchmark ignores;
x64 has 2216 passes and seven ignores. Wine has 2208 passes, seven
ignores and exactly three baseline snapshot-codec failures. No
stress failure, timeout or retry occurs. Both ARM Test262 thresholds
and zombie's JIT report match their references; threshold zero keeps
its two baseline legacy-function-arguments failures. The downstream
suites pass. Native wasm-crate commands execute zero tests, which is
not Wasm execution coverage; the owner retired wasm32 acceptance.

All nine strict generated-code pairs pass: 678 chunks and 1356
verified companion images, zero semantic rewrite rules or body
exemptions. The final committed engine source is unchanged from this
accepted milestone. The separate S6 exit validation also passes.
ARM formatting checks all 274 Rust files changed since main; all
five strict clippy configurations pass and rustdoc keeps eight
baseline warnings. Its library is 2387/0/7, doctests 11/0/1, with
unchanged inventories and no workspace diagnostics. Downstreams are
host 37, timers 6, fs 0, web 63/0/1, node 47, runtime 184/0/1,
and native wasm 0/0/0. Fixtures preserve outputs, compiles 56/10/160
and collections 21/6/16; sustained allocation collects 194 times.
Difftest reports 138 agreements and 12 budget skips, with empty
stderr and corpus. ARM Test262 is 53575/0/3 at threshold 8 and
53573/2/3 at zero, matching the reference reports exactly.

The S6 exit x64 library is 2216/0/7; Wine is 2208/3/7 with exactly
its baseline failures. x64 clippy is clean, Windows has its same
23 warnings, both inventories are unchanged, and Test262, fixtures
and difftest match their references. Full ARM stress passes
2357/25/5 cases at intervals 1/100/1000, plus seven expected ignores.
Full x64 stress passes 2190/22/4, plus seven ignores. Neither exit
run has a failure, timeout or retry in its stress suite. Every
source/config hash, expected test name, outcome and phase exit is
verified. Final evidence status explicitly leaves acceptance open.

Original failures remain preserved: stale remote Cargo artifacts,
GC-fixture/native-stack sensitivity, the stale inventory-test count,
and source-pin, archive and measurement-parser rejections. Corrected
runs have their own labels and directories. The release binaries
retain original build manifests; exact cfg(test)-only certificates
connect their compiled source to the final source. They are not
represented as rebuilds from the final test bytes.

Default-options Vue results

The S0 absolute instruction cap passes. Fifteen interleaved rounds
on Crystal CPU 5 give final median 2,668,510,178 instructions versus
matched main 3,298,528,568. Every final sample is below 2.97 billion
(range 2,665,659,654–2,669,110,873). GNU time is inside perf, so these
counts conservatively include its instructions. Process wall time
also includes launching and reaping perf; RSS is engine-only. Main
uses the same rustc 1.99.1 toolchain; the retained rustc-1.99.0 main
stays separate.

Against matched main, default Vue instructions fall 19.100%, cycles
22.548%, wall time 21.760%, and cache misses 7.971%. Paired 95%
intervals exclude zero for each. Branch misses rise 15.388%
(95% interval +13.620..+16.504%); their causal source has not been
isolated, and aggregate speed does not waive this component issue.
Engine RSS changes -1.412%, with an interval spanning zero.

Node v24.22.0 runs the same fixture in median 188.446 ms versus
TRust's 498.537 ms, a 2.646x wall-time ratio. This is one Vue workload,
not an overall engine comparison. Exact binaries and all raw counters
are retained. Initial major faults remain in the samples.

The unchanged S0 ownership classifier reports a raw sampled bucket
of 1.47%, versus original S0 9.05%, fresh matched main 9.08%, and
retained main 9.16%. The halving criterion passes. Each fresh profile
contains eight successful default-options executions and zero lost
samples. Selected self totals are 68.8/66.16/65.93%; they are not
renormalized. These serial sampled-symbol point estimates have no
confidence interval and are not exact ownership-time measurements.

Instrumented component results and open bounds

The 360-sample, fifteen-round component matrix enables metrics for
all engines to obtain GC and compilation data. It is a distinct
protocol from the default-options counters above. Its auxiliary Vue
counter run is retained separately; the original clock-label parser
rejection is preserved as metadata failure, not an engine failure.
Engine RSS comes from inner GNU time, not the launcher's highwater.

Against matched main, instrumented Vue/React/Djot wall times improve
18.816/12.996/11.979%, and cycles 19.353/16.972/12.540%. Engine RSS
changes +1.235/+48.593/-0.980%; React fails the +25% bound, with a
paired 95% interval of +45.810..+57.717%. Its median is 24916 KiB.
The throughput gains do not establish memory acceptance.

Total GC shares, including exit, are Vue 4.216%, React 4.978%, and
Djot 8.718%. React is borderline: its median's 95% interval is
4.960..5.061%, and individual samples reach 5.152%. Sustained
allocation is 36.209% at JIT and 28.429% at bytecode. Excluding exit,
the same shares are 3.918/4.330/7.799% and 34.449/27.063%.
Djot and sustained allocation fail the 5% bound either way. Main's
per-cause pause timings do not exist; no-exit shares are unavailable.

Observed longest pauses are 2.081/1.283/5.400 ms for the fixtures
and 21.824/25.236 ms for sustained JIT/bytecode. Relative to 12e,
median longest sustained pauses rise 3.456/4.768%; their intervals
extend above 5%. Sustained-bytecode instructions rise 1.228%.
Those costs are recorded explicitly for the final cleanup step.

Other main-baseline component questions remain: React/Vue bytecode
compile time +16.469/+7.260%, Vue's largest native chunk +56.594%,
empty-startup RSS +22.669%, and React median longest pause rising
from 0.215 to 1.206 ms. Branch and main compile different counts
(56/10 versus 55/9 on Vue/React). The whole-process comparison
includes that work; it does not isolate the cause of each component.

Occupancy evidence

The isolated census adds a read-only method and post-collection
logging in two files, with no stored fields or allocator changes.
Six production/diagnostic pairs
have identical checksums and all 194 raw collection records,
including minor-collection suffixes. Each has 190 minors and four
majors. All recorded large-cell bytes and counts are zero.

At the final task-boundary minor, allocated small-cell bytes / usable
cell capacity are 46,557,024 / 49,717,680 (93.643%) at JIT and
46,453,920 / 49,522,864 (93.803%) at bytecode. After the explicit
exit major they are 38,637,792 / 39,980,208 (96.642%) and
38,637,600 / 39,590,832 (97.592%). Spares add 0.75 MiB before exit
and 2 MiB afterward. Free cell capacity after exit is 1,342,416 and
953,232 bytes respectively; card tables and class tails are separate.

These are allocated size-class bytes, including retained old cells
and zombies. They are not precise strong reachability or requested
payload sizes. The census excludes backing buffers, allocator and
side-table overhead, reserved address space and other process RSS;
it does not establish whole-RSS fragmentation or timing acceptance.

I4 requires an explicit contract decision

The plan says generated code and IC cells never cache raw interior
pointers across possible collections and instead rederive them from
tagged words. That literal statement is not true of this engine.
Non-owning Binding and code locations remain cached. The bounded
source review verifies live-owner identity, weak address pins,
non-recycled structural generations/code epochs, and execution leases
across reentry at every inspected use. Invalidation precedes freeing;
fixed-layout reads rederive slots. No new defect was found, but that
review cannot silently turn the literal ban into a satisfied invariant.

Proposed wording for owner review: a cached raw location is non-owning
and must have a complete stable-owner/ABA and structural/code-validity
proof before dereference. Preserve owner and execution lifetime across
reentry, and revalidate or rederive after invalidation. A raw cache
must never be the sole root. This is a proposal, not an amendment.
The alternative is further implementation work to satisfy the ban.

Other carried limits and disposition

Production collection remains safepoint-only. NoGcScope/BorrowScope
are test-only controls, not production dynamic borrow counters. Native
conservative scanning retains the recorded compiler/ABI contract and
its limitations. N-API pending finalizers still lack a teardown flush
and discard callback exceptions. The previously deferred card/range
summary work is still separate; no scope or criterion is waived here.

The instruction and ownership goals have concrete passing evidence.
Memory, GC-share, component and I4 acceptance questions remain open.
The recommended disposition is to keep the completed implementation
on the review branch and resolve those questions before merging.

Primary evidence: target/s6-step12fr/final-step-review.json;
target/s6-step10/m12f-v3; target/s6-exit/aux-verified-v2.json and
aux-review-v2.json; the two Vue counter directories;
ownership-profile; occupancy; gate-verified.json;
zombie-milestone/verified-all.json; and final-exit-verified.json.
The invariant inventory and its test-only source links are
in target/s6-step12fr/final-invariant-inventory.txt and
final-audit-corrected.json. The tracked narrative retains the same
limitations.


S6 exit review repairs, 2026-10-23 (this commit)

The owner's review identified five GC lifetime regressions against
main, all predating step 12. The release repro matrix retained 24/24
passing main runs and 19/24 failing branch runs across interpreter,
bytecode and JIT. The disposal failures include release crashes.
The repaired release build passes all 24 runs, including the NaN
control. The original scripts and every failing observation remain
under target/s6-review-fixes; neither main nor the earlier evidence
was changed.

DisposeFrame roots the current and remaining resources together with
normal/abrupt completion. A resource stays in that record through its
call, await and error folding. The VM publishes VmDispose in its
already traced coroutine before the first advance. Suspensions keep
that state; completion/termination clear it. No persistent Rooted
registration is added to a coroutine.

Iterator.zip/zipKeyed use a RootedVec for partial results. JSON.parse
roots the actual source record and traces its primitive values with
an iterative walk, preserving iterative drop. Class evaluation uses
one traced pending-elements record for fields, transforms, private
members and static elements; FieldInit tracing also serves installed
ClassInfo. Object.groupBy roots actual converted PropertyKeys and
groups, plus a bounded pair for the current element/key conversion.
Publication into the final owning object has no intervening callback.

Nineteen tests cover all tiers, normal/abrupt completion, actual async
suspension, all zip modes, source strings/duplicate JSON keys, computed
Symbol keys, decorators/private members and IteratorClose. They check
explicit roots before real minor/major collection, avoiding success
from dead native/JIT slots, and check retirement after unwinding.
Separate source-record tests prove precise retention, death after drop
and iterative traversal/drop at 50,000 levels on a 1 MiB stack.

Ten fresh omitted-root variants compile and produce twelve intended
assertion failures. Build errors, empty filters, crashes and timeouts
do not count as mutation evidence. The final test checkpoint helper
uses the same activate/drain/task-boundary sequence in every feature
configuration. The first gate and preflight configuration failures
are preserved; production code did not change during that repair.

Tested source snapshot: 797 files, SHA-256
fd9ee16059bebbdc3c2030c774b79300b42a7a12313cdc35f8244f8f7321b216.
The final zombie focused run passes all 76 individual cases: all
nineteen tests, normal and stress interval 1, on native x64 and Wine.
Independent checks compare raw per-case output, exits, exact
names/counts, source maps and binary hashes after archiving.

Both full gates match their recorded baselines. Crystal's native
library is 2406 pass / 0 fail / 7 ignore; x64 is 2235 / 0 / 7.
Windows under Wine is 2227 / 3 / 7, with exactly the same three
snapshot-codec failures. Each inventory adds nineteen names and
removes none. All nineteen also pass normal/stress1 individually
under Wine. No native stress case fails, times out or needs a retry:
Crystal covers all 2413 tests and zombie all 2242, including every
established slow-list case at intervals 100 and 1000.

Five strict Crystal clippy configurations pass; zombie's native
clippy is clean and its 23 Windows diagnostics match the reference.
Rustdoc's eight warnings are unchanged, doctests are 11 / 0 / 1,
and the workspace and downstream results match their baselines.
The native Wasm wrapper check executes both existing empty harnesses;
this does not claim a wasm32 build or Wasm specification-suite run.

Release fixture outputs, compilation and collection counts match,
as do sustained-allocation checksums and 194 collections in both
tiers. Difftest is 138 agreements and twelve existing budget skips.
All nine routine .sum2 files (678 chunks) are byte-identical to the
previous S6 exit; this repair changes no JIT emitter or baked layout.

native machines. Crystal threshold0 is 53573 / 2 / 3 with exactly
the two existing staging reference failures. All reports match the
references byte for byte; rounded 100.0% is not a zero-failure claim.
